1722. JOHN FUZZEY and JOHN DAVIS were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of August, 15 planes, value 30s.; 2 saws, value 2s.; 2 squares, value 3s.; 1 pair of compasses, value 6d., and 4 chisels, value 4s., the goods of Robert Malom; 2 planes, value 7s.; 1 pair of compasses, value 6d.; 2 chisels, value 6d.; 1 screw-driver, value 6d., and 1 square, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Lincoln.
ROBERT MALOM. I am a carpenter, and was working at Belvoir-terrace, Bridge-road, Vauxhall. On Saturday night, the 11th of August, I left my tools all safe, and on Monday morning, I was going to work, I found the back kitchen window broken open, and all my tools from a box, and under the bench gone. I missed those stated in the indictment. I went to Mr. Graham, the pawnbroker, in Tothill-street, to give information, and as I came out of there I met Fuzzey coming in with a basket of tools - I let him go in, and then claimed them - they were part of mine.
THOMAS LINCOLN. I lost some tools in another house in the same terrace.
THOMAS PACE. I am an officer. On the morning of the 13th I was fetched to Mr. Harris's, a pawnbroker, and found Davis there, offering tools in pawn - he said they were his own. I searched him, and found a pair of compasses in his pocket - Malom claimed them.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I took Fuzzey in charge with the tools. I found two squares on him at the watch-house.(Property produced and sworn to.)
FUZZEY - GUILTY. Aged 18.
DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-135]
1723. MARY HURLEY and ANN DAVIS were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 2 pieces of printed cotton, containing 21 yards, value 1l., the goods of Sarah Dyson and Mary Dyson.
SARAH DYSON. I am in partnership with my sister Mary - we have a linen-draper's shop, in Margaret-street, Hackney-fields. On the 8th of September the prisoners came to purchase some print - we showed them several - none suited - they bought none; and as soon as they left, I missed a piece of cotton. I ran out, but not seeing them, came in again, and my sister said something. I then missed another piece, and ran out again and passed a street, and in returning met them coming out of that street. I told them I missed two prints; they said they would go back if I made no alarm, and as I got to the door, I stopped to call a neighbour - they got a little before me into the shop. I went in and saw the green-print go from Hurley's hand on the counter - the other piece was then on the counter.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. They gave you a good deal of trouble, did not they? A. Yes; there were several prints on the counter. I did not miss the green pieces first - one of them had a basket, I think it was Hurley - they offered to be searched afterwards; neither of them had a green shawl on, that I observed.
MARY DYSON. My sister called me into the shop; she ran out, and I missed the green print; she ran in again - I said the green print was also gone; she ran out, and brought them back, and I saw the green print in the basket, before it was thrown on the counter; and, soon after, I saw both pieces on the counter; Hurley then said,"Search me;" I said, "There is no occasion to search you, you have thrown enough out of your basket;" she said, "Will you swear a person's life away in that manner! Oh! what lies."
Cross-examined. Q. Had one of them a green shawl on? A. Yes; I am sure the prints were not on the counter a few minutes before.
MARY HIGHLAND. My husband is a presser. I saw the prisoners in Margaret-street - Davis took something out of her pocket hole, and put into the basket; she then took something else more bulky, which appeared to be the green print, and put in - they carried the basket between them.
Cross-examined. Q. What time was this? A. Between three and four o'clock; I never saw them before.
THOMAS FRANKLIN. I am a constable. I received them in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
HURLEY - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Three Months.
DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.

1724. JULIA HASWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, 3 blankets, value 3s.; 2 pillows, value 2s.; 1 sheet, value 2s.; 1 pair of snuffers, value 3d., and 1 snuffer-tray, value 3d., the goods of Elizabeth Chorley.
ELIZABETH CHORLEY. I am a widow, and live in Grove-place, Bethnal-green. On the 3d of July I let the prisoner a furnished room; on the 18th I went into the room, and asked for the loan of a pillow - she hesitated; I thought all was not right; I sent for an officer, and called her into the parlour; we went up and missed these articles - the duplicates of them were found in a cupboard in her room - she said she intended to return them.
FRANCIS SOAMES. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Green-street, Bethnal-green. I have a blanket pawned on the 12th of July, for 1s., and a pillow, on the 13th; and some cups and saucers, in the name of Haswell. I cannot swear to the prisoner.
EDWARD HALES. I am a pawnbroker. I have a blanket, pawned for 1s., and two or three other articles, they were all pawned by the prisoner.
JAMES ESSEX. I am an officer, and took her; I found duplicates of the property in a pocket-book in her room.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Confined Three Months.

1725. GEORGE WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of July, 2 shirts, value 30s., the goods of James Soper.
JAMES SOPER. I live at Edmonton. On the 24th of July, on coming home, between seven and eight o'clock, I heard our drying ground had been robbed. I applied to the constable, and next day I saw a pawnbroker, who described a person to me. I saw the prisoner carrying a bundle on the road, and had him secured, and taken into a public-house; I asked what he had in the bundle; he said two shirts; I opened them, and said they were mine. I had the fellow shirt on - I never saw him before.
JOSEPH DOSSETT. I am a constable. I took the prisoner with the bundle, containing two shirts.
MARY MAINE. I am a servant to Mr. Soper; I hung the linen out about one o'clock, and missed it between two and three.

[trialAccount t18270913-140]
1728. LUCY CATHERINE BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, 2 blankets, value 5s.; 2 pillows, value 4s.; 1 table-cloth, value 1s. 6d.; 2 pillow-cases, value 9d.; 2 spoons, value 4s.; 2 sheets, value 6s., and 1 candlestick, value 6d., the goods of James Rowell.
MELYAR SOPHIA ROWELL. I am the wife of James Rowell, a cabinet-maker: we live in Smith-street, Somers'-town. The prisoner hired some apartments of me, ready furnished - she paid me very well. I went into her room two or three days previous to her being taken, and missed this property - some of it has been found.
WILLIAM BAKER. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Somers'-town. I have two blankets, a sheet, and a candlestick, pawned at different times by a woman, in the name of Brown. I have a table-cloth, which I can swear was pawned by the prisoner for 1s. 6d., in July.
GEORGE HAVILL. I am a constable. I was sent for on the 23d of August, and took the prisoner - she gave up some duplicates of this property, and said she intended to redeem them on Friday.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. How long had she been with you? A. Between four and five months: she might be poorly, but never had any medical attendant - there was 2l. 13s. due when I spoke to her; she had given me notice to quit, and the expiration of the notice would have been in two days. I will swear that the first time I missed any thing from her apartments, was on the Tuesday night previous to the Friday on which the notice would have expired. I spoke to her of it on the Wednesday - I gave her into custody on Thursday.
Prisoner's Defence. My remittances did not come in, and I pawned some sheets, which the prosecutor knew of- and the day I was committed here, she told me she was not a fool, she knew I had done it. Poverty alone induced me so far to forget myself.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-141]
1729. MARY LANG was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of August, 1 piece of gold, of the value of 10s.; 1 sovereign, and 3 pieces of foreign silver coin, value 4s. 6d., the property of Michael Moore.
MICHAEL MOORE. I am a sailor, and Italian. I came here in the Saucy Jack, and have got no ship since. On the 15th of August, about eleven o'clock at night, I was in White's-yard; I met the prisoner, who took me to a house - I was very tired - I took off my jacket, and put it on a chair. I had some foreign gold and silver in the pocket - I fell on the bed, and went to sleep directly - she was then in the room. I awoke in about half an hour, and saw her over-hauling my things; I jumped out of bed, felt my pocket, and the money was gone; she put the money into her mouth; I tried to get it out, but could not. I called the watchman, who came; she then said, "I give charge of this man for ill-using me:" I said, "I give charge of this woman for robbing me;" I did not find all my money- there was 9s. 6d. taken from her mouth at the watch-house. I lost all the rest - I had not given her any thing - I was to pay her for the night's lodging certainly, but it was not agreed what. I lost one sovereign, and some Columbian money, and 9s. 6d. in silver.
CHRISTOPHER WARD. I heard the cry of Murder! and Watch! - I went to the room, and the prisoner said, "I give charge of this man for ill-using me;" and he said, "You have got 1l. 14s. 6d. English money of mine, and if you don't give it me, he shall take you to the watch-house." I took them both to the watch-house - I saw no silver but what the prosecutor had in his hand, and which he said he got from her month - her mouth was bleeding - they were quarrelling together.
JOHN DANGATE. I was the watch-house keeper. The prisoner was brought to me, and the prosecutor accused her of robbing him of 14s. 6d., one sovereign, two quarter-dollars, and one half-dollar - the prisoner said she had not got any thing upon her person. I searched her, and found in the left side of her mouth (which was bleeding) 9s. 6d. in silver, and there was sixpenny worth of copper in her hand; this half-dollar, and two quarter-dollars, the prosecutor gave me, and told me he took them out of her left hand.
Prisoner. When he got up into the room he asked me for a light, and said he thought he had lost some money - he pulled out a 5l. note, and said, "Some of those girls in the public-house have robbed me;" I said, "Recollect you have not lost it here;" and then he sent me for something to drink, and gave me 6s. I did not rob him - he was asleep above an hour; and if I had been disposed to rob him, I should have taken all his money.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-142]
1730. CHARLES LINSCOTT was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of June, 1 silver spoon, value 14s., the goods of Mary Clay, widow.
MARY CLAY. I am a widow, and live in Wapping, with my son, who is a baker; the prisoner used to come to the shop very often; he came into the bake-house, on the 6th of June; some of the girls gave him something to eat; in two days afterwards the spoon was missing.
MARY ISRAEL. I keep a silversmith's shop. The prisoner came into my shop, two or three months ago - I cannot tell whether it was June or July - I was before the Magistrate, but do not know when - I have a very short memory.
Q. Did you state to the Magistrate, at the examination, on the 24th of August: - "The prisoner came to my shop, and threw the spoon on my counter - I asked where he got it; he said his mother sent him with it?" A. Yes; it was broken. I do not know his mother - I weighed it; it was 13/4 oz. - I paid him 8s. 3d.; here is the spoon - I live in at No. 4, Upper East Smithfield, near the Mint - I deal in clothes as well as silver - I did not ask where his mother lived. I sell my silver to the refiners.
Q. Is this spoon worn out? A. It is; because it is broken; it is not worn out.
Prisoner. Q. What day did you buy it of me? A. I cannot tell.
RALPH CLAY. I am the prosecutrix's son; she informed me she had lost a spoon. I was accidentally passing Israel's shop, about a week after, and saw a spoon which appeared to me to be the same. I got an officer, who went and brought up Israel; the prisoner had been in my employ, and was discharged on the 6th. If this spoon is ours, it is much disfigured since I saw it last. I told the Magistrate the spoon produced there was mine - I cannot swear to it now. I knew the spoon I saw in the window was my mother's; if this is the same, it has been much worn, and knocked about since. I believe the one I saw to be ours, but the parts of this do not fit the bowl.
MARK ISRAEL. The spoon has been in my possession since - I have not altered it.
- JEFFREY. I was before the Magistrate - Israel produced a spoon there.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-143]
1731. JAMES M'DOUGAL was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, 1 bundle of small-hand paper, value 10s., the goods of Joseph Bargett.

On the 24th of August, about half-past nine o'clock, at night I saw the prisoner take a parcel from under his arm from Bardgett's shop-door; he reached in, and took it - I sprang my rattle, and called Stop thief! he threw it down in Well-street, and I took him.
JOHN BARGETT. I am a tailor. This paper laid on my shop floor; the door was shut.
GUILTY. Aged 17.

[trialAccount t18270913-144]
1732. HENRY PERRY and JOHN NOBLE were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of July, 1 table-cloth, value 4d.; 2 petticoats, value 4s.; 1 cap, value 4d.; 1 pair of stays, value 4s.; 1 bed-gown, value 1s.; 1 shift, value 1s. 6d.; 2 shirts, value 4s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 6d., and 1 apron, value 6d., the goods of Mary Summerfield, widow.
MARY SUMMERFIELD. I am a widow, and live in Ann's-place, Hackney-road, and take in washing. I sent my son James for a bundle of dirty linen, from Mrs. Donald's, near the Rose and Crown public-house, Cambridge-heath; he was brought home without it - I found the prisoner in custody.
JAMES SUMMERFIELD. I am near eight years old. About half-past nine o'clock in the morning, I went to Mrs. Donald's, who gave me a bundle for my mother - Perry came to me, just over the bridge, and said, if I would take a note for him he would give me 6d. - Noble was on the other side of the road - I said, very well; he took me down a street opposite, about four yards - Perry showed me the house I was to go to; he told me to put my bundle down, and he would mind it, white I went with the note, which he gave me - I put down my bundle, and saw Perry run away with it; he gave it to Noble, who was across the road, and they ran away together - I called out, and some people came - I saw Perry caught - I am quite sure of them both.
WILLIAM GLASSICK. I am a butcher. I was in Pollard's-row, about nine o'clock in the morning, and saw this boy crying; the two prisoners were running before him - Noble had the bundle under his arm - I followed, and saw Noble throw it down - I took it up immediately - Perry was running first.
ROBERT JOHN RUSSELL. I am a carpenter, and was at work near Cambridge-heath. I heard a cry of Stop thief! ran out, and saw Noble who had just been stopped - I took hold of him, as we crossed the street; he said, I was no officer, and had no business with him - I took him to the watch-house.
THOMAS MILLER. I heard an alarm, ran and took Perry, who somebody had stopped; the boy gave me this blank paper, folded up like a note.
JOHN ESSEX. I received them in charge.
PERRY - GUILTY. Aged 15.
NOBLE - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-145]
1733. JOHN SIMPSON and SAMUEL BROWN were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 pair of trousers, value 20s., the goods of Thomas Thwaites.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I am an officer. On the 8th of September, about a quarter past seven o'clock, I was in Broad-street, St. Giles, and saw the prisoners go to Mr. Thwaites shop - Simpson made several attempts to get these trousers, which hung inside the door - he got them at last by a strong pull, and gave them to Brown, who was looking in at the window - I had seen them together some time before. I ran over and seized Brown with the property; another person stopped Simpson.
THOMAS THWAITES. I live in St. Giles; these trousers hung inside my door; they were pinned to a coat; it would require a strong pull to get them down.
SIMPSON's Defence. I saw a waistcoat piece in the window; I was looking at it; the other prisoner came and asked the time, I felt some one brush by me - I looked at the window again, and a man came up, and said, "I have got you;" he would not tell me what for.
SIMPSON - GUILTY. Aged 24.
BROWN - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.

1735. HENRY FORSYTH alias PEAKE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, 60 lbs. weight of lead, value 10s., the goods of John Featherstone, and affixed to a certain dwelling-house of his, against the statute.
ELEANOR JONES. I am a widow, and live in East-street, Red-lion-square. I let the house No. 4, Great Ormond-street, which belongs to my brother, ( John Featherstone,) to Mr. Mortimer - I saw a pump in the yard about three days before the 24th of August, on which day, the watchman came and told me it was gone. I went in the evening, and missed it - I have seen one at the office, which appears to me to be the same; I do not see any difference in it.
JOHN WILLIAM MORTIMER. I took this house. On the evening of the 23d, about nine o'clock, I saw the pump safe in the yard, and at nine the next morning, the watchman came to tell me it was gone, and that the prisoner was in custody - it was then missing.
HENRY TURNER. I am a watchman. On the 24th of August, a quarter before one o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner in Great Ormond-street, with this pump on his shoulder - I asked him what he had got - he made no answer; I then took charge of him - I have tried this pump to the place, and it appears to me to have belonged to there. There was another man with him, but I never lost sight of the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw this pump laying on a dunghill; it appeared to have no owner; a young man saw me take it up; he went to the watch-house, but they would not hear him.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270913-148]
1736. JOHN FRANCIS and CHARLES SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, 1 live turkey, price 12s., and 18 apples, value 1s., the property of William Hierons.
WILLIAM HIERONS. I am a farmer, and live at Perryford, near Harrow. On the 3d of August my servant missed a turkey - I went into the yard, and it was gone - I had seen it safe in the afternoon of the day before - it is what we call a milk-white one, and was moulting at the time - I know nothing of the prisoners - I also missed some apples from some small trees; I took some apples to the office, and what I saw there corresponded exactly.
WILLIAM GREAVES. I am a patrol of Paddington. On the 3d of August I was going my rounds, and at White's-bridge I saw the two prisoners coming across the country; one had a bundle under his arm, and the other this basket across his shoulder - I took a different direction, and met them in a lane, and asked what they had got - Francis said, "I have got nothing, not much;" I said,"What have you got?" he then said, "A turkey, I bought it of a man in a cart at Harrow, for 5s., for a gentleman in the Borough" - I asked what part of the Borough he lived in - he said, he did not know; I then asked where he was to take it to - he said, to the gentleman's brother in Westminster - I asked where there - he said he did not know; I asked if there was any direction on the turkey, and whether it was alive or dead; he said it was alive, and there was no direction; I then asked Smith what he had got; he said, "Some apples, to make a dumpling;" I asked where he was going to to make it; Francis replied, he was going to his house; I asked where that was - he said, in Westminster; I asked what street, and what number - he said he did not know; I then took them into custody, and have brought the turkey here.
FRANCIS BRADBURY. The prisoners were brought to me with the turkey and the apples - Francis said he had bought the turkey of a man near Harrow, who had forty or fifty of them; it was in a small basket, doubled up head and heels, to crowd it in.(Turkey produced and sworn to.)
FRANCIS - GUILTY. Aged 49.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 40.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-149]
1673. JOHN HARVEY was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 2 pieces of leather, value 1s. 4d., the goods of William Thomas Binckers.
MR. WILLIAM THOMAS BINCKERS. I am a leather-cutter, and live at No. 52, Compton-street. On the 10th of August I saw the prisoner - I have known him for the last twelve years; he came into the shop and took a piece of leather out of the basket, looked at it, and asked for some others. I had some suspicion, and took notice of what I put before him. He looked at them and asked for some others; and while I was getting them, he reached over, and slipped down two pieces by the side of the counter, and caught them with his knee. He then bought a pair of me for 71/2d., which he paid for, and went out; I pursued him, and met the constable in the street - we went to his lodgings. He had been home, and was gone out again; we wanted to search, but his wife would not let us, unless we had a warrant - we went to the office, and they told us to go back and break the door open. We went back and found these two pieces of leather in a pan of water, which they had not been in above four or five minutes. I can swear to them - we found the prisoner in Newport-market.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. But he has bought a great number of pieces of leather of you, and whether these were not some of them you cannot tell? A. Yes; I can - I had bought this one pair that day, and they have particular veins on them; being in water so short a time will not alter them.
CHARLES BOWLER. I am a constable. I went with the witness and found these pieces of leather in a pan.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought a pair of soles for 71/2d.; for which I paid - and if he had any suspicion he naturally would have given an alarm, it being in the middle of the day; I went straight home to my dwelling - I left the inner soles, and went towards the Strand - when I was going down Newport-market I met a man I knew, and while talking to him the prosecutor and the constable came up and charged me with the robbery. They took these two pairs of soles from my apartment, where there were several pairs, all with the prosecutor's mark - they had all been bought at his shop.
THOMAS LINDEMAN. I live at No. 30, Little New-street, and am a shoemaker. Leather being steeped in water, makes it browner - this part appears to have been in water as high as this mark; being in water will alter the appearance so materially as to make it difficult for a person to identify it.
THOMAS CLEMENTS. I am a bootmaker, and live at No. 33, St. Martin's-street, Leicester-square. The effect of steeping leather in water will alter the colour, so that no man can identify it. There is nothing at all uncommon in the appearances of these two pieces of leather.
GUILTY. Aged 52.
Confined Twelve Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-150]
1738. JULIA SHEAN was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of September, 1 watch value 2l., the goods of Mark Allen.
ANN ALLEN. I am the wife of Mark Allen - he is a seafaring man - I live at Poplar. On the 5th of September I left a watch on the mantel-piece - when I went out a little before nine o'clock in the morning; I came back a few minutes after ten - the prisoner had left her key in my place before I went out, and when I returned it was gone, and the watch likewise - I then went after her and took her to one of our officers who found the duplicate in a box with three half-crowns.
WILLIAM BROWN. I was sent for to take the prisoner. I searched her and found this box, which contained one sovereign, three half-crowns, and this duplicate of a watch; she had before declared she had not seen it.
THOMAS NEAT. I am a pawnbroker. This watch was pawned with me, between eleven and twelve o'clock on the 5th of September, by the prisoner, for 30s.; I gave her a sovereign and three half-crowns.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This woman keeps a bad house. She has two or three husbands alive; on the night before she picked up a man and brought him home; they had breakfast and then went out; the man came back and asked me to pawn this watch - I said "Where is the landlady?" he said, "She has got a drop." I came back, and they were not at home; the door was shut - I then opened the window and took my key.
WILLIAM BROWN re-examined. Q. When you went there, did she say anything to you about it? A. She said all the property she had about her was a few halfpence - we took her in a back room; she threw out those few halfpence - we then searched, and found the box and duplicate: she at first said, before the Magistrate, that Mrs. Allen gave her the watch, and 1s. to pawn it; she afterwards said a young woman gave it to her - in coming home, she said she was a poor unfortunate creature and was sorry she had done the crime.
GUILTY. Aged 47.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-151]
1739. THOMAS KING was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, 1 wheel-barrow, value 14s., and 1 washing-tub, value 5s., the goods of John Shann.
JOHN SHANN. I am a confectioner, and live at Twickenham. I lost a wheel-barrow and tub on the 30th of July, from the gate of Hanworth-park, in the high road - I had been taking ice out of a well in the park - the prisoner lived in my service about three years ago.
THOMAS MATHER. The prosecutor came to me about a wheel-barrow and tub. I went the next morning to Uxbridge fair, where I met the prisoner - I asked him what he had done with the wheel-barrow and tub; he told me where they were - I found them at Hounslow.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating, that the barrow stood in the middle of a road, three-quarters of a mile from any house, and by direction of one Maunder, who had employed him, he took it to his, (Maunder's stables), and immediately he was taken he told the officer it was there.
NOT GUILTY.

The prosecutor did not appear. NOT GUILTY.

1741. MARY SPENCER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, 1 counterpane, value 10s.; 2 sheets, value 6s.; 1 blanket, value 5s., and 1 bolster, value 3s., the goods of William Edge Conway.
MARY CONWAY. I am the wife of William Edge Conway - we live in Carnaby-market - the prisoner lodged with us. On the 20th of July I missed the counterpane, two sheets, a blanket, and bolster, which I had let to her with the lodging; she had been taken, I believe, on another charge - the officer came to my house, and we went into her apartment - I then missed this property.
JOHN HAWKES. I am a pawnbroker. I produce these articles, which were pawned by the prisoner, on the 10th of July.
MARGARET BARBER. The prisoner sold to me this bolster one day in July.
GEORGE AVIS. I am an officer. I took up the prisoner - she said nothing about the things.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
There was another indictment against the prisoner.
