1741. WILLIAM SHAW was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, 1 watch, value 4l., and 3 seals, value 2l., the goods of Daniel Tomlin.
DANIEL TOMLIN. I lost my watch, chain, and seals, on the 16th of July - I had left them in the privy at Fitzroy-market, about nine o'clock that morning. I returned in the evening, and missed them - I have not seen them since.
SUSAN EAVES. My mother lives in Fitzroy-market. I saw William Shaw with a watch in his hand, a little after nine o'clock in the morning - I do not know whose it was; he afterwards told me he had sold it for a guinea - he said he had found it in a curious place, but did not say where. He said the man that sold it had the shilling, and another boy, and him went partners in the money.
Q. Did you see the watch? A. Yes; it was a largish silver watch, a steel chain, three seals, and a key, which appeared to be a gold one - the key was a hollow-ring, without a stone it.
THOMAS FRAMPTON. I took the prisoner, and asked him about the watch; he said that Scroggins and him had sold it for a guinea. I heard that it was Tomlin's watch, but I did not hear the prisoner say so, nor what sort of a watch it was.
DANIEL TOMLIN re-examined. Mine was a good sized silver watch, with three gold seals of different sizes, a metal key, with a ring, and no stone in it.
Prisoner's Defence. I went into the privy, and saw the watch. I came out with it in my hand, and saw a boy, who snatched it from me, and ran away; he afterwards came back, said he had sold it, and gave me 10s.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-155]
1742. JOHN JONES , alias SPRANG , was indicted for stealing, on the 10th August, 16 quires of paper, value 5s., the goods of John Gratwick Clements.
JOHN GRATWICK CLEMENTS. I am a bookseller and stationer, and live in Little Pulteney-street. I was in the parlour, behind my shop, on the 10th of August, and saw the prisoner come into the shop with long strides, and lay hold of the paper, which was at the farthest extremity of the shop - he had to reach over the counter to get it. I ran out, and he dropped it on the counter - he had moved it about a yard. I gave an alarm, and followed him. He was stopped in my sight.
Prisoner. Q. Were you not sitting in your parlour at the time? A. No, I was standing - I did not see you with a pipe in your hand; there is a curtain before my parlour window - but I was looking, because I had been robbed the night before.
THOMAS PENNY. I heard the cry of Stop thief! and ran out - the prisoner went some distance, through three or four courts, and came round into the street again. I had an opportunity of seeing him plainly when he came out of the shop, and am quite certain he is the man - I have no doubt whatever - he said at the office that he went in to buy a sheet of paper - I saw no pipe upon him at all.
Prisoner's Defence. I went out to buy some tobacco. I saw no one in the shop; I took a bit of paper out of my pocket to light my pipe from the gas, and it was the shadow of my arm that he saw when he came out. I certainly did run, but it was to avoid the unpleasantness of being locked up.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-156]
1743. THOMAS WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, 1 saw, value 5s.; 7 planes, value 24s.; 3 chisels, value 2s.; 1 rule, value 2s.; 1 pair of pincers, value 6d.; 1 gimblet, value 2d., and 1 pair of compasses, value 4d., the goods of Edward Milford.
EDWARD MILFORD. I am a carpenter, and was working, on the 3d of August, at an unfinished house in Kingsland-road. I left all my tools in an adjoining house, which was finished; and when I went, about six o'clock in the morning of the 4th of August, I missed most of my tools.
WILLIAM LOOSEMORE. I am a pawnbroker. This plough was pawned with me by the prisoner, on the 4th of August.
ALEXANDER INNES BURGESS. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner brought all the rest of these tools to me, which he said was his father's property. I suspected, and detained him.
THOMAS GARTON. I am an officer. I was sent for, and took the prisoner - I searched him, and found some compasses in his pocket, also two knives, and 3s.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw those tools in a field. I solemnly deny stealing them.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-157]
1744. JOHN BRALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of September, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Joseph Martindale, from his person.
JOSEPH MARTINDALE. On the 1st of September I was in Southampton-street, in the Strand, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night; a person stopped me in the street, and asked if I had lost any thing. I then missed my handkerchief - I did not see the prisoner till he was in custody.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I saw the prisoner following Mr. Martindale, who had two ladies on his arm. I saw the prisoner make several attempts to get the handkerchief, and, at the top of Southampton-street, he got it out - I rushed forward, and he threw it behind him - I seized him, and another person picked it up for me. I got a person to overtake Mr. Martindale, and told him of it.
Prisoner's Defence. This officer seized me, and said,"D-n you, I have got you at last." The gentlemannever gave charge of me - I was standing at the top of the street when the gentleman and ladies came by - I moved to let them pass, but had not touched the handkerchief.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-158]
1745. SARAH JACOBS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 20 sovereigns, and eight 5l. Bank notes, the property of John Thompson, from his person.
JOHN THOMPSON. I am a seaman. On the 25th of August I saw the prisoner, about six o'clock in the afternoon, in a public-house in Ratcliff-highway - I sat with her an hour or more, and drank with her; there was a bit of a wrangle, and I went out - she then took hold of my arm, took me along, put her hand into my bosom, and took my money out - there was 40l. in notes, and a parcel of gold; she snatched the purse out of my bosom, and gave me a shove: a man came up, and seized me right across the breast, and asked me what was the matter - I said I had been robbed; he gave a drive, and ran away after the woman in the crowd. I am quite sure she is the girl, but the man escaped - I went after her, but could not see her again, till, I think, two nights afterwards.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Had you ever seen her before? A. Yes, I had seen her some years back, but never had any acquaintance with her. I received the money the day before, at Somerset-house. I had passed Friday night at home at my lodgings - I had had a drop, but I knew what I was about - I turned out on Saturday morning about seven o'clock. I went into another public-house, but did not go to sleep - I went home to go to sleep- I first saw her about six o'clock in the evening, at the Ship and Shears public-house. I had been home to lie down, and went out in the afternoon to buy some things, and kept myself sober - the prisoner and several others came in - I was in that house from six till near nine o'clock- I believe I drank with all the women; about a quarter of an hour before I went into the street, I had overhauled my money, and put the heaviest part of it in my bosom - the other change I put into my jacket; when the man came up to me he collared me, and asked what was the matter - I said I had been robbed, and he gave me a drive, which staggered me across the street.
WILLIAM COLEMAN. I am a seafaring man. I was coming along Ratcliffe-highway one Saturday night, about four weeks ago. I saw the prisoner, who had hold of the prosecutor's right arm; another woman was with her; the prisoner said, "I will take you home;" and he said, "So do;" there was one Will Bucks there, whom I know very well, and some other persons - they tried to pull up his Guernsey frock; the prisoner then pulled something out of his bosom, I do not know what; and ran away in the crowd - one of the men caught him by the collar, and asked what was the matter - he said he had been robbed, but did not say how he had lost his money. I did not see that he fell down.
Cross examined. Q. He was a little groggy, I suppose? A. He had been drinking, but not much. It was one of the men who took hold of him - I do not know who - I did not see any of them strike him; the prisoner had a light gown, and I think a straw bonnet; she had a light shawl, with spots on it, which appeared to me in the dark to be black spots.
CHARLES LEBBY. I am a sailor, and was in Ratcliff-highway, on the evening in question; a man came to me and said, "This man will be robbed pretty soon;" there were four young men behind the prosecutor, who tried to get his frock up; the prisoner then put her hand into the frock, and took out something which looked like a pouch, and put it into her bosom; she then went into the crowd, and a young fellow came up, and took hold of the prosecutor by the collar - I heard him say, "I am robbed! I am robbed!" - I did not see him on the ground.
Cross-examined. Q. Could he have been knocked down without your seeing it? A. No - I knew the prisoner before by sight, but never knew her name; she had a straw bonnet on, and a dark gown, and a shawl, but I did not notice that.
WILLIAM NEEDHAM. I am the landlord of the Ship and Shears public-house; the prosecutor came into my house about the middle of the day, and left it about six o'clock, when we were lighting the gas. The prisoner and another came in - I asked where they were going; the prisoner made me some reply, but I told her to go along about her business, as she was one I never liked to see; she then went away, and the prosecutor immediately afterwards; he was not in company with her at all in my house; he had a sort of brown purse in his hand, and some one wanted him to leave it with me; and for me to give him a receipt for it; but he said, "I have worked for it, and I can take care of it;" he went out with the purse in his hand - I saw it had notes in it.
Cross-examined. Q. This you say was six o'clock in the evening; now, if he swore that he stayed till nine o'clock, he would swear that that was not true? A. Consequently he would; if he said he put the purse in his bosom, and if he said he stopped two or three hours, it would be untrue; it is all false; there were plenty of people saw him.
CATHERINE WHITE. On this Saturday, the prosecutor, who lodged with my father and mother, asked me to go with him to buy some shirts; we went out about half-past two o'clock; he went into a cook-shop, and asked me to have something to eat, which I would not; we went on, bought the things, and then went to Mr. Needham's: we got there about five o'clock; we had two or three pots of beer. I sat and drank with him about two hours or more - I gave the bundle we had been buying, to Mr. Needham's daughter, to take care of: a man then came in, and there was a bit of a wrangle; the prosecutor pulled off his jacket to fight, and he had his money in his hand - I said,"He will be robbed," and I wanted him to leave it there; he then went out of the house, I think between eight and nine o'clock: when he came to the door, there was a little woman there, but I cannot say who it was; he had paid some sovereigns for his goods, and there were some notes in the purse, but I do not know how many - I saw him go out of the house with the money in his hand - I went back and asked for the bundle at the bar; as he went out the little woman tapped him on the shoulder.
Cross-examined. Q. Then that little woman who tapped him on the shoulder had not been in the house? A. No, she had not, but kept looking in, while he was showing his money; he said he was a British seamen, and pulled out his money in his hand; as soon as he cameout of the house, he mixed with the crowd of persons who were passing, and I suppose he had the money in his hands; he was quite tipsy.
WILLIAM JUDGE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner up in company with Coleman, on suspicion of this robbery - Thompson was with me at the time; she said,"I know nothing at all about it" - I said "What made you run up Cannon-street so?" she said "I did not run, that I know of" - I was then going to take her to her lodging in Bluegate-fields, but she was going past, and said, she did not lodge there then - I said, "I know where you lodge," and took her to her room; I asked the landlady, if that was her room; she said it was - I then asked her what money she had got; she said all the money she had was one farthing, which was on the mantel-piece, and which I found - I then searched, and in a box under the bed I found this purse, and six sovereigns in it, which she said a seaman had left with her, when he went to sea.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not she know you were an officer? A. Yes, she did; she did not tell me where she lived, but was going past the place.
JOHN THOMPSON. This is not my purse. I know nothing of it.
Prisoner's Defence. I am innocent. A young man left me 4l. when he went to sea, and has sent me 2l. since.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-159]
1746. ROBERT TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 4 pairs of boots, value 4s.; 5 pairs of shoes, value 15s.; 5 bed-gowns, value 10s.; 9 caps, value 15s.; 1 watch, value 1l., and 1 coat, value 1l., the goods of Evan Thomas.
LOUISA THOMAS. I am the wife of Evan Thomas, tailor, of Old Tothill-street. On the 7th of August, the watch was in the drawer, the coat hung on the back of a chair, and the other articles were in the shop - the prisoner is a neighbour's son, and lives just by - I had seen these articles safe when I put up the shutters in the evening, and missed them about seven o'clock the next morning. The house had been entered by the back premises, through a hole, which had been forced in the partition - these shoes, and this bed-gown are mine.
THOMAS PACE. I am an officer. The two houses were formerly one - there is a partition now between them. I went to the prisoner's father's house, on the 8th of August, with a search-warrant - in one corner of the shop next to the prosecutor's premises, I found a hole, and a bag hanging over it; I lifted up the bag, and found this blue nightgown, and this pair of shoes; and on the other side of the partition, I found this cap, which the prosecutrix identified. I was looking after the prisoner the whole of the day, but the prosecutrix met him, and took him to the watch-house. I saw him there, and asked him what he had done with the things - he said he had given them to his brother, and knew no more about them. His brother, I am sorry to say, is a very bad lad. He was taken up, but not committed.
ROBERT GREENHILL. I am an officer, and received the prisoner at the watch-house. I found on him this key of the prosecutor's street-door, which hung up behind the door on the Monday, and was missed on the night of the robbery.
WILLIAM HALL. I am an officer. I went with Pace, and saw him find those things. I went round, and found where the hole came through, into a very dark place, under the prosecutor's staircase. GUILTY. Aged 12.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-160]
1747. ABRAHAM USILL was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, 1 fixture. (i.e.) 1 iron stove, value 10s., belonging to Richard Pemberton, and fixed to a certain building of his.
JAMES DENTON. I am clerk to Mr. Pemberton - he has a house in Wapping-street, which the prisoner agreed to take for 40l. a year. There was an agreement, which was signed by both parties, and the prisoner was to have the use of the fixtures - there were stoves fixed in the house at the time he entered, on the 28th of April. He said that he was in the iron way - he occupied the house, but never resided there; his lad and one of his men were there. On the 16th of July I went there, and missed a stove from the room, on the second floor - the prisoner was not there. This is the stove, which I have no doubt was in that room.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. You saw the house before it was let, and you saw this particular stove there? A. Yes; he was to be a yearly tenant; I had received the rent for it before then - the stove was fixed.
WILLIAM CRUTCHLEY. I was in the employ of the prisoner. I cannot say exactly when he went to this house, which is 265, Wapping-street - he did not carry on any business there. On the Saturday before I was taken up, he sent me to a green-grocer's, to say that he had a stove for sale - the green-grocer was not then at home, but he came afterwards and had the stove, which came, I think, from the first floor in that house - I had helped the prisoner to take it down the night before.
Cross-examined. Q. When was this? A. About seven o'clock one Saturday night, but I cannot tell what month. It was the week before he was taken up - he told me the things were his property, and I was not to take any notice.
SAMUEL GODDARD. I am a green-grocer, and live in Gravel-lane, The last witness came to me with a message one Saturday night, the 14th of July, I went with him to 265, Wapping-street - I there bought this stove of the prisoner - he asked me 16s., but I got it for 4s. 6d.
Cross-examined. Q. I suppose you gave the worth of it? A. I gave as much as it was worth to me - I might have kept it seven years; I gave a fair market price.
BENJAMIN BLABY. I am an officer. I took up the prisoner on a charge of taking away fixtures, out of this house at Wapping, and selling them - he said he thought they were his own - he had taken the house, and had a right to the fixtures.
Prisoner Defence. I took the house and warehouse, to carry on the ship-chandlery business; I undertook to put the house in repair; and this grate I was going to replace with a new one, which would have been three times its value. I had goods ordered, and I expected them in the day after I was taken, but I was obliged to countermand them to save my credit; I had not run away from the house - I was there two or three times a day.
ROBERT COATS. I am a carpenter, and live in Webber-street, Lambeth. I know the prisoner had taken a house, and there was an agreement between him and me, for me to put it in repair; this was about the 16th or 20th of June; I went there twice to see what repairs would be wanted: he said there were very old stoves on the premises, and I told him that I could recommend him to a person, who would let him have some modern stoves cheap; I had intended to have taken the old stoves myself, and made him an allowance: I have nineteen houses myself, and Lewis, who collects rents, was with me when this agreement was making; he made no secret at all about his taking the house, or of there being old stoves there.
MR. BRODRICK to BENJAMIN BLABY. Q. Did you go to give any countermand of orders? A. No; I went to a house where I understood he had given orders for some brushes, and while I was there, he came to urge the completion of an order, and I took him.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-161]
1748. ELIZABETH MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 1 watch, value 1l.; 1 ribbon, value 1d., and 1 watch-key, value 1d., the goods of Nicholas Murphy, from his person.
NICHOLAS MURPHY. I am a fisherman, and live at Limehouse; I have frequently seen the prisoner at her mother's house; I met her on the Monday morning, about ten o'clock, in the Bull's Head, public-house; I was with her all the rest of the day, in several public-houses; when it came on bed time, I asked her where she was going to sleep; she said she had no where in particular: I said I would go and pay for a bed for her; we went into the Match-walk, and I paid for a bed for her; I sat down by the bed side, and went to sleep; in the morning I missed my watch, and asked her about it; she said she knew nothing about it: I let her go, but she was taken afterwards. This is my watch - it was pawned at a pawnbroker's; but I have made two voyages since it was taken.
JAMES JEFFERY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner at the Three Compasses, public-house, in Shadwell-market, about a month after the watch was taken; she said she did not steal it, but the old woman stole it, and gave it to her to pawn.
JOHN GRINSHAW. I am a pawnbroker. This watch was pawned with me, on the 17th of July, by the prisoner at the bar.
Prisoner's Defence. It was given to me by a woman, to pawn for herself: I went and took her the money; she took 1s. of it, and told me to keep the rest: I met her three days after in the street - she asked me for the duplicate; I said I had it not in my pocket, but I would give it her when I got home.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-162]
1749. SARAH OWENS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of September, 1 purse, value 1d., and 9 shillings., the property of Richard Gee, from his person.
RICHARD GEE. I met the prisoner in Whitechapel, about half-past two o'clock in the afternoon of the 5th of September. I went with her up a court in Wentworth-street - she said she did not want money; but she asked me to give her something to drink. I went up stairs, gave her 4d. to get a quartern of gin - she fetched it, gave me a glass of it, and took the rest herself; she put the measure on the mantel-piece, and in a minute some one tapped at the door - she said "They want the measure;" she took it off the mantle-piece, and snatched my purse containing nine shillings, and ran down stairs. I went down and asked the mistress where she was; the woman told me to get out, and said she did not keep a bad house. I said I was sure she was there, but the woman would not let me see. I went and got an officer, and he found her at the next house.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was she searched at the watch-house? A. Yes - there was half a crown and a silver pencil-case found on her - this was about half an hour after. She did appear to be in ill health. I am a pensioner, and served his Majesty thirty years. I think I had seen her before in Whitechapel. I was quite sober I had only had a pint of beer.
ROBERT CRAIG. I am an officer. The prosecutor came to me, and said he had been robbed. I searched for the prisoner at the house, which is a brothel in Wentworth-street. I found her in the corner of the room in the next house, the door was locked.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you not threaten to send her over the herring-pond, as you call it? A. No - I did not - nor did the prosecutor. She had been standing in the corner of the room. I went as soon as the prosecutor gave me information, which was soon after three o'clock; she had had time to get away - the prosecutor appeared quite sober.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of the prosecutor. I had been put to bed but three weeks, and had not been out of the room; he came to the door and said I was the woman. He then begged my pardon, and said I was not; he then returned with the officer, and said I was the woman.
RICHARD GEE re-examined. Q. Did you say she was not the woman who robbed you, and beg her pardon? A. No - I never did.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-163]
1750. WILLIAM PLUCKROSE was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 1 umbrella, value 1s.,; 1 snuff-box, value 1s.; 1 pair of spectacles, value 7s., and 1 pocket-book, value 6d., the goods of John Roberts, from his person.
JOHN ROBERTS. I lost the articles stated, on the 25th of July. I had been dining with some friends in the City, I had drank rather freely. I went on to Holborn, and got into a hackney-coach - the coachman took my case in hand, and drove me home; there was a stranger followed me into the coach. I do not exactly know what happened there.
Prisoner. Q. You recollect that you introduced yourself first to me to take a pinch of snuff, and told me to tell the coachman to take you to Covent-garden, and in going along, you took the pocket-book out of your pocket, to see if I could find a card of your residence? A. I recollect nothing about it - I might do so. The next morning the coachman called on me, and asked if I had lost any thing. I said I had, and he desired me to attend at Marlborough-street.
WILLIAM LOCK. I am a hackneyman. The prosecutor went by me on the evening in question - the prisoner had the umbrella in his hand when he called me from the rank to take the gentleman in: I then drove Mr. Roberts to his house - we had some difficulty in finding it, but we found it by the card which the prisoner has been speaking of. I was paid by a person in the house, and was going home - when I had got about twenty yards, the prisoner called to me, that he had left his umbrella in the coach. He came up, opened the door, and got in, and was shuffling the cushions about; I got off, and told him an umbrella was easily found, to take it, and go along, which he did, and I went on for about half a mile, when he came after me again, and wanted to get in again, as he said he was going my way; I said he should not get in, but he might get upon the box, which he did. When we had got some distance he wanted me to let him get in again, for some other things; I said there were no other things - he said Yes, there was a silk handkerchief, with some pastry in it; I said I saw none, and there were none in the coach. I kept him in conversation until I found a watchman, and then gave charge of him; he was taken to the watch-house, and there said the umbrella was his own, and he said so again at the office; I told him to open the door and take the umbrella out, but I did not authorise him to get into the coach, nor was any thing said about it.
WILLIAM KIMBERLEY. I am an officer of St. George, Hanover-square. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house by a watchman and a coachman, about one o'clock in the morning; he had this umbrella in his hand - I searched the coach, and found the snuff-box and pocket-book in one corner, under the seat. The watchman gave me the spectacles, and said they had been taken from off the seat.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was going along Holborn, and when I came within four or five feet of Mr. Roberts I kicked against the umbrella; I took it up, and considered it as my own; there were four or five persons round Mr. Roberts, laughing at him; I said, "You had better call a coach;" the waterman said, "No one will take him unless some one goes with him;" I said it was a pity he should be out all night, and I would take him to his residence. Before that he had handed me his snuff-box. The waterman asked me for something, and I gave him a halfpenny; Mr. Roberts said he lived in St. Martin's-lane, Long-acre - we went there, but no such person was known. I said "Where do you live?" he said, No. 9, Wood-street, Cheapside - no such person was known there. He said, "It is all right - drive about all night;" he afterwards said, "Go to No. 8, Covent-garden-market;" the coachman said, "I will go there, and if that is not right I will put him into the watch-house;" in going along I asked him if he had got a card in his possession - he said Yes, pulled out a pocket-book, and it was No. 2, Newport-street; the coachman said he would go there: we found that was his residence, and there we left the gentleman. Now, do you suppose that if I knew there were such articles, that I should not have embraced the first opportunity of taking them? I certainly did call after the coach, and demanded the umbrella, which I considered as my own; I then went on a second time, and said to him, "If I had known you had been going my way, I might as well have rode as walked;" he said, "It is not too late now, get up." I then said, "I have left a silk handkerchief in the coach, which is of value to me - it cost my wife 3s. 6d.;" he replied, "I have a great notion you have been taking improper opportunities with that gentleman," and he gave charge of me."
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-164]
1751. HENRY READ was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 clasp, value 1s., and 3 rows of coral beads, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Jones, from the person of Sarah Jones , spinster.
THOMAS JONES. On the 10th of August my daughter Elizabeth was in the street, with my daughter Sarah, (who is seven months old,) in her arms. I heard a cry, went out, and saw the prisoner running - I pursued him, and called Stop thief! he turned up the first turning, and when he got near the top, two men stepped out to stop him - he then turned round, and I caught hold of him, and asked him for the beads: I then brought him back to my own door. - The beads were afterwards picked up in the kennel, two or three yards from where I collared him.
ELIZABETH JONES. I was at the door, with my little sister, Sarah, in my arms; the prisoner came up, with another person: the prisoner unclasped the beads, and took them from her neck; he ran into the middle of the road, and I screamed out, "Father!" I saw my father bring back the same person who had taken them.
SARAH KING. I saw the prisoner brought back - and in about a quarter of an hour or twenty minutes I was going out for a pint of beer, and picked up some beads in the kennel; I inquired where Mrs. Jones lived, and gave them to her.
JOHN SHIELDS. I am an officer. I received the beads and produce them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A lad ran past me, and pushed against me; I turned round, and immediately heard a cry of Stop thief! this gentleman took me, and said, "You have got my girl's beads;" I said, "What beads? I have not seen any."
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-165]
1752. MARGARET WALL was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 purse, value 6d.; 1 sovereign, 1 half-sovereign, 7 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the property of Thomas Camm, from his person.
THOMAS CAMM. On the 8th of September I was in Wentworth-street, Whitechapel, a little before twelve o'clock at night; I saw the prisoner: she came and took hold of my arm, and wanted me to go in doors with her, three or four times - I resisted; she then came in front of me, and took from my pocket my purse, containing one sovereign, one half-sovereign, seven shillings, and a sixpence; she ran away, and I followed close after her, till the watchman took her - I never lost sight of her. The night constable found the property in the watch-house.
Prisoner. You pushed against me twice, you was so much in liquor; I put my hand against you, to keep you from falling, and when the watchman came up he asked you if you had lost any thing. Witness. No - I deny all that.
PETER LUMLEY. I am a watchman. I stopped the prisoner, and took her to the watch-house; she had nothing on her, but I saw something laying on the ground; she had thrown some money away before she got to the watch-house - there was a sixpence of it picked up.
SAMUEL GREEN. I was in Wentworth-street last Saturday - Lumley called me to assist him to the watch-house with the prisoner - and on going along she threw something away; I stopped, and found this one sixpence.
JOHN DUNGATE. I am a watch-house keeper. I found this purse facing our table in the watch-house, under where the prisoner had stood; here is one sovereign, one shilling, and one sixpence in it, and from her person fell five penny pieces.
THOMAS CAMM. I know this purse to be mine, and here is a memorandum in it.
Prisoner's Defence. I had a sixpence and eleven pence in my hand; they knocked it out of my hand. The watch-house keeper got up to take the candle; he lifted up the flap of the table, and found the purse nearer to the prosecutor than where I was. The prosecutor was so much in liquor that he could not stand up to give the charge, and he said he had just left two other women.
PETER LUMLEY. No, he did not, upon my oath.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-166]
1753. STEWART McWATERS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 seal, value 1l., and 1 watch-key, value 5s., the goods of Benjamin Hobbs, from his person.
BENJAMIN HOBBS. I am a mason. I was at the Three Tuns public-house on the 13th of August - I was in liquor, and went to sleep (from four o'clock till about seven,) in the tap-room, and when I awoke my watch was gone; I had not seen the prisoner in the house - I know nothing about him; it was safe when I fell asleep.
LEONARD GEORGE NEEDS. I am a pawnbroker. This watch was pawned with me, by the prisoner, on the 13th of August, about ten minutes before five o'clock in the evening.
JOSEPH PRESTLEY. I was with the prosecutor at the Three Tuns; he was very tipsy and asleep; I did not see any thing of the prisoner.
DANIEL DAWKINS. I am a constable. I went to Knightsbridge barracks, and took the prisoner - he told me some girl had given him the watch.
Prisoner's Defence. The officer knows I got the watch from a companion, who was in liquor; and desired me to go and pawn it.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-167]
1751. MARGARET SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of August, 1 blanket, value 20s., the goods of Maria Dowman; and CATHERINE GRIFFIN was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing it to have been stolen.
MARIA DOWMAN. I live in Charlotte-street, Bloomsbury, and am single - Sullivan was in my service; I missed a table-cloth on the 6th of August; she had been with me but a few days. I said nothing about it till I missed the blanket; I then said no one could take it but her, and I would send for an officer - she then said she had taken it, and given it to a woman named Griffin, the wife of a watchman, and begged I would forgive her - I told her I could not, and I sent for Griffin, and asked her for the blanket and table-cloth - she then went with the officer and brought it. It had been in a locked cupboard on the Friday, and was gone on the Saturday - Sullivan came home very tipsy on the Saturday, and I spoke of it on the Monday.
Cross-examined by MR. QUIN. Q. Did not this blanket belong to a servant of yours? A. It belonged to a servant whom Sullivan succeeded; I had borrowed it of her, and it was in my closet.
PHILIP GURLEY. I am a pawnbroker. I took this blanket in pawn, on the 6th of August, from Griffin, in the name of Murphy.
GEORGE LANE. I am a patrol - I went with Griffin to her apartments, where I found the duplicate and this blanket.
SULLIVAN's Defence. This blanket was Mary McCarty 's, I took it to her to get my gown out, and to get me a shilling's worth of tea and sugar - the woman it belongs to, said that she could not swear to the blanket.
MARIA DOWMAN. This blanket was put on my bed when my own was washing. I can swear to it from the size, the moth holes, and from every particular.
SULLIVAN - GUILTY. Aged 27.
Confined One Month.
GRIFFIN - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-168]
1755. JONATHAN BROTHERTON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 1 plane, value 6d.; 1 chissel, value 3d.; 1 ladle, value 2d.; 7 toy candlesticks, value 7d.; 3 candlestick springs, value 2d.; 5 handles, value 2d.; 2 chains, value 2d.; 3 knobs, value 2d., and 3 tinman's tools, value 2s., the goods of Moses Simmons.
MOSES SIMMONS. I am a tinplate-worker. I lost my tools some time previous to the 7th of August. I had had them a great while before; I had seen some of them three or four months previous - I was ill in July, and when I came down again, I missed a copper tea-kettle from the back of my shop, which no one could have taken but the prisoner, who is in my employ - I told an officer; we searched his lodging, where we found some of these tools, which I know to be my property.
Prisoner. My master lent me these tools, and told me I might have any for my own work at any time. Witness. No, I did not.
JOSEPH TATUM. I live in Rosemary-lane. Some of these tools were brought to my shop by the prisoner, about six months ago, and I bought them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSEPH BARRS. I am an officer, and took the prisoner into custody.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Confined Nine Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-169]
1756. MICHAEL BRYAN was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of July, 1 watch, value 20s., the goods of Thomas Payne.
THOMAS PAYNE. I am a shoemaker, and live in Charles-street, Globe-fields - I missed my watch when I was going to bed on the 22d of July; it was safe attwenty minutes before seven o'clock that evening; the prisoner lodged with me, and came home that evening at twenty minutes before seven, and went out again; he came home at eleven o'clock, and I then gave charge of him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Will you swear that he had not left the house at half-past six o'clock? A. I will take my oath, that he came in between twenty minutes before seven and seven.
LEVY SOLOMON. I live in Rosemary-lane, about a mile from Globe-fields. As I stood at my door on the Sunday in question, the prisoner came and offered me a watch, and said he wanted money - I offered him 15s., and he agreed to take 18s., which I gave him; this was between nine and ten o'clock in the evening.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you ever seen him before? A. No. I do not know whether this is a usual time for selling watches, but I keep my Sabbath on a Saturday - I gave the watch to my son-in-law.
JOHN JACOBS. Mr. Solomon gave me this watch, and I put it into my pocket. I afterwards gave it to the officer.
Cross-examined. Q. How came you to give it to the officer? A. I was minding another shop for my father-in-law, and I shewed it to a fellow-shopman; we had some dispute about the value of it, and I said "Go to the pawnbroker's next door, and ask what they would lend upon it;" they said 1l.; the pawnbroker then gave information of it, and the officer came and took it.
ROBERT DAVIS. I am an officer. I produce this watch which I got from Jacobs on the Monday. The prisoner was apprehended on the Sunday.
JOHN BRADMAN. I am a watchman. I took charge of the prisoner on the Sunday night; I was going by at half-past ten o'clock, and Mr. Payne told me, if I saw the prisoner to take him; while I was there, he came home, and I took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MR. PHILLIPS to MR. PAYNE. Q. Was there not a young woman in your house? A. There was a young woman out of place, who slept with my daughter; but from the time she came in, till she went to bed, I kept sight of her; the seals of my watch were in my hat in the room where the prisoner slept; they were covered with this handkerchief; if he chose he might have taken them, but they were only brass.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-170]
1757. JOSEPH BAKER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 3 saddles, value 3l. 10s., the goods of Benjamin Sealy.
EDWARD HANDS. I am a watchman of Saint Luke's. On the 20th of August I went into Crown-court, between four and five o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner coming along the court, and another person with him. The prisoner had three saddles on his right shoulder - I caught hold of him, and he immediately threw them down - I called a porter to assist me, and we took him to the watch-house.
THOMAS MORGAN. I am foreman to Mr. Sealey; he is a livery stable-keeper. On the 19th of August I locked up these saddles safe in his stables - I went to the Leaping Bar public-house, to sleep, and the next morning, when I went to the stable, I found the door open, and the watchman there. I then looked and found the first padlock broken off; the second stable was on a springlock, that was broken - the third stable had a padlock, and the staple of that was drawn out. I know these saddles to be my master's.
JAMES MADDEN. I am a porter. I was coming out of my door, and saw the prisoner in custody of the watchman; he told me to take up the saddles. I put them into my basket, and took them to the watch-house.
JOHN BROWN. I am watch-house keeper. I went to see where they had been taken from, and found two locks broken, and the staple of the other drawn.
THOMAS FARROW. I am a watchman. My brother watchman called me to assist him, which I did.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going up Golden-lane, about half-past four, and met a man, who looked like a groom; he asked me to take these saddles to Smithfield. Soon after I took them, he made off.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-171]
1758. MARGARET BURKE was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of July, 11 yards of silk, value 30s., the goods of William Andrews and Nelson Andrews.
ROBERT LLOYD RICE. I am an assistant to Messrs. William and Nelson Andrews, silk-mercers, who live in Oxford-street. On the 28th of July the prisoner came and looked at some shawls; while I turned my back, I suppose, she took the silk, which I saw fall from her, under her clothes.
Prisoner. I met a young woman, who wanted me to go with her to buy a shawl - I went with her: the shopman got over the counter, and in getting over, he knocked a piece of silk down on the floor - which I know was black; this is brown - and at the office he said he did not know whether it fell from me or from the counter. Witness. I saw her doing something which I thought was adjusting her stocking - I thought she appeared different to what she was before; and I said, "You have got something, I am sure." I then took hold of her, and it fell from her - I took hold of the other, but she went away: the prisoner acknowledged afterwards that she had taken it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSEPH WORMALD. I received charge of the prisoner. I searched her and found two half-crowns and a duplicate - in going along she asked me what I thought she would be done to - I said I could not tell.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-172]
1759. JOSEPH EASTON was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, 1 box, value 4s.; 7 gowns, value 38s.; 3 pairs of stays, value 12s.; 1 veil, value 3s.; 2 caps, value 5s.; 2 petticoats, value 3s.; 3 pairs of stockings, value 3s.; 1 shawl, value 2s.; 1 pair of boots, value 3s.; 1 tea-caddy, value 3s.; 3 aprons, value 2s.; 4 collars, value 4s., and 1 pocket-book, value 2d., the goods of Caroline Stokes.
CAROLINE STOKES. I am single, and live in Wilde-court, Lincoln's-inn-fields. On the 27th of August I was leaving my situation at the George and Blue-boar, public-house, in Holborn - I took one large box away in a hackney-coach, and forgot to take another large box, whichcontained the property in question - when we got to Wilde-court, the coachman took that one box up stairs with me; and then I came down to look for the other, but it was not there; the coachman said there had been but one box put in - I then went back to the inn and my box was gone; there was a man with the coachman - but I do not know who he was.
ELIZA DWELLY. I am bar-maid at the George and Blue Boar. I saw the prosecutrix go away; she went from the back door, and the prisoner took the luggage out of the house. He took a large box and some other trifling things - in a short time she returned, and asked if she had not left a box there - I said she had, but the man who took the first box out, had come back and taken it.
WILLIAM PHILLIPS. I am a porter at the George and Blue Boar. I recollect bringing two trunks down stairs - a man took the first one away; I then brought down the other, and the same man took that; but I do not know who he was.
FRANCIS FAGAR. I am an officer. On the 30th of August the prosecutrix gave me information, and from the description I received from Dwelly, I took the prisoner on the 5th of September. I afterwards discovered where a young woman lived who cohabited with the prisoner - I went to her lodging, and there got a duplicate from a box of a gown and pair of stays; I then found three gowns and some other articles, at two other pawnbrokers - I found no duplicates of them, but the young woman pointed out where they were.
MARY JONES. I live at Poole's-place. The prisoner lived with me about two months ago; he gave me these articles, and said he had found them - I pawned them.
DANIEL STEPTOE. I am a pawnbroker - I took in some of these articles from Jones.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I cannot say what day of the month it was; but on the same evening the prosecutrix says she lost the things, I was at Islington on business, and staid there till between one and two o'clock in the morning - I was coming along between Exmouth-street, half asleep and half awake, and I kicked against a bundle tied in a handkerchief - I took it home, and in the evening I met this young woman, and asked her to pawn them.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-173]
1760. ROBERT EDMONDS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 waistcoat, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Jorden.
THOMAS JORDEN. I am a tailor, and live in Monmouth-street. On Saturday last I offered this waistcoat for sale to a man in the street - while I was in conversation with him the prisoner came up and took it from me; he ran, and I followed him, and cried Stop-thief! his companions came across him, and took it from him.
JOHN FALIN. I heard a noise and went out - I took the prisoner into custody. The prosecutor was standing by him- he said, at the watch-house, that if I would go with him to a certain court in the Strand, I might, perhaps, find the waistcoat.
THOMAS FAGAN. I am an officer. I was in Broad-street; I saw the prisoner running, and the prosecutor after him - I stopped the prisoner, but saw no waistcoat on him.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-174]
1761. ANN GOWEN was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, 1 pail, value 2s., the goods of John Tarber.
MARY TARBER. I am the wife of John Tarber. He is a porter, and lives in Feathers-court, Strand; on the 6th of September I went out about half-past seven o'clock in the morning, and left my pail in the yard - I returned in about ten minutes, and it was gone.
SARAH DOBSON. I live opposite Mrs. Tarber. I saw the prisoner go into her house with a basket, and come out with a pail in the basket; she gave the basket to another person who was with her.
THOMAS RICE. I pursued the prisoner, and took this pail from her; she had been into my house first.
SUSANNAH STEELE. On the 6th of September, about half-past seven o'clock in the morning, my neighbour called to me, and said a woman had stolen a pail - I went down, and saw her with a pail in her hand, and another woman with her; I pursued her till Rice came up and took her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I did not take the pail - the other woman said, "Hold this pail," and I had it not five minutes.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Confined Sixteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-175]
1762. JOHN GILLMORE and JOHN CLARKE were indicted for stealing, on the 2d of August, 2 shoes, value 7s., the goods of James Pearcy.
JAMES PEARCY. I am a warehouse-keeper, and live in St. John-street, Smithfield. I lost two odd shoes on the 2d of August; the officers brought them back to me, and the two prisoners. I had seen them in my shop window about an hour before.
JAMES NEWTON. I am an officer. I was going to work, and saw the prisoners lurking about Mr. Pearcy's shop window; I saw Clarke go into the shop and return in about half a minute, with something under his coat - Gillmore then went into the shop, but came out again directly, and they went away together; I went up to them and found these two shoes (which I produce,) on Clarke.
GILLMORE's Defence. As I was going along I met this lad, he saw these two shoes lying at the door, and took them.
CLARKE's Defence. I saw these shoes near the door, and I said to Gillmore, "Shall I take these up, and give to the beadle?" he said "Yes. if you like." I took them.
GILLMORE - GUILTY. Aged 16.
CLARKE - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-176]
1763. HARRIET HORNET was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September, 2 pewter pots, value 3s. 6d., the goods of Francis Mason.
FRANCIS MASON. I keep the Olive Branch public-house, in Gray's-inn-road . I know these two pots to be mine - I loose a great many of them.
JOHN BLAKE. I am a constable. I took these two pots from the prisoner, as she was coming out of the OliveBranch yesterday week; she had one in her reticule basket, and one in her apron.
Prisoner's Defence (written.) I have been a cripple for two years, and am bed-ridden. I have undergone several operations, which has reduced me to great distress - I parted with every thing I had in the world. In coming up Swinton-place, I saw these pots on the rails; knowing them to be Mason's, I took them, intending to take them home; I had half-a-pint of beer in his passage, and then went to the corner; before I thought of delivering the pots. I was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18270913-177]
1764. JAMES HOWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 leg of lamb, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Richard Sargent.
RICHARD SARGENT. I am a butcher, and live in Poland-street, Hanover-square. On the 10th of August I came in and missed a leg of lamb; the prisoner had been into my yard - I met him in the passage, and stopped him with it in his hand.
LEWIS JULIAN. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had found the lamb tied in a bundle.
GUILTY. Aged 45.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Seven Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-178]
1765 JOSEPH HILL was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of August, 1 pair of trousers, value 11s.; 1 pair of ear-rings, value 1s. 6d., and 1 key, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Gager.
ANN GAGER. I am the wife of Thomas Gager - he is a brewer; he supplied the prisoner and other persons with beer, at the St. Katherine-docks. On the 11th of August I lost the duplicate of a pair of trousers, and a pair of earrings out of this box; the prisoner had been in the habit of coming there, but on that Saturday he came with two other persons to have his bill made out. I had seen the articles on the mantel-piece before he came in, and on the Monday they were gone.
JOHN SPENCER. I am shopman to Mr. Watts, a pawnbroker. I took in this pair of trousers from the prisoner, on the evening of the 11th of August.
CHARLES WILLIAMSON. I know these trousers; I had taken them from Mrs. Gager, and had lent her 3s. on them- I do not know who it was took them out of pawn, but my young man, who has now left me, swore at the Police-office, in the prisoner's presence, that he was the man - the prisoner denied it.
Prisoner. He said, to the best of his knowledge, I was one of the three who went to the shop. Witness. No, he said so positively.
Prisoner's Defence. She came and chastised me about the trousers, but I know nothing about them.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-179]
Fourth Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1766. EDWARD JOHNSTON was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, 9 live tame fowls, price 18s., and 1 bag, value 1s., the property of Thomas Jenkins.
JAMES HORNE. I live at Hendon, and work for Mr. Thomas Jenkins. I went to work about half-past six o'clock, on the 25th of August, and on going through a little wicket-gate, into a bleaching-ground, I saw a wicket open, which goes into an orchard. I looked further, and saw the string was cut, which fastened the wicket. I directly went to the hen-house, found it was broken open, and nothing in it, but one guinea cock - I informed the servant - I went into the road, and met a man, whom I asked about the fowls; he asked me if I should know them again; I said, Yes, and one in particular. I have seen them since, and know them.
WILLIAM TOOLEY. I am a constable. On the morning of the 25th of August I heard some fowls go by my window; I got out of bed, looked out, and saw some men go past with fowls - I got up, with a neighbour, followed them, and took them; the prisoner was one - he said he was employed to carry them, and was to have three halfpence and his breakfast, when he got to London. I found six other fowls on another person - the prisoner had three in this bag - the other person ran away.(Fowls produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-180]
1767. THOMAS METCALFE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 1 pewter pot, value 1s. 8d., the goods of Walter Morrison.
JAMES MATTHEWS. I am a builder. On the 7th of August my son came and told me that a man had taken a pot, and put it under his apron. I went out and saw the prisoner running in Augustus-street, Regent's-park - he jumped down the area of an unfinished house - I could not tell which, but I waited a short time - two men came up, and I beckoned to them to watch; I went into a house, where I saw him standing. I asked where the pot was; he said he knew nothing about it; I brought him out, and then he said if I would let him go, he would tell me where it was; we then went into the house, and one of the other men found the pot behind some bricks. I took him to Mr. Morrison.
GEORGE MATTHEWS. I am the son of this witness. I saw the prisoner take the pot off the rails, and went and told my father.
WILLIAM JEMMERSON. I am a constable. I know Mr. Walter Morrison - he keeps a public-house in Augustus-street, Regent's-park; this pot is marked with his name; he cannot attend - he has put his mark upon it - it is marked"W. Morrison, Regalia, Augustus-street, Regent's-park."
Prisoner's Defence. I was returning home to Water-lane, a person overtook me, and said I had a pot, but I had none.
GUILTY. Aged 47.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-181]
1768. JOHN MARTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 3 saws, value 20s.; 1 axe, value 1s., and 1 rope, value 6d., the goods of John Cooper; and 1 bell, value 1s., the goods of John Meggs.
JOHN COOPER. I am a sawyer. I was at work for Mr. Meggs, in Kingsland-road. I left three saws and a line, in the saw-pit house where I was at work, about six o'clock, on the 16th or 17th of August; I fastened the door after me, and when I returned the next morning, the place hadbeen broken open, and the tools were gone - the saws were found at a saw-maker's, in Whitechapel, a day or two afterwards.
JOHN FOALE. I work for Mr. Hughes, a saw-maker, of Church-lane, Whitechapel. I bought these saws of the prisoner, as old steel; they weighed 171/2lbs. I gave him 11/2d. a lb. for them. I did not ask him whether they were his - I considered them as old saws - and, being worn out, I bought them.
JOHN COOPER. These are my saws.
Prisoner' Defence. A man sent me to sell them, and said I was to have 21/2d. per lb.
GUILTY. Aged 56.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-182]
1769. JOHN MUNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, 5 yards of floor-cloth, value 15s., the goods of Charles Barron.
JOSEPH LEATHART. I lodge with Charles Barron, in Upper Seymour-street - he is a cabinet-maker. Soon after four o'clock on the morning of the 24th of August; when I came down, I missed this floor-cloth, which must have been drawn from under the door. I went up stairs again, and in about a quarter of an hour I looked out of the window, and saw a person standing against the gas-light post. The prisoner came up to him with this roll of cloth under his arm - I went down and pursued them - the other got away - I took the prisoner into custody; when I first saw him he was about four doors from our house - it was then about five o'clock. The other person had something in his hand, but I could not see what - the prisoner said he found it.
CHARLES BARRON. I am master of this house. I know this cloth to be mine.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going along, and that young man asked me to carry this for him, which I did. He turned his head and said "There is somebody coming," and he ran away. I did not run because I had done no harm.
GUILTY. Aged 58.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-183]
1770. HENRY MOORE was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of August, 1 hat, value 10s., the goods of Thomas Green Phillips.
CHARLES NEWTON. I am servant to Mr. Thomas Green Phillips, a hatter, of Hackney-road. On the 2d of August, about two o'clock in the day. I saw the prisoner about two hundred yards from Mr. Phillips', with this hat on his head. I went up to him - he stopped, took it off, and said"Is this your hat?" I took it from him, and he ran away. I followed, and cried Stop thief! - he was taken in Union-street.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Where did you place it? A. On a rail outside of the door, tied with a string. I did not see him untie it.
RICHARD RILEY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and produce the hat.
THOMAS GREEN PHILLIPS. I am a hatter. This hat is mine - it was outside my door. I know nothing of the prisoner.
Cross-examined. Q. How many hats do you tie outside your door? A. Sometimes twelve - it is a great thoroughfare, but it is a wide pavement. Four witnesses gave the prisoner a good character, one of whom engaged to take him into his employ.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-184]
1721. WILLIAM ORSON and JOHN JAMES LAMB were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, 1 jacket, value 6s., the goods of William Bruce.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. On the 30th of July, I was in Whitechapel - I know Mr. Bruce, he lives at Whitechapel-road, and is a salesman. I saw the two prisoners come up to his shop - they passed it too and fro, in conversation together. I saw Lamb take this jacket, and put it into his apron. I ran across the road, and took hold of them both together.
Prisoner LAMB. Q. Did you see Orson along with me? A. Yes - he was about a yard or a yard and a half from him at the time he took the jacket.
DANIEL PARRY. I am an officer, and was with Keys; what he has stated is correct. I had seen the prisoners in company together for days before.
WILLIAM BRUCE. I keep a shop in Whitechapel-road. I missed this jacket from the front of my shop - I know it to be mine.
LAMB's Defence. I was destitute of house and harbour. I had applied to the Tower to be sent to the Navy, and they would not receive me, being wounded through my left leg; and, seeing the jacket, I was tempted by hunger and starvation to take it. I was paid off at Yarmouth some months ago. Orson was not with me - he only came and asked me the occasion of the mob.
ORSON - GUILTY. Aged 28.
LAMB - GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-185]
1772. JAMES PICKERING was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, 1 jacket, value 7s.; 1 rule, value 6d., and 1 handkerchief, value 2d., the goods of John Kidgell.
JOHN KIDGELL. I am a labourer, and work at Milbank. On the 27th of August I put down my jacket, waistcoat, and hat, between two and three o'clock, by the side of some bushes; a person called to know if I had lost my jacket; I looked up, and saw the prisoner with it in his right hand; I went up to him, and he dropped it, but he had not power to run, as he was hardly clear from the bushes - I had seen him about a quarter of an hour before, looking about the ground.
WILLIAM CLARKE. I am employed at Milbank. I saw the prisoner take the jacket, and I went and told the prosecutor; I saw him put his arm through the bushes, and he was pulling the jacket through - I am sure he is the man.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me with the jacket in my hand? A. I saw you drawing it through the bushes.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 54.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-186]
1773. WILLIAM PUTNAM was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August, 2 live tame geese, price 8s., and 2 live tame ducks, price 4s., the property of John Worman.
SARAH WORMAN. I live at Edgware, and keep geese and ducks. On the 9th of August, between one and two o'clock in the morning, I lost three ducks and two geese;my ducks were in the hen-house, and the geese under the cart-lodge; I had locked them up the night before, but the place was broken open in the night; I saw them again at Mary-le-bone office next morning; my husband's name is John.
JAMES GIBBS. I am an officer. On Thursday, the 9th of August, I was with Webster, at the top of Lisson-street, about half-past ten o'clock in the morning; we saw the prisoner with a hamper; the ostler of the Red Lion came and took the hamper from him; I followed, and saw them go down a mews, and pitch the hamper; I went and took it, and found in it three ducks and two geese, which had just been killed - they were quite warm; they were both taken, but the ostler was discharged.
PHILIP WEBSTER. I was with Gibbs; his statement is correct; the prisoner was first carrying the ducks, and when I stopped him, he said his cousin had given them to him.
Prisoner's Defence. They were found on another person - he gave them to me to carry down the street.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Publicly Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-187]
1774. JOHN AARON STUNT was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of July, 1 watch, value 30s., the goods of Edward Edwards.
LYDIA ANN EDWARDS. I am the wife of Edward Edwards; he lives in Howard's-place, St. James, Clerkenwell; he has been in the silver trade some years; I have known the prisoner for some years - I am mother-in-law to his mother. On the 12th of July, I had been out, and when I came home I went to the fire-place to look at the watch - it was near twelve o'clock; I heard a knock at the door it was the prisoner; he came in and said his father would send me 20l. the next day or the day after; he then asked for a little water; I said, "I will go and get some beer, and what you leave will serve for our dinners;" I got him some, and he then said, "I must go, or my master will stop my money;" I said, "What money?" he said, "My master allows me a guinea a week while I am apprentice;" as soon as he got out I saw him running; I went back to look at the watch, and I saw it was gone - it must have been taken while I was gone to fetch the beer - it was a silver watch, capped and jewelled; I sat down in a chair, and thought I should have died.
EDWARD EDWARDS. The watch was my property; I left it safe in the morning, and when I came home it was gone; the prisoner has been bound apprentice to a goldbeater in Fetter-lane, but left in May last.
AARON STUNT. I am the prisoner's father; I have to pay Mrs. Edwards 50l. a year, which was a legacy left by my wife's father, during her life, which I have paid for these twenty years; I did not send my son to her at that time, (nor for months prior), with any message whatever - I know nothing more.
THOMAS VANN. I am an officer, and apprehended the prisoner on the 15th.
Prisoner's Defence. The watch was missed on the Thursday, and no application was made to any office till about an hour before I was taken.
MRS. EDWARDS re-examined. Q. Had any one else come into the room, who could have taken the watch? A. No, no one else.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-188]
1775. WILLIAM THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, 1 time-piece, value 10s., the goods of Richard Millen.
RICHARD MILLEN. I live in Cow-cross-street; I am a broker. On the 5th of August about six o'clock in the afternoon, this time-piece stood in my shop about six yards from the back part; I was in the parlour, and heard the shop door open; I went in, and the prisoner had moved this time-piece about four yards, and was putting it into a pail, which he had brought with him. When I saw him, he pretended that he wanted to sell the pail.
JAMES JERRY. I am a watch-flutcher. I was returning home, and saw the prisoner with a pail in Cow-cross-street - I thought it was curious to carry it on a Sunday, and I watched him to the shop; I saw him with the timepiece in his hand.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that he had gone to the shop to sell the pail, but denied having touched the time-piece.
GUILTY. Aged 61.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-189]
1776. GEORGE WEBB was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 2 oaken posts, value 8s., and 5 pieces of oak, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Jones.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
THE REY. THOMAS JONES. I am a clergyman, and have a house at Enfield. The prisoner was in my employ till the latter end of April. On the 6th of March I returned from the Contiment, and on the 7th. as I was walking over my premises, I missed some posts from the drying ground; he said he did not know they were missing - I left England again, and returned on the 6th of July. My gardener had before that discharged the prisoner - on the 20th of July I applied for a search-warrant, to have his premises searched.
JOHN WILSON. I went to execute the search-warrant at the prisoner's lodgings, I found these two shafts and a cart, which appeared to be the same wood that Mr. Jones had lost - I also found these rails.
WILLIAM SIDNEY SMITH. I am an officer. I was present at the execution of this warrant - I found this tailladder; these articles were all on the cart, which were found on his premises.
REV. MR. JONES re-examined. Q. Have you examined the wood? A. I believe it to be wood taken from my premises - I am convinced the shafts must have been my linen-posts, because they are perfectly similar in the nature of the wood, and because the two ends of the shafts are very dark, in having been in the ground and there me some of the rails in the ladder - they are the same kind of oak with some that have, and I am inclined to conceise it to be mine - I have not the least doubt in the world of it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You are a clergyman you state? A. Yes; I am chaplain of Whitehallchapel. This paint on the rails, exactly corresponds with the paint on the rails of my sheepfeld - and there are five in another which corresponds with the others I have lost,and the nail holes correspond with the nail holes in my fold - I did not miss these rails till the cart was found - I remained in this country till the end of April. These things usually belong to carts; my carpenter told me that this is a foreign oak; he is dead. I bought them of a timber-merchant at Edmonton - I was not inclined to press the case, because I did not consider it sufficiently strong.
COURT. Q. Were you under recognizance to appear? A. Yes. The prisoner was to take care of my horses, cows and sheep, when I went abroad. The posts were fixed in the earth; they were not in sockets, but in the earth.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-190]
1777. JOHN WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 4 half-crowns, and 15 shillings, the monies of Robert Goodson.
ELIZABETH GOODSON. I am the wife of Robert Goodson - he lives in Rosemond-street, Clerkenwell. Between two and three o'clock in the afternoon of the 25th of July, I was sweeping the parlour, and on turning round I saw the prisoner on the wrong side of the counter; he appeared to be returning from the till; I was frightened, and let him go: I then called Watkins out of the bake-house, who pursued - he ran down Corporation-lane, but was taken; he had taken two handsfull of silver, and left the till open: there was 8s. 6d. found on him, but I missed more than that; I had seen the till safe not two minutes before. The prisoner was a stranger to me.
THOMAS WATKINS. The prosecutrix called me - I pursued the prisoner, and caught him in Ashford-street; he had 8s. 6d. in his hand; he offered it to me, but I refused to take it.
GEORGE HEFFER. I am an officer. I received charge of the prisoner, and produce the money, which I have had ever since.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-191]
1778. JAMES WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August. 5 handkerchiefs, value 10s., the goods of Thomas Dexter.
WILLIAM COVENTON. I am in the employ of Thomas Dexter - he is a pawnbroker, and lives in Whitechapel-road. On the 4th of August I saw the prisoner take these five handkerchiefs off the ground - I cannot tell how they came there; I had hung them up in the morning on a book, about two yards within the shop; it was between two and three o'clock; he stood just outside and reached in - he had not been in the shop; I followed him, stopped him, and asked what he had got in his hat - he said nothing. I told him a person had seen him put some handkerchiefs into his hat, and desired him to come back - he said he would; he turned, and came back a few steps - he then gave me the handkerchiefs out of his hat, and wished me to leave him - I said"No, I will take you back;" he kept dodging me about, and then ran away. I pursued, called for assistance, and some gentleman stopped him, and took him to my master's, who gave him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
