FOURTH DAY. MONDAY, SEPTEMBER 17.
Third Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1779. SARAH PROOM was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, 1 shift, value 2s., the goods of William Dunford.
The prisoner pleaded GUILTY. Aged 40.
Confined Seven Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-193]
1780. SAMUEL SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of July, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Burge, from his person.
JOHN BURGE. On the 23d of July I was at the Camel public-house, Kingsland-road; I had been playing at Skittles, and pulled my jacket off - my handkerchief was in the pocket; I missed it - I made inquiries, and no one knew any thing of it; I had the doors closed, and every body searched. The prisoner had been there, but was gone - he returned in about a quarter of an hour, and was told what had taken place; he came to me, and said, "I beg your pardon - I know where your handkerchief is, and if you will go with me you shall have it;" we went to a stall in Huntingdon-street, just by - he asked Archer's wife for the handkerchief which he had left there; she said her husband was gone out, and had got it with him; he then said to me,"It is all right - you shall have your handkerchief;" I said,"When I get the handkerchief I shall say it is all right - but you must go back with me;" Constantine came, and took him, and as we were going to the office we met Archer, running with the handkerchief. My jacket had been off a quarter of an hour or twenty minutes - I do not know whether it was taken while my jacket was on or not.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Were not many persons about? A. Yes, and many coats and hats were off- it might have been put into the prisoner's hat. When I first missed it I offered half-a-gallon of beer to any one who would give it me, if they had done it for a joke. The prisoner told me he knew where the handkerchief was, but the officer had then been sent for.
COURT. Q. Did he know the officer had been sent for? A. Yes - I told him myself, when he returned, but at the time the officer was sent for he was not there. When Archer came, he said he had brought the handkerchief, which the prisoner had left with him, and told him he would call again presently; neither Archer nor his wife said he left it with them to find the owner - he said he did it for want - he had had no work for a long time.
JOHN ARCHER. The prisoner put this handkerchief into a little basket under my stall, and said he would call for it presently. As soon I heard what had happened I went and took it down to Constantine.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that he had put his hat on the ground while he played at skittles, and after putting it on again he found a handkerchief in it, which he left with Archer, to take care of while he went to inquire who had lost one. He received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-194]
1781. JAMES RYAN was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Andrew Garrett, from his person.
ANDREW GARRETT. On the 4th of August, at a quarter before nine o'clock in the evening, I was in Elm-street, Gray's Inn-lane; the prisoner and another person had made an attempt at my pocket, while I was in Cold Bath-fields, but when I got to Elm-street, the prisoner snatched the handkerchief from my pocket; he ran away - I pursued him, and took him to the watch-house; when I turned round he was secreting it in his breast, but in running he threw it away, and I have never seen it since.
Cross-examined by MR. QUIN. Q. Elm-street, I believe, is not a very light street? A. There are gas-lights in it. I am positive he is the person who made the two attempts - it was my handkerchief he was secreting; he turned several corners, but I did not lose sight of him till I secured him, in Laystall-street.
JOHN HUTT. I received the prisoner at the watch-house, but I found nothing on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going towards Spa-fields - I met a person I knew, and when I got to the corner of Elm, street I turned down a court leading to Mount-pleasant and some person called Stop thief! I never saw the handkerchief.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-195]
1782. WILLIAM STEVENS and JOHN STRATFORD were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Francis Weedon.
FRANCIS WEEDON. On the 30th of July I was in Goswell-street, with my wife, about half-past eight o'clock in the evening; she said she felt something at her arm - I felt my pocket, and my handkerchief was gone, which I had used within an hour. I saw two boys go by at the instant, but I cannot identify them - Roe immediately came up, and asked if I had lost any thing; he said, if I would follow him, he thought he could find the persons who had it; we went on; he turned a corner, and took Stevens - I followed him, and took hold of Stratford - I saw Stevens drop my handkerchief from his pocket - I took it up, and put it into my pocket; there were no other persons round at that time.
JOHN ROE. I was in Goswell-street, and saw the prisoners, with two others, following this gentleman and lady; and saw the gentleman feeling his pocket - I asked if he had lost any thing; he said he had - I went on to Turk's Head-court, and found the two prisoners; the witness caught hold of Stratford - I am quite sure they were with two others whom I had seen altogether just before.
JAMES FORDHAM. I received the prisoner, at the watch-house, and produce the handkerchief, which I received from Mr. Weedon.(Property produced and sworn to.)
STEVENS - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
STRATFORD - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-196]
1783. MARY LAWRENCE was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 shawl, value 2s. 6d., the goods of George Yardley.
JANE YARDLEY. I am the wife of George Yardley; we live in Church-street, Bethnal-green. On the 7th of August I went out about eleven o'clock, and left the key in my door - I returned in a quarter of an hour, and found the key had been turned, and my shawl gone off my table - I saw it again at Mr. Blitz's door, on the Friday following - I know it by a darn, which is my own work - I know nothing of the prisoner.
JUDITH BLITZ. The prisoner brought this shawl to me on the 8th of August, and I bought it of her - I had not seen her before; she came to my shop again on the Sunday morning, and I sent for the officer.
RACHAEL BLITZ. I was present when my mother bought this shawl of the prisoner.
GEORGE KERSHAW. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-197]
1784. WILLIAM CROPLEY and SAMUEL GRIFFITHS were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of August, 4lbs. weight of bacon, value 2s., the goods of Ethelbert Holborow.
EDWIN CLEWETT. I am shopman to Ethelbert Holborow, cheesemonger of Clare-street, Clare-market. On Wednesday evening, the 1st of August, I was at the window, and offered two pieces of bacon to a woman; she refused them, and had some cut off; in about half an hour the officer came, and asked if I had lost any thing - I looked, and said I had lost two pieces of bacon, which I described - I know nothing of the prisoners.
WILLIAM McGREGOR. I was on duty, and saw the two prisoners going by the shop - Griffiths took one piece of bacon, and as I was crossing to him, Cropley took the other; they ran down Stanhope-street, where I took them, and found it on them.
DANIEL REARDON. I was with McGregor, and saw what he has stated.
CROPLEY - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Six Weeks, and Whipped.
GRIFFITHS - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Confined Four Weeks and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18270913-198]
1785. JAMES CLARK was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 10 yards of printed cotton, value 5s., the goods of William Dobinson.
WILLIAM MACE. I am shopman to William Dobinson, linen-draper, of High-street, Shadwell. On the afternoon of the 31st July, I was serving in the shop, and heard a girl say a man had taken a piece of print - I jumped over the counter, and found the prisoner about twenty-five yards off, with the print in his hand.
GEORGE SMITH. I saw the prisoner in the street; he was coming towards me - when he came up I told him to surrender himself, or I would knock him down; he would not - I knocked him down, and kept him till Mace came up and found the print.
JOHN MIDDLEDITCH. I took up the prisoner; he said distress had tempted him to do it, and the print was outside the door, which was in his favour.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-199]
1786. ROBERT FERGUSON was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of August, 1 tea-kettle, value 2s., the goods of Rebecca Southey.
WILLIAM BENNS. I am a special constable. At a quarter before six o'clock on the morning of the 18th of August, I saw the prisoner and another walking to and fro, in Torrington-square - I watched them, and saw the prisoner take a cord from his pocket, and drop it down the area of No. 4; he drew up this kettle, put it under his arm, and gave the cord to the other - I went up and took him with the kettle; he said he had done it for a lark.
MARY STALLAN. I live with Mrs. Rebecca Southey(who is single), at No. 4, Torrington-square; this is her kettle - I had set it out about a quarter of an hour before; the constable came and asked if it was mine.
Prisoner's Defence. It was not done with an intention to rob.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-200]
1787. HENRY SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of July, 13 yards of silk, value 2l. 9s.; 11/2 yards of calico, value 1s. 3d., and 1 piece of cord, value 2d., the goods of William Sweet.
SOPHIA SWEET. I am the wife of William Sweet - he is a servant in Mr. Harford's family, Piccadilly; we lodge at No. 9, Bryanstone-street, Portman-square. On the 5th of July there was thirteen yards of silk, one yard and a half of white calico, and some cord, lying on the kitchen dresser, near the window - I had seen it safe between twelve and one o'clock that day, and missed it in about a quarter of an hour - I saw it again at the pawnbroker's, about the 10th of July.
Cross-examined by MR. QUIN. Q. Had not you lost several other things? A. Yes; there was a servant in that house taken up on suspicion, but he was discharged; I never saw the prisoner near the house.
GEORGE WILLOUGHBY PERUZZI. I am a pawnbroker. I have thirteen yards of silk, some calico, and some cord, which were pawned by the prisoner on the 5th of July - this prosecutrix called in a few days afterwards, and claimed them as hers - the prisoner came again on the Monday, and brought a coat for sale, and I stopped him; he gave his direction, No. 32, William-street.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you know the prisoner? A. Yes; I had seen him before, and am positive of his person.
JOHN ANDREWS. I am an officer. I was fetched to the pawnbroker's; I found the prisoner there; he had brought a coat and some shoes to redeem some silk, as I understood.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been after a place, and as I returned home, a man came and asked if I wanted to buy any silk - he wanted 30s. for it, and I gave him 26s. - there was a person with me, who is here.
HENRY SANDERS. I live at No. 17, Circus-street - this silk resembles some silk I saw a man offer for sale at the Stingo-gate, in the early part of July, I cannot tell the day - it was offered to me, but as my wife had a silk dress I did not buy it - it was not the prisoner who offered it - it was an elderly man; he offered it to several people there; I am there every day, to take up the coachmen's tea - there was a coachman here of the name of Hood, but he is gone.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-201]
1788. WILLIAM WOODHALL was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 coat, value 3s., the goods of William Pascoa.
WILLIAM PASCOA. On the 31st of July I was carrying my coat about for sale, and a man, who I believe was the prisoner, took it from me, in Little Earl-street - he ran off with it, and I followed some distance, but did not give an alarm. I saw it again in the officer's possession some days afterwards - I believe he is the man.
JOHN TURNER. I took the prisoner to the watch-house for an assault, on the 31st of July - he had this coat partly on when I took him - I asked where he got it; he said it was stolen out of a drunken spree.
PHILIP RILEY. I was at the watch-house; the prisoner told me he had bought the coat for 1s. 6d.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270913-202]
1789. ANN MATTHEWS was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, 1 watch, value 35s., the goods of John Cody, from his person.
JOHN CODY. On the 4th of August I was a little tipsy, and went with the prisoner to a house - I had seen her before once or twice - I staid there about a quarter of an hour; I did not see her take the watch, but missed it as soon as I got away. I saw her again in two or three days afterwards, and asked her about it; she said it was pawned - I am not sure I did not give it her. She took me to where it was, and I promised to let her go if I got it, but the pawnbroker would not let us have it that night, and I was not certain of its being mine.
WILLIAM SHEPPARD. I took the prisoner into custody - she gave up the duplicate, and said the prosecutor had promised to let her go if she gave it up.
JOSEPH KING. I am a pawnbroker. This watch was pawned by me, but not by the prisoner.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-203]
1790. MARY ANN ROBINSON and ELLEN CONNOR were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, 1 watch, value 3l.; 1 chain, value 6d.; 2 seals, value 10s., and 1 key, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Gray, from the person of Sarah Brown, spinster .
SARAH BROWN. I am single. On the 27th of August, in the evening, I was at the Brown Bear public-house, Bow-street - Thos. Gray gave me a watch there to take care of for him, as he was not going home that evening; he remained there drinking till after it was lost; the prisoner Connor came in and cut my gown down, but I did not feel it, till I felt the knife sticking at my breast - I put up my hand and then missed the watch - I caught hold of her directly, and saw her pass it to Robinson.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What are you? A. A servant; I had been drinking wine - I cannot say how much; I was sick there - I had been to Kensington, and had the wine - I drank nothing at this house; I was not sober. Gray called for three half-pints of gin, which the prisoners and other people drank, but I drank none - Gray is not here.
ELIZA STEPHENS. I am cook at the Brown Bear - Brown came in with Gray, a baker, very tipsy indeed - she could hardly stand - Gray was drunk, and attempting to fight every body in the room - the prisoners werethere - Brown called for a glass of rum and water, and some gin; they both went up to the prisoners and interfered with them - Brown was very sick, and said she had got the man's watch; she wanted to go out, but my mistress said she should not go out with the watch - I heard her say, "Gray, I have lost your watch, I did not miss it till I found this knife here cutting my gown;" I saw that her gown was cut - an officer was fetched, who desired me to search the prisoners, but neither the knife nor watch was found.
Cross-examined. Q. Did Brown take gin with them? A. Yes; they all had gin together.
JOHN DOODY. I am a watchman - Brown called me into the house, and said she had lost a watch; I went in and took the prisoners to the watch-house.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-204]
1791. HENRY STEVENS was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of July, 1 watch, value 30s., the goods of Thomas Walter Pride, from his person.
THOMAS WALTER PRIDE. I am a tallow-chandler, and live at Paddington. On Monday, the 23d of July, about twenty-five minutes after ten o'clock at night, a person, whom I did not know, walked with me from the Hay-market to Crawford-street; I saw a person take my watch; the man immediately ran away; I called Stop thief! but he turned a corner, and I lost sight of him; the watchman brought the prisoner back soon after - I should not like to swear to the person, or the dress, of the man who robbed me, but he had something round him which appeared to me to be a black apron, but it proved to be a bag.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were you sober? A. Not so sober as I am now.
THOMAS CLARK. I am a watchman. I heard a cry of Stop thief; the prisoner was the first man who was running; I took him about two hundred yards from Crawford-street; others were running - I saw the prosecutor in a quarter of an hour - he was sitting down; he had been drinking, but was not to say drunk - the prisoner had something black round his waist.
JOHN BOYD. I am sergeant of the night; I came up when the prisoner was taken; he dropped some keys from his hat - I found no watch on him.
RICHARD HARTLY WALL. The prisoner was brought to the office; I saw something being poked through the wall; next morning I went in, and found this bag in his hat.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-205]
1792. ELIZABETH GRAHAM and SARAH SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of June, 60 penny-pieces, the monies of James McGoverin, from the person of Richard Braham .
RICHARD BRAHAM. I am servant to James McGoverin, of Buckeridge-street. On the 12th of June I was in Buckeridge-street, and had these penny-pieces in a basket; I put it on a post to rest; Graham came and took them out - there were four more girls with her - she was the first who came up; I did not see her take them, but I missed them in four minutes: a woman behind me said I was robbed by one of those girls; I laid down the basket to go after them, and saw Smith, who was one of the four, drop the 5s. paper of halfpence - I took it up - I lost two papers, but only found one.
MARY DONNELLY. On the evening of the 12th of July, I saw Braham resting his basket on a post, near the corner of Church-street, St. Giles' - I stood talking to him - these young women came up; Graham took the paper of halfpence out of the basket, and passed it to Smith; I told him of it, and helped him down with his basket, while he pursued them - I saw the paper brought back.
Prisoner SMITH. Q. Was there not a fight in the street? A. There was a crowd, but I saw no fight; Braham had 10l. worth of copper in his basket. I at first thought she took it in fun, till she gave it to Smith.
EDWARD HEDGES. I am a beadle. I was sent for, and took up the prisoners; I went up into their room, but had a great difficulty to take them.
GRAHAM's Defence. I stood by, but did nothing to the basket; I said, "That man has taken something;" this woman came, and said, "How dare you say so? it is more likely to be one of you."
SMITH's Defence. He struck Graham, and made her nose bleed; I took her up stairs to wipe her face, and the beadle came and took us.
RICHARD BRAHAM. I struck at her, as she appeared to be coming towards the basket again, but did not hit her.
GRAHAM - GUILTY. Aged 20.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-206]
1793. THOMAS JAMES and GEORGE BUTLER were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of August, 1 shirt, value 5s., and 1 pair of stockings, value 2s., the goods of Robert Potts; and 1 gown, value 10s., and 1 shift, value 3s., the goods of Charlotte Potts, from the person of William Ponsden .
SARAH PONSDEN. I live in China-mews. On the 18th of August, at nine o'clock at night, I gave my son William three bundles to deliver, one containing these articles, belonged to Mr. Potts, of Little Gower-place, whom I wash for; he was to take it there; my little girl, who went with him, came and said he was robbed.
WILLIAM PONSDEN. I am eleven years old. My mother gave me a bundle to take to Mr. Potts; I had delivered two other bundles, and had Mr. Potts' left; I was in Little Gower-street - the two prisoners came running along, and James snatched at my bundle; he could not get it, and snatched again - I hallooed Stop thief! he then made a third snatch, and got it. I had seen him before - he had followed me two or three times; they ran away together, and James gave the bundle to Butler - they ran down Little Gower-place, and tried to throw it over the pales, but it fell down, and I took it up; I carried it on to Mr. Potts' - some persons were pursuing the prisoners.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did not the watchman ask you if Butler took it? A. Yes; I said James took it, and gave it to him; James always had his hand in his pocket; I never observed that he had but one hand - he only used his left hand to take it, I am sure.
JOHN CAMPBELL. I am a street-keeper. I was in Gower-street; a boy said I was wanted; I went to Little Gower-place, and saw Butler in custody; the lad said a man had taken a bundle, and given it to Butler, who tried to throw it over the pales; and a gentleman said he saw him try to throw it over, and dropped it. I took him tothe watch-house - he said he received the bundle from a man unknown to him; and, on Monday morning, he said James was in his company - James was then taken. I had never seen them together.
Cross-examined. Q. He said this in James' absence? A. Yes.
WILLIAM JAMIESON. I apprehended James on the 20th of August - this lad identified him at Marlborough-street.
JAMES' Defence. I know nothing of it.
BUTLER's Defence. The bundle was thrown into my hands - I threw them away directly.
JAMES - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.
BUTLER - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Six Months.

1795. ANN BROOKER was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, 1 purse, value 6d.; 13 sovereigns, 17 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the property of William Barton, from his person.
WILLIAM BARTON. On the 27th of July I met the prisoner in the Haymarket, and went to a house in Union-court, Westminster; I went to bed with her - I had thirteen sovereigns, and 17s. 6d. in my purse; I put my trousers on a chair by the bed-side - I did not go to sleep - she complained of being thirsty; in about three-quarters of an hour she said she would get up, and go and have a pint of beer - she went away, but did not return. I got up and missed my trousers and money - I was sober; I saw her at Bow-street afterwards, and am certain of her person.
GEORGE BLACKMAN. I am a patrol. I apprehended the prisoner, in consequence of information, on the night of the 7th of August, at No. 2, Union-court, Orchard-street, Westminster - I had been looking for her several days, and she had been absent from her lodgings.
ELIZA BOND. I live at No. 16, Union-court - the prisoner lodged at No. 2. On the 26th of July, I had the care of her child, which is now in her arms; she left it with me on that day, and I did not see her afterwards - she used to call daily to see it before.
WILLIAM BARTON. I am sure I did not go to sleep - that nobody else came into the room, and that the money was safe when I undressed.
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw the man.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Of stealing, but not from the person.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-209]
1796. ANN MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, 4 half sovereigns, and one 20l. Bank note, the property of Francis Steward, from his person.
FRANCIS STEWARD. I am a sailor. On the 4th of August, about twelve o'clock at night, I was in Whitechapel, and had a 20l. note and four half-sovereigns in my breeches pocket; the prisoner spoke to me, and I went with her to a house, which I could not find afterwards. I had the money in my hand when I went to her room. I wrapped the sovereign in the notes while we sat there, and put them into my pocket; the prisoner took them out, and ran away directly. I met her in the street three or four days after - I was not above five minutes with her, but am certain of her.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What information did you give the officer respecting the street you was robbed in? A. I do not know the street; I never mentioned the name of it, or the number of the house - I never said it was No. 20, White Lion-street, or that it was at Mrs. Clark's. I met her again on the Wednesday following, in the same place - I did not give charge of her; and, on the Friday after, she was brought to me by a shipmate - and was secured.
COURT. Q. Why not take her on Wednesday? A. I was afraid she would run from me.
JOHN NORRIS. On Saturday, the 4th of August, the prosecutor came to me between five and six o'clock, and said he had been robbed; I went with him - he could not point out any house. On the Friday he sent for me, and said he had seen the woman, and his shipmates were going to bring her down; she was taken, and he swore positively to her - she was remanded twice, but he had given us information of quite another person altogether.
Cross-examined. Q. Has not the other person absconded? A. We have seen her since, but he swore positively to the prisoner.
JAMES HANLEY. I am an officer of Worship-street. The prosecutor told me he had been robbed at Mrs. Clark's, No. 20, White Lion-street - he took me there, and showed me the room.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-210]
1797. EDWARD PURSEY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, 1 watch, value 30s.; 2 seals, value 6s., and 1 key, value 1s., the goods of Thomas Larke, from his person.
THOMAS LARKE. I live in Union-place. On the 14th of July, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night, I was going home with Thomas Dean, my landlord - the prisoner ran up against me, and said, "I beg your pardon, Sir;" and as he said that, he drew my watch out of my pocket. I caught it with my left hand, and caught him with my right hand.
THOMAS DEAN. I was with Larke; his account is correct; I was about six feet before him; he called me; I turned, and saw the prisoner in his hands; he gave me the watch; I took it to the office - Larke was sober.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going along - this gentleman was in a mob, and seemed in liquor; he took me, and said I had robbed him; he took the watch from his pocket, and gave it to the other witness.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-211]
1798. JOHN SHEHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 1 watch, value 5l., the goods of James Donohoe, from his person.
JAMES DONOHOE. I am a labourer, and live in Buckeridge-street. On the 7th of August I was in the Hareand Hounds public-house, Buckeridge-street, for about two hours, in an upper room; I had known the prisoner some time - he sat by my side; I was quite sober, but having been up two nights, was rather sleepy; he said if I laid down there he would see me all right; I slept on the table for about three-quarters of an hour - when I awoke, I missed him and my watch; I had wound it up before I went to sleep.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. This was at a club, was it not? A. Yes; there were fifty or sixty people in the room; Sullivan was chairman - I did not find the prisoner in another room. I charged Kilronen with knowing who had stolen it, and charged him with stealing my tobacco-box - the prisoner came to me the same night, and asked if I had said he stole the watch, and I shook hands with him, and gave him some beer - he denied taking the watch - Kilronen gave me my tobacco-box. I did not tell the prisoner I did not suspect him.
JOHN BURK. I live at St. Giles. On the 7th of August, at night, in going to the Hare and Hounds for a pint of beer for my father, the prisoner spilt part of it in the house - I kept up to him, and asked why he did that - he did not answer me; I heard one Herring say to him, "Is it all right?" he said "It is;" I saw Shehan have a watch in his hand, the key and chain slipped between his fingers, as he put it into his breeches pocket - it was a silver watch, a brass chain, gold seals, and gold ring - he ran off as fast as he could.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you been told how many seals the watch had? A. I used to see it every day - my father told me it was silver, and a brass chain; it was just after eleven o'clock - it is a dark place, but there is a gaslight - I was never in trouble; Donohoe sells fruit, and my father sells fish.
COURT. Q. When did you mention this? A. Next morning, when I heard the prosecutor had lost his watch.
JAMES DONOHOE. I shook hands with the prisoner next day; I had heard what Burk had seen then.
MICHAEL KELLY. I am a watchman. On the 8th of August, at nine o'clock, I was going my rounds, and met the prosecutor, who said he believed the prisoner had took his watch, for he had been seen with it.
Cross-examined. Q. Was that the following day? A. Yes - he said he had been talking to the prisoner, and he had offered to go before a Magistrate. I went and found the prisoner in bed.
Prisoner's Defence. I was waiting at the door to see my wife, to get 6d., to go into the club-room; I went in, borrowed 6d., and the prosecutor called me to him; they were pushing one another about; Sullivan left the room with me about eleven o'clock - the prosecutor was then accusing Kilronen of taking his tobacco-box and watch; I went home, and next night heard I was accused; I went and drank with him.
WILLIAM SULLIVAN. I was chairman at this free-and-easy club - I left the chair about a quarter to eleven o'clock, and went into the parlour; Shehan and two others went with me; he was in the room till I left the chair, I am certain; while we were drinking in the parlour the prosecutor came into the parlour, walked out, returned, and said he had lost his watch; the prisoner had not been out of my company: we went out together, and saw him at the bar, accusing Barney Kilronen of robbing him of his watch.
COURT. Q. Was no search proposed? A. None; Shehan sat near him. Kilronen was taken to the watch-house, and to the office next day.
THOMAS KENNEDY. I know this public-house - it is a dark dull gateway; there is no gas-light there; Burk could not see what he has described - it is the darkest place in the parish; it is called Rats' Castle - I am not one of the club.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-212]
1799. MICHAEL COLLINS, WILLIAM MURPHY, and DENNIS MURPHY, were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, 164lbs. weight of lead, value 16s., the goods of our Lord the King .
SECOND COUNT, calling it the goods of Benjamin Charles Stevenson.
THIRD COUNT, stating it to be the goods of the Right Honorable William Sturges Bourne, William Dakers Adams, Esq., and Henry Dawkins, Esq.
MR. BOLLAND conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM OXLEY. I am a constable of St. Martin in the Fields. On the morning of the 27th of July, in consequence of information, I went to Scotland-yard, at five o'clock, with Lemon - I saw the three prisoners digging there; no other person was near them - I waited about three quarters of an hour, and saw them take a large piece of pipe out of the earth - open the trap-door of a dung hole, and put it in there; they then took two or three shorter pieces, and put them there - they worked on till eight o'clock. I then saw Collins and William Murphy take it up, chop it into pieces, and put it into a barrow, which Collins wheeled down Parliament-street; William Murphy ran and joined him in Parliament-street (Dennis Murphy remained at work). I followed them to Schofield's, in Dean's-yard - I saw them take the lead out and carry it into the shop; I immediately went in, seized William Murphy, and told Schofield it was stolen goods; here is the lead, it is pieces of large pipes.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. William Murphy and Collins took it away? A. Yes; but Dennis came to the very spot where they were taken, in about ten minutes - eight o'clock is the time they leave work; they were working at the office of the Board of Works, Scotland-Yard.
WILLIAM LEMON. I am an officer. Oxley's account is correct; I saw them take the lead and put it into the dunghole - I followed William Murphy and Collins to Schofield's; Dennis came there afterwards and was taken.
WILLIAM SCHOFIELD. I am a plumber and live in Dean's-yard. On the night of the 26th of July, William Murphy came to my house, and asked if I would buy any lead; he said there was some which laid near the surface of the ground, and the people had given it them; I said he might let me see it; next morning William Murphy brought it into my shop - I did not notice Collins near.
EDWARD CROCKER. I am a labourer in trust, under the Board of Works. I received information, went to this spot, and missed this lead - it is an old service pipe, which came from the mews, and served the reservoir; it is the property of the Crown - I have not compared it, but it ismissing from the place, and this appears to be the same; I suppose it has been down two hundred years, it is worth about 16s.; it is remarkably thick.
COLLINS - GUILTY. Aged 23.
WILLIAM MURPHY - GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
DENNIS MURPHY - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-213]
1800. JOHN CRAWLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of August, 12lbs. of lamb, value 6s., the goods of William Stayner.
WILLIAM STAYNER. I am a butcher and live in Whitecross-street. On the 11th of August, about half-past nine o'clock at night, I missed a quarter of lamb.
CHARLES COLE. I am eleven years old. I saw the prisoner take the lamb off the hook; it hung in the street - I am certain he is the man; I told master, who went after him, but the lamb was not found - he was taken in about ten minutes; I knew him before - he appeared rather tipsy.
THOMAS HARRISON. I met the prisoner that night - he was very drunk.

1801. GEORGE CHURCH and THOMAS ENDERSBY were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 2 pairs of shoes, value 9s., the goods of Samuel Bugler.
CHURCH pleaded GUILTY. Aged 13.
SAMUEL BUGLER. I live in Shoreditch, and am a shoe-maker. On the 1st of September an officer produced two pairs of new shoes to me, which were mine, and have my initials on them; they were in the interior of my shop.
THOMAS PETO. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Hoxton. On the 30th of August, about one o'clock, Endersby brought this pair of shoes to me - Church was with him. I asked who they belonged to, he said to his father - I asked him for a better account; he said he would fetch his sister, who could tell me more about them - they then both ran away, leaving them with me.
HENRY FREAKS. I am a pawnbroker. Endersby pawned a pair of child's shoes with me, on the 29th of August, for 9d.
JAMES FOWLER. I apprehended the prisoners in consequence of information. I had seen them together - I asked where they got the shoes; Endersby said they were his father's.
ENDERSBY's Defence. I met Church in William-street; he asked me to pawn a pair of shoes for his aunt, and said he would give me half the money; he told me to say they were his father's, and that he lived in Willow-walk.
ENDERSBY - GUILTY. Aged 13.
Both Transported for Seven Years.

1802. JOHN DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of August, 1 pair of trousers, value 14s., the goods of David Farrow.
JOHN BROOKS. I am shopman to David Farrow, a salesman, of High Holborn. On the 1st of September the prisoner came, and my boy showed him some things, and called me into the shop; another person came in, and then a third; two of them went into the counting-house, and tried on several pairs of trousers - I had my suspicions. I went and saw the prisoner putting a pair into his hat - the others then went away. I attended to the prisoner - he was there about half an hour; he tried on a pair of drab trousers, and said they would not fit him - he then put on his own. I understood he was going. I walked to the door before him, and accused him of it - he said he had nothing; he took off his hat, and there was nothing in it. I detained him; he unbuttoned his own trousers, and produced this pair from the seat of them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MICHAEL SHINE. I am a watchman. I was called in, and saw the trousers found in those the prisoner had on.
The prisoner pleaded distress. GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.

1803. WILLIAM FULLER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, 34 yards of printed cotton, value 20s., the goods of William Neville.
ROBERT BYERS. I am servant to William Neville, who lives in Mile-end-road. This is his cotton. I was out when it was taken - it was safe two hours before.
JOHN WALLIS. I am a groom. On the 20th of July I was sitting in a chaise, nearly opposite Mr. Neville's. I saw the prisoner and another take this print from the shop. I jumped out and secured the prisoner - he scuffled with me, and dropped it - the other got away - Wheatley picked it up.
PETER WHEATLEY. I picked up the print, and saw the prisoner scuffling with Wallis.
JAMES LEA. I took him in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-217]
1804. FREDERICK GLOVER was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of September, 16lbs. of lead, value 20s., the goods of Richard Smart, and fixed to a certain building of his; and a brass cock, value 2s., his property.
RICHARD SMART. I have a stable in Barnsbury-mews, lslington. On the 5th of September, I went there, and missed this pipe and cock, which served the stable with water.
JOHN ELEMENT. I am a watchman of lslington. I was in Barnsbury mews. Lane called me, and said the prisoner had got a parcel of lead - I took him to the watch-house with it.
SAMUEL LANE. I stopped the prisoner on the 5th of September, with a basket of lead in his hand - he put it down by his side - another person was with him; the prisoner denied having it, but I saw it in his hand - he was within six yards of the stable.
THOMAS GRAFTON. I am constable of the night; here is the basket and lead. I compared it with what is left in the stable - it matches exactly.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going to see a young man. I had not touched the basket.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.

1806. WILLIAM JESSOP was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of September, 1 truck, value 30s., and 18 sacks, value 30s., the goods of James Holland.
DAVID PHILLIPS. I am a watchman. On the afternoon of the 5th of September, I was at my door in Tufton-street. and saw the prisoner come round with the truck, and seventeen sacks, as he said; he offered them for sale for 5s. I told him, if he would go with me, I would take him to a person who would deal with him - I took him to the officer.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I am an officer. Phillips brought the prisoner to me, with the truck and sacks.
THOMAS GILLEGAN. I am in the employ of James Holland. On the 5th of September I had been out, and collected these sacks. I left them at Pimlico for about twenty-five minutes, and they were gone.
JAMES HOLLAND. I am a salesman at Covent-garden-market. I sent Gillegan to collect these sacks.
Prisoner's Defence. I had some beer at the King's Head, Public-house; some girls got on the truck, and I wheeled it down. I did not intend to sell the sacks - I meant to wheel them back.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-220]
1807. MARY HODGE was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of July, 7 gowns, value 30s.; 3 frocks, value 9s.; 5 caps, value 5s.; 1 shirt, value 3s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 1s.; 3 pinafores, value 2s.; 4 aprons, value 3s., and 2 shifts, value 1s., the goods of Patrick Hughes.
MARY HUGHES. I am the wife of Patrick Hughes; we live in Union-place, Chelsea. On the 15th of July these articles were in our back parlour and front kitchen. I went out that evening at eight o'clock; I returned at twelve, and the watchman gave me information. I knew the prisoner, but had not seen her for four years.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MARY HUGHES. I am the prosecutrix's daughter. The prisoner came to our house that evening, and sent me out three times for a young woman whom she knew. I did not go far the first time, but came back and said I did not see her. I went as far as the Star and Garter public-house the second time; and the third time, as I returned, I met the prisoner with a bundle in my mother's red handkerchief - this was a little after eight o'clock; she asked if I had seen the woman - I said Yes. I went home and missed the things, which were safe when I went out.
JOHN CANNEY. On the 15th of July, I was in the back room of this house where I lodge; the prisoner came in and told a young man and I, who were writing a letter, to come into the front room, as we should be quiet - we did so, and in a few minutes we came into the front room. I saw her tying up a bundle - I knew nothing of her before; she brought the bundle, and put something on my box. I went up stairs, and as I came down, saw her going out - I followed and enquired for her.
JANE ROBERTS. I am a publican. On the evening of Sunday, the 15th of July, the prisoner came and asked me to let her leave this bundle in my bar, which I did. I gave it to Allen.
WILLIAM ALLEN. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner. I received the bundle.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I never saw the bundle.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-221]
1808. JAMES LEONY was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of August, 1 looking-glass, value 40s., the goods of James Criswick.
JAMES CRISWICK. I am a carver and gilder, and live in Greek-street, Soho. On the 8th of August I went out, and as I returned, I saw the prisoner coming out of my shop with this glass - he dropped it at the door.
WILLIAM CRAIG. I am an officer. I heard a cry of Stop thief! and secured the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
EDWARD HUDSON. I was with my master, and saw the prisoner come out with the glass. I am certain he is the man - I followed him.
Prisoner's Defence. He met me and charged me with it, but I never had it. GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-222]
1809. JAMES MATON, WILLIAM HUNT, THOMAS McCOY and THOMAS TOMLINSON were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of July, 2 silver-spoons, value 40s., the goods of Fanny Sanson.
THOMAS KENWARD. I am servant to Mrs. Fanny Sanson, who lives at Hampstead. On the 9th of July, about half-past five o'clock in the afternoon, I saw Hunt in front of my mistress' house; another lad was with him - I asked what he wanted; he said, a drink of water; they went off directly, and did not shut the gate after them; they went down the road; I suspected something wrong - I went into the dining-room, where the cloth was laid, and from the corner of the table missed two spoons; the window was open; they could get in from the front of the house - I pursued immediately, and saw four boys together in a field - I caught Hunt, McCoy, and Maton - I found the spoons on Maton.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Does not Mr. Philip Sanson live in the house? A. Yes; he is Mrs. Sanson's son; it is her house.
JOHN ADAMS. I am a constable. I received Hunt, Maton and McCoy in charge, with the spoons.
JOHN BUSTARD. I apprehended Tomlinson the following day, from the information of one of the prisoners. I told him it was for a robbery at West End; he did not deny it - I took him at his mother's in Somers'-town - I said, "You were with the boys who were taken up at Mr. Sanson's;" he said he was.
HUNT'S Defence. I took the spoons - I know nothing of the other boys.
MATON - GUILTY. Aged 15.
McCOY - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Confined Fourteen Days.
HUNT - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Judgment Respited .
TOMLINSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-223]
1810. ROBERT JACOBS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 15lbs. of horse-hair, value 8s., and a bed-tick, value 2s., the goods of John Henderson.
JOHN HENDERSON. I am a seaman. I had some horse-hair and a bed-tick, on the 25th of August, at my house in Devonshire-street, Mile-end. The prisoner came to the house to purchase the hair; he rolled it up, took it, and walked off with it; he left a pair of steel-yards and abag behind him - I went out, but could not see him - I am sure he is the man - I have not found it.
Prisoner. His wife called me in, and asked if I bought horse-hair. Witness. She did no such thing - I was present.
Q. I said, "You do not dispute my word, I will return bye and bye?" A. You did not.
ANN FRANKS. I live next door to Henderson. The prisoner goes about buying old rags and iron; he came and asked if they had any thing to sell; they said some horse-hair; he took it out of the old tick, and put it into the new one; he weighed it, and called me in; he then took it on his shoulder, and went away with it; we thought he was going to put it into his bag, but he was gone.
JOHN NORRIS. I am an officer. I received information, and went and took the prisoner next morning, at his house, behind the hospital in Whitechapel.
Prisoner's Defence. I left my steel-yards and bag there; they said I might take it, and return again - I could not sell it that night; it weighed 15lbs. I said I was short of money, and could not pay then.
JOHN NORRIS. He did not say this to me.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-224]
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1811. ELIZABETH BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of August, 1 coat, value 40s., the goods of James Price.
JAMES PRICE. I am guard of the Birmingham coach. On the 12th of August, about half-past five o'clock, I was at the Green Dragon public-house; my coat was on the seat in the house - I was told it was stolen, and found it in possession of the prisoner.
MARY TOWNSEND. I was at the Green Dragon. I saw the prisoner come in to sell nuts - I saw her take the coat off the seat - Price was at tea in the bar - I told my husband, who fetched her back with it.
GUILTY. Aged 66.
Confined Eighteen Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-225]
1812. JOSEPH SMALLWOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of August, 1 half-crown, and 2 halfpence, the monies of Elizabeth Oakley, widow.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
ELIZABETH OAKLEY. I am a widow, and keep a small shop, in Haberdashers'-place, Hoxton, I let a stable, behind my house, to William Shackell; the prisoner was in his employ, and knew my house and family. On the 10th of August, between ten and eleven o'clock at night I marked a half-crown, a shilling, and a sixpence, which I put into the silver division of my till; no other silver was there - I had closed my shop, and finished business for the night; my niece marked some copper. I got up about half-past five o'clock, and at a quarter to six, I saw the prisoner in my shop, which was not then opened for business; no customer had been; my niece and I then went into the parlour, shut the door, and put up a thick curtain, so that we could not be seen; we turned it aside, and had a fullview of the till and desk; the prisoner came to the back door, when the servant had taken down seven shutters; as soon as he set his foot in, he called "Mrs. Oakley!" pretty loud- I made no reply; he instantly advanced into the shop, and went on stooping, with his head below the counter, to the till, and drew it out, then raised his head, and looked into it, put his hand in, and took something out - he then took something out of the copper drawer; he went to the desk and tried that, but it was locked; he returned in the same position, went to the back door, ran up the first pair of stairs, stood there a few seconds, and returned, went out, shut the door, and ran into the stable - I had seen the money safe in the till just before - I examined it again directly, and the half-crown was gone - I sent for an officer, and he was taken - I went to the watch-house, and saw him struggling with the constable; he declared he had not set his foot in the house that morning; the officer would search him, and found the marked half-crown, which I knew directly; he threw some copper on the table in the scuffle.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. Is your son in partnership with you? A. Yes; I keep the house; this was housekeeping money - it did not belong to the trade - I had taken it from the till before - I have a certain sum for housekeeping; my son lives in the house; if I do not lay it all out, I have the rest.
COURT. Q. Did you take the money out of the till to mark it? A. No, from my pocket - it was my own, for housekeeping.
ELIZABETH FOSTER. I am the prosecutrix's niece. I marked some copper with a cross, and put it into the till; I saw the prisoner come into the shop about a quarter to six o'clock in the morning; I had heard him in the stable - we placed ourselves so that we could see - he came in and stooped round the counter, drew out the till, and took something from it - I saw him take 21/2d. from his pocket at the watch-house, and two of the halfpence were what I had marked.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see the half-crown marked? A. Yes; I cannot say whether my aunt took it from her pocket or the till.
RICHARD CONSTANSTINE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner into custody at the stable; I told him the charge at the watch-house; he denied it. Mrs. Oakley came, and said, "How can you deny it, I saw you do it, and know you have got the money." I found a marked half-crown and 7s. in his fob; she claimed the half-crown; some halfpence were thrown on the table, which I put into my pocket, but my wife got them and spent them; here is the half-crown; Foster claimed two of the halfpence.
MRS. OAKLEY. This is the half-crown; here are two marks on each side the crown.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-226]
1813. WILLIAM VIDLER PRITCHARD was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 1 square, value 2s. 5d., the goods of William Smith and Josiah Smith.
JOSEPH TILEY. I am apprentice to William and Josiah Smith, cabinet-makers, of Charter-house-lane. On the 14th of February I went to work in the shop, and brought my square with me; I lent it to Foster, and never saw it again, till I found it in pawn on the 10th of August; the prisoner worked for my masters, but not on the premises.
GEORGE FOSTER. I was an apprentice to the prosecutors. I borrowed the square and left in on the bench; he inquired for it a few days after and it was gone.
THOMAS POCOCK. I am a pawnbroker. On the 22d of February this square was pawned by the prisoner; I knew him before.
WILLIAM KIRBY. I am an officer, and took him into custody.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner Defence. The pawnbroker cannot recollect me. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-227]
1814. JOHN HITCHCOCK, CHARLES HAWKINS, and JAMES PAGET, were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of July, at St. Leonard, Bromley, 1 gelding, price 21l., the property of Joseph Kain.
MR. JOSEPH KAIN. I am a shipowner, and live at St. Leonard's, Bromley, in Middlesex. On the morning of Tuesday, the 12th of July, I lost a gelding out of a field, at the back of my house - I had seen it secure about ten o'clock the night before; the gate was then locked. On Tuesday morning my gardener came and told me the gate was broken open, and the gelding gone - I had handbills printed, and advertised it in the Times Newspaper - and on the Sunday evening, three Bow-street patrols came to me - I know the gelding to be mine; I know nothing of the prisoners.
FREDERICK GROSSMITH. I am a Bow-street officer. About half-past two o'clock in the morning of the 12th of July, I was with McWilliams and Tyrrell, in Church-row, Bethnal-green; it was just then getting light. I saw Hawkins riding on a gelding, at the corner of Busby-street, which leads out of Church-row - I suspected all was not right, and ran after him; he was only walking - I pursued him up Busby-street, and when I came up with him, I asked him whose horse that was; he hesitated a minute or two, and then said, he was a drover, and was coming from Romford, that about a quarter of a mile below Bow-bridge, a man in a fustian coat hired him to ride the horse to Church-street, Bethnal-green, and he was to have 1s. - but he was then riding away from Church-street - Tyrrell had got up by this time; he took Hawkins; I lead the horse to the corner of Brick-lane; McWilliams went to the farther end of Church-street, near Shoreditch, where Hawkins said the man was to wait for him - he returned in about ten minutes, and said there was no one waiting; that nobody was there but the watchman - we then took Hawkins to Bethnal-green watch-house. Tyrrell and I took the horse away, and at the corner of Hare-street, Brick-lane, we saw Hitchcock coming along - this was about six hundred yards from where we met Hawkins; we took him, and Tyrrell found a quantity of oats in his pocket; he was walking toward Church-street - we took him to the watch-house - we asked him where he had been - he said in the brick fields all night; we said nothing to him before we searched him. We asked Hawkins, in his presence, if he was the man who gave him the horse; he said No; we locked them up. I shewed the same horse to Mr. Kain, who claimed it.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I am an officer. On the 12th of July I saw Hawkins go down Busby-street, with the horse, as my brother officer has stated; we saw Hitchcock; he was dressed as Hawkins had described - I stopped him and found some oats in his pocket. I then took off his hat, and found in the lining of it, the remains of some oats - I asked Hawkins if he was the man; he said No.
JOHN McWILLIAMS. I am an officer. I was with the other officers - I left them at the top of Brick-lane, and went to the end of Church-street, where Hawkins said he was to meet the man. I waited there ten minutes or more, and could see no man; I enquired of the watchman; then came back and told them nobody had been seen there.
JOSEPH ADAMS. I am a street-keeper. I received information from Worship-street, that Paget' was wanted - I met him on the 28th of July, took him, and told him I wanted him for horse-stealing; he said, rather than go to the watch-house, he would go to Garton, as he knew it was all right - we went part of the way and he ran off, down an entry; I called out, and he was stopped at the end of Longalley - I took him to Garton, who said he was the person wanted.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS. I am a watchman. At a quarter to ten o'clock on the 28th of July, I heard Adams calling Stop him! I stopped Paget in Meeting-house-court, Longalley; he said "Let me go, it is the street-keeper after me."
JOSHUA ARMSTRONG. I am an officer. After Hawkins was taken, I went to Paget's house but could not find him at home.
THOMAS GARTON. I was with Armstrong; in consequence of Hawkins' confession before the Magistrate, I went to take Paget.
THOMAS WATERS. I am an officer of Worship-street. I went into the lock-up room, and Hawkins said it was very hard he should suffer as an innocent man, and wanted to tell me something - I said, "Don't say any thing to me; if you have anything to say, I will take you in to the Magistrate;" what he said was taken down - I made him no promise or threat.
RICHARD SKILLERN. I am a constable of Bow-street. Hitchcock was apprehended on the 12th, and discharged; after that I heard he was wanted - I went and took him again on the 13th.
WILLIAM KAIN. Grossmith shewed me the horse; it was mine - it is worth twenty guineas.
JOSHUA ARMSTRONG. (Looking at the examinations returned by the Magistrate.) This is signed by Mr. Twyford; and I saw Hawkins make his mark to it (read.)
"John Hitchcock came to me yesterday, at one o'clock, and told me Mr. Paget wanted to speak to me about a horse, it was a job to fetch a horse. I went, and Hitchcock and Roberts were there; they asked if I would go to fetch the horse - we went to the bridge at Mile-end, and Hitchcock told me to wait there while he went into a lane; when he returned, he led a chesnut-coloured horse, and told me to ride on to Church-street, Bethnal-green, and when I got to Church-street I was stopped - Hitchcock told me it was a Justice of the Peace's horse. CHARLES HAWKINS X his mark."
RICHARD SKILLERN. The other prisoners were not present when the statement was made - they were not in custody.
HAWKINS - GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 24.
Recommended to mercy by the Prosecutor, believing others were concerned with him.
HITCHCOCK - NOT GUILTY.
PAGET - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-228]
1815. WILLIAM ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of August, 1 desk, value 30s., the goods of Adam Murgatroyd.
ADAM MURGATROYD. I live at Knightsbridge, and have a house at Pimlico; the prisoner lodged in that house. I believe he had the first floor; I let the whole upper part to Mr. Hill. On the 13th of August my young man sent for me - I went there, and missed this desk from my shop.
JAMES THOMAS LENNY. I am shopman to Mr. Murgatroyd. I saw the desk safe in his shop, at Pimlico; the prisoner could get to the shop from the private door - I saw it safe on Saturday night, the 11th of August, about nine o'clock, and missed it on Monday afternoon; I found it in pawn that evening.
Prisoner. Q. What time was the shop open? A. About half-past seven o'clock. I suspected you, because you offered me some duplicates the day before, and said you were in distress - I know it by a private mark; I saw you leave the house that evening, and followed you to No. 5, Charlotte-street; you were taken next morning.
EDWARD DAVIS. I am shopman to Mr. Rochford, a pawnbroker, of Jermyn-street. I produce a desk, which was pawned by the prisoner, in the name of Jones, on the 13th of August; I am certain he is the person.
JOSEPH COOPER. I am an officer, and took the prisoner on the 14th of August. I was searching for the duplicate, he said, I had no right to search his things - I asked when he was last in Jermyn-street; he said on Saturday - he said it was useless to deny it any longer - that he had done it, and was sorry for it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I admit pawning it. I met an acquaintance named Jones, who owed me 7s., which I asked him for - he said he was short of money, and wanted to pawn the desk, which he had got, but felt tenacious of going to a pawnbroker - I pawned it for him and gave him the money; he paid me 5s., I allowed him 2s. for the duplicate.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-229]
1816. JAMES SHELDON was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Henry Petus.
HENRY PETUS. I am barman to Mr. Pool, of the Princess Royal, public-house; my handkerchief was taken off the seat on the skittle-ground - the prisoner was playing there with others; it was safe about four o'clock. I might have dropped it.
WILLIAM WINTERBOTHAM. I live with Mr. Walker, pawnbroker, of Tabernacle-walk; the prisoner pawned this handkerchief on the 28th of August, between eight and nine o'clock.
SAMUEL TAYLOR. I am an officer, and apprehended the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I found it on the ground.
NOT GUILTY.

1817. EDWARD SAMPSON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of August, 1 watch, value 35s.; 1 chain, value 1s.; 2 seals, value 14s., and 1 key, value 6d., the goods of William Prince.
WILLIAM PRINCE. I am a labourer, and lodge in Golden-lane - the prisoner visited a relation there. On the 1st of August I missed my watch from a drawer in my mother's room, on the second floor. I informed an officer, and we took the prisoner in the Strand that night - he denied having any of my property; it was found in his trousers pocket.
WILLIAM EDWARDS. I found it on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that he had called on the prosecutor's mother, and was looking at the watch, when she suddenly came into the room, and he put it into his pocket, not wishing her to see that he had it, but did not intend to steal it.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Fourteen Days.

THOMAS JONES. I work for Mrs. Norton, a farmer, and so does the prisoner - I felt in his jacket pocket, and found ninety-seven horse nails. I never put nails into my pocket- I sent him out, and took the jacket to the prosecutrix - I cannot swear to the nails.

1819. THOMAS WALKER was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 3 handkerchiefs, value 2s., the goods of Joseph Thimbleby.

Prisoner. I picked them up close to the door - I had eat nothing for four days.

[trialAccount t18270913-233]
1820. WILLIAM WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 2 counterpanes, value 8s.; 1 telescope, value 3s.; 1 flute, value 4s.; 2 coats, value 12s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 6s.; 2 shirts, value 4s.; 6 pairs of stockings, value 4s.; 1 pair of breeches, value 2s.; 1 set of bed-furniture, value 12s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s., and 2 waistcoats, value 2s., the goods of Henry Daniels.
FRANCIS HARRIS. I am a porter; I live in Southampton-row, Camden-town, in the house of Henry Daniels. On the 7th of August, about three o'clock in the afternoon, I was coming home, and saw a man at the door; I went up, and asked what he wanted - he said Daniels; I said he was not at home; I then went in, and saw the prisoner in the passage - the other man called to him, "There is none of them at home." I went up stairs, and found the doors were open - I came out to look for Daniels, but could not find him. I returned, and found the prisoner and the other man in Daniels' room, packing the things up - I forced into the room, and took the prisoner; he struggled, and got from me - the other man ran out; Peckard caught the prisoner - I am sure he is the man - they were putting the property into bags.
WILLIAM PECKARD. I live in the neighbourhood. I heard an alarm given by Harris, and caught the prisoner, who was running without a hat; I brought him back, and asked where his hat was; he said he had left it in the room - it was found there, and he owned it.
JOSEPH SAUNDERS. I am a patrol, and received him in charge.
HENRY DANIELS. This is my property, I had left it in the drawers.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.

1822. CATHERINE CORNELL was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of July, 1 watch, value 15s.; 3 spoons, value 5s., 1 watch, value 18s.; 2 table cloths, value 1s. 6d.; 2 pillow-cases, value 6d., and 3 sheets, value 9s., the goods of Thomas Kirkwood, her master.
THOMAS KIRKWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Brick-lane, Old-street; the prisoner was my servant. I had suspicions, and on the 24th of July I missed a watch and some sheets; I sent for an officer, who found the watch and a pair of salt-spoons on her - I had a good character with her.
CHARLES WORLEY. I am shopman to Mr. Castles, pawnbroker, Old-street. I have a watch and sheet which were pawned by the prisoner in June.
EDMUND PEELE. I am a headborough, and live in Brick-lane, Old-street. I was sent for, and found a watch, two salt-spoons, and some duplicates, on the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
