Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18270913-236]
1823. ANDREW SULLIVAN and JOHN KELLY were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 2lbs. 10ozs. weight of chrome yellow, value 6s., the goods of George Deakin Midgley, their master.
GEORGE DEAKEN MIDGLEY. I am a chemist, and live in the Strand; I am manufacturer of an article called chrome yellow; the prisoners were my labourers - the officer informed me he had taken Sullivan, and wanted Kelly. I directed him to be taken; I went to Marlborough-street, where I saw 2lbs. 10ozs. of chrome yellow. I missed some, but could not be certain how much - other people also make it.
WILLIAM NELSON. I am an oil and colourman, of Broad-street, Golden-square. Last Tuesday night, at a quarter to ten o'clock, Sullivan came to my shop, and asked if I would purchase some chrome yellow; I asked what quantity; he said he had 2lbs. handy, and could have more; I asked what he wanted; he said 1s. I asked to see it; he ran across the street to the other person, I think, who gave him something in a handkerchief - and when he returned, as he only asked a quarter of the usual price, I detained him, and sent for a watchman; he ran out- I followed him, brought him back, and gave him in charge; he said he was the manufacturer, and lived near Vauxhall-bridge - he afterwards said in Milbank-street, and next day said he took it from the prosecutor.
RICHARD MASON. I am a watchman. I was calling ten o'clock; Nelson sent for me; he delivered Sullivan to me- I saw this stuff in the shop; I did not see Kelly.
MR. MIDGLEY. This is my property; it is a peculiar colour, which I could not produce till within the last three weeks; it is worth 6s.
SULLIVAN'S Defence. I picked it up; I did not know the value.
KELLY'S Defence. I know nothing about it. I only held it for him while he went into the shop.
SULLIVAN - GUILTY. Aged 26.
KELLY - GUILTY. Aged 34.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-237]
1824. CHARLES BREWER was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 truck, value 30s., the goods of Richard Mason.
RICHARD MASON. I live in Grey Eagle-street, and sell ginger-beer, and employed the prisoner on the 14th of August to carry it out on a truck - he never returned; he was taken at Bartholomew-fair - I found it in Hobbins' possession.
WILLIAM HOBBINS. On the 15th of August I was at Mitcham-fair, and saw this truck for sale. I bought it of the prisoner for 13s.; he said he was the owner. Mason claimed it.
THEOPHILUS WHITING. I live in Three Swan-yard. I saw the prisoner go out with the truck on the 14th of August.
FRANCIS GITTINS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner at Bartholomew-fair.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-238]
1825. JOHN CARNEY was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of August, 2 shoes, value 10s., the goods of George Evans.
SARAH EVANS. I am the wife of George Evans, a shoe-maker; we live in Whitechapel-road; the prisoner came in on the 2d of August, with another young man - the other purchased a pair of shoes, and while he was paying me for them, I saw the prisoner take one shoe off the counter, and conceal it under his apron - they went out, I followed, and collared the prisoner; he said he had nothing - I found a pair of shoes under his apron.
THOMAS SMITH. I am a constable, and took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-239]
1826. ELIZABETH COOK was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 1 pewter pot, value 1s., the goods of Robert Parker; and 1 pewter pot, value 1s., the goods of John Smith.
ROBERT PARKER. I keep the King's Head public-house, Edgware-road . On the 17th of July I lost this pot- I went out, and took the prisoner one hundred yards off, with a bundle, in which was three pots, and he had mine in his hand.
JOHN SMITH. I am a publican; one of these pots are mine.
JAMES GIBBS. I am an officer, and took her in charge.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-240]
1827. JANE CHAPMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 2 sheets, value 6s., the goods of Timothy Nolan.
CATHERINE NOLAN. I am the wife of Timothy Nolan; we live in Charles-street, Drury-lane. I let the prisoner my second floor room - on the 20th of August, when I asked her for rent, she said I should have it in the afternoon, but she left, and I missed the sheets. I met her in Long-acre, and she gave me the duplicate of one.
JULIUS HOCKLEY. I am servant to Mr. Newby, pawnbroker, of Drury-lane. On the 17th of August a sheet was pawned in the name of Ann Chapman. I cannot swear to the prisoner, but this is the duplicate I gave her.
WILLIAM KING. I am an officer. I received her in charge on the 27th of August, and on the way to the office she acknowledged taking the sheets.(Sheets produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A woman who took the room with me, took the sheets, and gave me the duplicate; I intended to redeem it.
GUILTY. Aged 52.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-241]
FIFTH DAY. - TUESDAY, SEPTEMBER 18.
Third Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1828. PETER DUBEY was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 1 shirt, value 2s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 4s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s., and 1 tin-box, value 1d., the goods of Henry Rose.
GEORGE DEVEREL. I am an officer. I found this tin-box in the prisoner's jacket pocket at the office, on the 31st of August; Rose was there, and said he had been robbed of these articles and the tin-box - the prisoner said it was his own property; he had been brought on the 30th of August to the watch-house, on suspicion of stealing some other things; he said he had taken them from the house where his brother was lodging, to take care of them.
HENRY ROSE. I lost my shirt, trousers, handkerchief, and snuff-box, on the 30th of August - the prisoner has my handkerchief on his neck now - I came that day from the India-house to Mr. Cole's. I saw the prisoner there, and he said, "I will cook for you, if you will give me a mouthful of rice;" I gave him a half-crown to go and get some beer; he went away, and did not return with it - he afterwards came and took my clothes away. I was a little tipsy, and went after him, but he got away - this was on Thursday night.
COURT. Q. Did you tell any body of it that night? A. No; I was not so drunk as not to know what was going on. I saw him take the articles; I know this tin-box to be mine; I saw him take it along with the other things.
GEO. DEVEREL re-examined. Q. When did you take this box from the prisoner? A. On the 31st of August; Rose came the next morning with another person, on another charge. I found on him a bag, which belongs to the other prosecutor and this box in it.
Prisoner. The things I took I delivered to the officer. I did not steal them; Stevens gave me them to take care of.
THOMAS STEVENS. Rose is a Lascar. There is a barrack for Lascars in Shadwell, and he was there; I saw the shirt and trousers, and other things belonging to Rose- the prisoner is a stranger. I never told him to take any of Rose's things, or to take care of them.
JURY to HENRY ROSE. Q. How do you know this box? A. It has been black, and the black is rubbed off - that is my handkerchief round his neck; it is quite plain, and has no border; it is three-cornered shape.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-242]
1829. JOHN ARNOLD was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 8 pieces of sole leather, value 4s., the goods of William Bailey.
WILLIAM BAILEY, JUN. I am the son of William Bailey, leather-seller, of Dean-street, Soho. On the 30th of August the prisoner came in as a customer - I saw him take four pieces of leather out of a binn and conceal them in his bosom, and some more in his pocket. I took it from him, and gave him charge.
WILLIAM MORRIS. I am an officer, and took him into custody.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going to the counting-house, to ask if they would give me credit for it; it was under my arm, not in my bosom.
GUILTY. Aged 63.
Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-243]
1830. THOMAS EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, 7 spoons, value 2l., the goods of William Hobson.
SUSAN KEATES PARFIT. I am servant to Mr. Wm. Hobson, who lives in Windsor-place, City-road. On the 14th of July the prisoner came into the kitchen; he went out in about eight minutes, and I immediately missed seven spoons, which were safe about five minutes before. I had been out of the kitchen while he was there - I knew him before; master had employed him to scour some empty houses down; we could not find him till the Monday following, when he was taken.
HENRY CHERRY. I am an errand-boy to Mr. Hobson. I heard of this loss, and met the prisoner on the Thursday following. I asked what he had done with the spoons; he said they were sold and melted down, and they could not have them any more. I said Mr. Hobson would have him taken - he said he did not care; he could not give him more than six weeks, for he did not break any locks.
JAMES TAYLOR. I am an officer, and took the prisoner into custody.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-244]
1831. GEORGE COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, 1 pair of half boots, price 2s., the goods of Thomas Hicks.
THOMAS HICKS. I am a broker, and live in Club-row, Bethnal-green. These boots lay on my table, about half-past three o'clock, on the 6th of September, and in aboutten minutes I missed them - I had been backwards; the prisoner came and asked me to buy a curry comb - I missed the boots and took hold of him; he said he would knock me down - he then produced the boots from his side pocket.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You deal in marine-stores? A. Yes.
WILLIAM GREVILLE. I am an officer, and took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoners's Defence. A young man who was with me dropped the boots out of his pocket, and asked me to put them into mine, which I did.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-245]
1831. THOMAS DELAY was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 1 watch chain, value 4l.; 2 seals, value 20s., and 1 watch key, value 5s., the goods of John Johnson, from his person.
MR. CLARKSON conducted the prosecution.
JOHN JOHNSON. I live in Holywell-street, Westminster, and am a pavior. On the 13th of September I was at the Haymarket Theatre, and left about half-past eleven o'clock- I was going up St. James-street, and the prisoner came in front of me, and siezed my chain and seals. I put my hand down, and caught the watch; the chain and seals were broken from it - I pursued him behind the coaches, but I never lost sight of him - I caught hold of his coat, the flap tore off - I seized him again; he threw me down, and we both fell into the mud, but I never lost sight of him.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me give any thing to any body? A. No; I had taken rather too much wine; my watch was safe at the corner of St. James-street.
WILLIAM DARDNER. I am a watchman of the Hay-market. I saw the prosecutor and prisoner laying one on other, in the road, in the Haymarket - I seized the prisoner, and took him to the watch-house - I found nothing on him.
SAMUEL LACK. I assisted in taking him to the watch-house. I looked down, about ten yards from him, and found a watch, with the spring broken; it was running down when I took it up; he was taken to the watch-house - that watch was claimed by another gentleman.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not say it was found one hundred yards from me? A. No - ten yards.
JAMES STAPLES. I am shopman to Mr. Gilbert, of Cockspur-street. I have some broken pieces of chain which came into our possession yesterday.
Prisoner's Defence. I was returning from a friend's house, this gentleman took hold of me, and thinking him rather in liquor, I endeavoured to get from his grasp; he seized me again, and we fell down; if he never lost sight of me how could I part with it?
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-246]
1832. MARY EDWARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of July, 1 pair of boots, value 5s., the goods of Eliza Amphlet, widow, and 1 pair of boots, value 6s., the goods of William Dudley.
WILLIAM DUDLEY. I am servant to Eliza Amphlet. On the 19th of July, at five o'clock in the morning, the watchman alarmed us, and I missed a pair of boots, shoes and brushes from the boot-hole, by the kitchen door; they were safe at ten o'clock the night before - I know nothing of the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JAMES BOUND. I am a farrier, and saw the prisoner on the 20th, in Bell-street, selling these boots to a Jew - I said nothing to them - Dudley came to me next day, and I told her of it - I know the prisoner and the Jew by sight. On the Monday I saw the Jew, and told him of it - I had seen the shoes and boots before - Dudley had shown them to me - the Jew gave up the property on receiving 4s.
WILLIAM DUDLEY re-examined. Bound pointed out a Jew to me, and I got the shoes from him - I got the boots from a pawnbroker's, which he took me to; he would not pay the 4s. to get them out, and I paid it.
FRANCIS BRADBURY. I am a constable. I took up the prisoner - I went to the Jew's house, but he has not been found since.
Prisoner's Defence. I slept at Porto Bello farm that night - I was coming to Paddington to buy some victuals, and met a women, who sold me these boots and shoes for 4s. - I saw the Jew, and sold them to him for 6s. 6d.
GUILTY. Aged 41.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-247]
1833. JOSEPH MARSHALL was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of July, 5 live tame rabbits, price 7s., the property of James Walker.
JAMES WALKER. I live in Hyde-place, Westminster. On the 22d of July, in the morning, I missed five live rabbits, which were safe the night before - I do not know the prisoner.
ELEANOR CHICK. I live at the Crown and Sceptre public-house. On the 22d of July, at half-past six o'clock in the morning. I was at my window; as I was getting up, I saw the prisoner climb the wall; he returned with two rabbits, which he gave to another person - I went to call Ridley, and when I returned I saw him give the other man two more; he then went and got another, and gave that to him.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Why did you not give an alarm? A. I called Ridley as soon as I was dressed.
THOMAS RIDLEY. Chick called me. I slipped on my trousers, and saw the prisoner over the wall with two rabbits, which he gave to a person on the other side; he got over, and carried one away in a bag; he took them from Walker's premises.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you say a word about this, till you heard the rabbits were stolen? A. I mentioned it to several people, and on Monday morning Mrs. Walker came to me about it; the wall is about five feet high; they also got over a fence.
JOHN WARNER. I was on duty with Walker, who is a watchman; he gave me information, and I took up the prisoner; he said he was in Chapman-street, with his brother, looking at a fight at the time.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-248]
1834. ROBERT ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of July, 3 books, value 8s., the goods of William Wilkins Wilton.
WILLIAM WILKINS WILTON. I am a bookseller, and live in High Holborn. On the 23d of July I saw the prisoner looking at my books; my window is open; he put some up, and took others down, and in about twenty minutes went away - I saw a vacancy, and went after him, and took him with three books.
FREDERICK DORRINGTON. I am an officer. The prisoner was given in charge - he pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Fifteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-249]
1835. MARY ANN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 1 watch, value 3l., the goods of William Saucutt.
WILLIAM SAUCUTT. On the 17th of July, about half-past twelve o'clock at night, I met the prisoner in Leonard-street, Shoreditch, and while I was speaking to her, I found her hand in my waistcoat pocket - I felt, and missed my watch; she denied it - I called Watch! she then asked if I thought she could be guifty of it - I took hold of her hand, and said I would not let her go; she then put her hand behind her, and gave it to me; she used bad language, and I gave charge of her.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. Did you not take liberties with her? A. I spoke to her - I asked who she was waiting for - she said her friend had got her key, and she could not get in; nothing more passed - I am a cabinet-maker - I was not in liquor at all - I did not ask her to go to a house with me.
JOHN RENCH. I am a watchman. I heard a call of Watch! and took her in charge - the prosecutor appeared sober.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you hear her say she was a respectable person, and he had no business to speak to her? A. No - she said he was not robbed, he had got his property again.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that the prosecutor had taken very indecent liberties with her, and as she had resented them, he charged her with the robbery.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-250]
1836. JOHN WALKER was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, 6 shutters, value 9s., the goods of Frances Corbell.
HARLEY ARCHER. I deal in building materials, and live in the New-road. On the afternoon of the 3d of August the prisoner brought me three shutters - I did not buy them, for while I was waiting on another customer, he left them, and went away; he said he had bought them of a Mr. Scott, in Shakspeare-walk.
JOHN SCOTT. I know nothing of these shutters. I employ the prisoner at times.
GEORGE DEVERELL. I took the prisoner in charge; he said he hoped I should take young Lazel, as he was as much to blame - I took him; he was bound over to prosecute, but is not here, nor is the prosecutrix.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-251]
1837. CHARLES POPE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of May, 1 pair of shoes, value 6s., the goods of Joseph Bordey, his master.
MR. PRENDERGAST conducted the prosecution.
JOSEPH BORSLEY. The prisoner was my clicker; he used to cut out, and deliver goods to workmen, and assist in selling in the shop. In consequence of information, I had him taken - I never gave him authority to part with these shoes.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. You are not always in the business? A. No; my nephew and neice act as cashiers; they are not here; they could not give him authority - I had the shoes in my hand, the day after they were made, and complained of the work; we had not above a dozen pairs of boys shoes in the house.
CHARLES OGLE. I am a tailor, and live in North Audley-street. The prisoner came to me about a pair of shoes, which I ordered of him, and he sent, or brought them home - I was making clothes for him, and did not pay him for them.
Cross-examined. Q. You do not know who brought them? A. No - they were never worn, as they were too large for my son - I kept them for about eleven months - I never settled with him; my son may have worn them in the house; but never went out in them.
MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Did you see him after they came? A. Yes, and told him they were too large, but thought they would fit him bye and bye; the prisoner saw them then.
JOHN LACEY. I made these shoes for Mr. Borsley, about Christmas, and know them.
CHARLES OGLE. I cannot say whether I had them before Christmas or after, but think it was before.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-252]
1838. GEORGE CRIBB, JOSEPH HANKINS, and JAMES MORLEY were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 1 watch, value 2l. 10s., the goods of Samuel Martin, from his person.
SAMUEL MARTIN. I was in Whitechapel-road on the 17th of August, it was towards the evening, I received a very heavy blow on my back; I looked round, and, on turning, my watch was snatched from my fob. I turned round, and laid hold of Cribb, who was close behind me; whether he struck me I do not know.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. What are you? A. A gardener - I sometimes work as a porter; it was a silver watch, and belonged to my son. I had worn it before, but did not wear any seals to show it. I was going along with a large geranium - I put the watch into my pocket about twelve or one o'clock, when I cleaned myself and came out - I know Jeffrey East, to my sorrow; he is my landlord; I did not speak to him that day - I have not seen him since; he never said I never had a watch. I saw Jackson next day; he called me into his house to try to draw me; I mentioned my loss to him. I did not tell him Sheen had seen me with the watch. I never told Evans I had taken it out of pawn that day from Philpots - I have worked for Mr. Payne, of the Bank.
THOMAS SMITH. I am beadle of Whitechapel. On the 17th of August I was in the road, and saw a mob - I found the prosecutor and Cribb having an altercation; he said he had shoved against him, and two more had stolen his watch. I took Cribb to the watch-house. I found no watch.
DAVID LEARY. I sell fruit in the street. I was in Whitechapel, and saw the prisoners going along, and threeboys behind them - Cribb was one; he was taken; the others ran away. I did not see them do any thing to him at all; he cried Stop thief! and the other two ran away; the officer came up in three minutes; Cribb tried to run away - he denied the charge.
Cross-examined. Q. What distance were you from them? A. About five yards - I did not see any thing taken.
GEORGE PINCOMB. I took up the other prisoners; Leary went and pointed them out to me - as we went along Morley was rescued by Mary Sheen, a girl of the town; but I afterwards took him; she said they were innocent; Cribb said before the Magistrate that he was with some carmen that afternoon - the watch has not been found.
MR. CLARKSON called -
JEFFREY EAST. I am a sawyer. and live in Wright's-buildings; the prosecutor is a tenant of mine - he has been badly off for the last five years. I never saw him with a watch in my life; he told me he had been robbed of one; I said it was odd, for I never knew him have one; he said,"Oh! Yes, I took it out of pawn yesterday morning from Philpot's, at Bermondsey." I know he often borrowed money to get victuals - I would not believe him on his oath.
COURT. Q. Why? A. I believe him a very bad character - he has no lease of my house; I want to get rid of him- I never knew him swear false; he is given to drink; I think he was fresh when he spoke to me; he had come home in the night in question quite tipsy; I would not believe him if he was sober, for I believe he never had a watch.
SAMUEL MARTIN re-examined. I was once taken to Union-hall for an assault, and was acquitted; this man's wife once took me there, but they were glad to make it up. I was never there at any other time; she charged me with indecently exposing myself.
COURT. Q. Were you ever charged with any thing else? A. Never.
THOMAS JACKSON. I live at No. 9, Wright's-buildings, Spa-road, Bermondsey, and live on my income. I have known Martin ten years; he was always in very indigent circumstances. I never saw him with a watch; I saw him on the 27th of August, in the morning, and told him I had read in the newspaper of his having been robbed of a watch- he said it was true, and that he had been hustled by three youths; I said I never saw him with one; he said, "I don't wish all the world to know what I do with my property;" he said he took it out of pawn at Thomas', which is now Philpot's; I said, "You must get a document from the pawnbroker to satisfy the Court that you had it;" he said there was no necessity for that; he afterwards said, "That is already done;" he said the person he was taking the geranium to, could prove that he saw it three-quarters of an hour before; I said, "Why, you was going there?" he said, "Yes, and while I was there, a person came in who had been robbed of 30s." I know Cribb's parents, but not till after this transaction.
COURT. Q. Have you quarrelled with Martin? A. No.
SAMUEL MARTIN. This man says very wrong. I was coming up the street, and he was very much inebriated, and called me an old rogue, and a d-d b-g-r, because I did not speak to him.
- SHEEN. My husband lives in Gloucester-terrace. Martin came to my house with a geranium; I do not exactly know the day; he did not complain of his loss to me, or in my presence.
LUCY PHILPOT. I keep a pawnbroker's-shop in Bermondsey-street. I know nothing of the prosecutor - I had no watch pawned in the name of Martin. I have searched the books to see; he has applied to me since, to ask if we give tickets; I said, "Yes, when we take pledges;" he appeared confused; I said perhaps he alluded to a watch which was inquired about; my husband said, "We have examined the file, and find no such thing."
SAMUEL MARTIN. I had pawned a watch there some years ago, in the time of Mr. Thomas, and wanted to know if they could find the ticket.
MARTHA EVANS. I am the wife of William Evans - we live in Wright's-buildings. I have known Martin five or six years; he is a very poor man - I never saw him with a watch. On the day the prisoners were committed, he came into my house and sat down, gave my son a paper and said, "There John, read that; your mother do'nt seem to believe I had a watch;" I said, "I never knew you had one;" he said, "Read that paper, it will convince you I took it out from Thomas the morning I lost it;" I did not see the paper.
JOHN OVERILL. I live in Amelia-row, Spa-road, Bermondsey. i have known Martin twelve or thirteen years; he was always a poor indigent man; he has a pension of 9d. a day, as a soldier - he generally gets drunk when he receives it, and is then in want.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-253]
1839. JOHN COSGROVE was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of James Lloyd Jones, from his person.
JAMES LLOYD JONES I am a draper, and live at Charing-cross. On the 5th of August I was in St. James'-park - two boys were going to fight, which caused a mob; a boy told me, a boy had taken my handkerchief - I felt and it was gone. I found the prisoner in Bertraun's charge. he pulled two handkerchiefs from his trousers; one of which was mine - I had not seen him near me.
ANGELIOUS BERTRAUN. I was in the Park, and saw the prisoner take this handkerchief from the prosecutor; he walked round the crowd; I took him and found two handkerchiefs in his trousers - Jones claimed one.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Are you sure you saw all this? A. Yes.
GUILTY. Aged 10.
Recommended to Mercy. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-254]
1840. DENNIS CONNOR was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of August, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Edwin Henry Thomas, from his person.
EDWIN HENRY THOMAS. I live at Birmingham. On the 2d of August I was in Coventry-street, and heard a cry of Stop thief! from a gentleman who was with me; I felt my pocket, and my handkerchief was gone - I had had it a very few minutes before; I turned round and accused the prisoner of taking it - he denied it, but put his hands behind him, and dropped it on the pavement - I called the watchman, who took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS WRIGHT. I am a watchman, and took him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. Several other persons were there. I never had the handkerchief.
EDWIN HENRY THOMAS. I am certain it fell from him; no other person was near.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-255]
1841. JOHN SULLIVAN and EDWARD MIDDLETON were indicted for stealing, on the 23d of August, I fixture (i.e.) 1 window-sash, with six squares of glass, value 6s., the goods of the St. Katherine's Dock Company .
JOHN THORBY. I am a watchman of St. Katherine's Docks. On the 23d of August, about one o'clock in the morning, I saw the two prisoners walking in front of a house, and in five minutes, saw them taking a sash out - I called another watchman. we went in and found the prisoners laying in a room, within two or three yards of the sash - I am sure they are the same men; it was an empty house - they had taken it down, and laid it against the wall.
WILLIAM SITGRAVE. I know this house; it has been in possession of the St. Katherine's Dock Company for some months; this is the sash.
SULLIVAN'S Defence. I merely went there to sleep, as I was out late.
SULLIVAN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
MIDDLETON - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-256]
1842. WILLIAM CARTY and BENJAMIN DOWNING were indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of William George Whitmarsh, from his perosn.
WILLIAM GEORGE WHITMARSH. On the 10th of September, about half-past eleven o'clock at night, I was in Leicester-square, with two ladies. I felt my pocket move, turned round, and saw Downing give my handkerchief to Carty(it was safe three minutes before) and Carty gave it to some women.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see it go from you? A. No - I have not got it back.
EDWARD ROURK. I am a watchman; the prosecutor gave the prisoners into my charge, and on the way to the watch-house, Carty offered to make up for it, if the prosecutor would let him go.
DOWNING'S Defence. I was going along, and took my hat off to wipe my face, this gentleman came and accused me of picking his pocket. I said he had better call the watchman, if he suspected me - I know nothing of Carty.
CARTY'S Defence. I was going along, and the gentleman took us - I was on before the gentleman.
Carty received an excellent character.
CARTY - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Six Months.
DOWNING - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-257]
1843. JANE FEARNLEY, SARAH BROWN, and LYDIA CHANDLER were indicted for stealing, on the 22d of July, 4 sovereigns, and 18 shillings, the monies of James Powell, from his person.
JAMES POWELL. On the 22d of July, between one and two o'clock in the morning, I was in the street - I could not get into my own lodgings. I met Fearnley, and west with her to her lodgings, which are a second floor back room in Collingwood-street - I was perfectly sober - I did not go to bed. I had not been in the room two minutes before I heard sovereigns jingle - she was sitting at my side. I had examined the contents of my purse previous to going there, and had spoken to nobody but herself. I put my hand to my pocket, and my purse was gone. I accused her of robbing me, and insisted on her returning it to me; she then called out, and the other two prisoners attempted to get into the room - I did all I could to prevent their coming in till the watchman came, but they did come in, and shortly after, I saw Chandler standing with her back to Fearnley, and putting her hand behind her - I suppose she took my purse from her. Brown shortly afterwards went out of the room - the watchman came in soon after, as the landlady had called him; he searched, but could not find my money - Fearnley and Chandler were still in the room.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were you quite sober? A. Yes - I had met some people on business, and left them about twelve o'clock, I slept in Collingwood-street after this, at the same house - it is in the City-road - I could not get a bed any where else. I suppose I was robbed before the other two came into the room.
JOHN YABSLEY. I am a watchman. I went to No. 3, Collingwood-street, and found the prosecutor, Fearnley, and Chandler, all in the second floor back room - he said he had been robbed of four sovereigns and about 20s. in silver - he accused Fearnley of taking it, and said Chandler came to the door and stood before Fearnley. I took Brown in her own room in the same house - she said she had got nothing - I found no purse - she denied having been into the room.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you not been away before you found Brown? A. Yes - a short time.
GEORGE WILMOT. I was with Yabsley. The prosecutor said Brown was the girl who left the room - she said"It is a lie, I was not near the room, and know nothing about it;" and that she had not been out of the house, but the landlady said it was false, that she had been out, and she let her in again - Powell seemed to have been drinking a little, and was alarmed.
JAMES POWELL. I am certain Brown came in with Chandler.
Cross-examined. Q. Did they come in together or not? A. To the best of my recollection they came in together.
FEARNLEY'S Defence. I met this gentleman, who went home with me. I refused to go to bed till he gave me some money - I got up to come away; he pulled me about very much; Brown came to the door but did not enter - Chandler came in; he then left off pulling me about; I said"You good for nothing wretch, I will have the watchman here;" he then said, I had robbed him - the money that chinked was 3s. 6d. I had in my bosom.
BROWN'S Defence. I was not out until I was taken.
JAMES POWELL. I heard the money jink on my left side - Fearnley sat close to my side.
Brown received a good character.
FEARNLEY - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
BROWN - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Six Months.
CHANDLER - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-258]
1844. JAMES MAGRATH was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of James Edward Groom, from his person.
JAMES EDWARD GROOM. I was in St. Martin's-court between nine and ten o'clock on the 19th of August I had used my handkerchief a few minutes before - I heard a person call, "Stop that gentleman!" I stopped; put my hand to my pocket, and it was gone - I saw the prisoner in custody at the time.
JOHN BOSTON. I was in St. Martin's-court. I saw the prisoner and another lad - I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the gentleman's pocket; I called out, and took it from the prisoner.
JOHN GROOM. I came up and saw Boston with the prisoner; he gave me this handkerchief.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This man came up, and said,"You have got that gentleman's handkerchief;" and he had it in his other hand at the time.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-259]
1845. WILLIAM SPARIB was indicted for stealing on the 11th of August, 1 hankerchief, value 3s., the goods of William Jackson, from his person.
GEORGE JAMES JENNINGS. On the 11th of August I was at the office, and received charge of the prisoner.
WILLIAM JACKSON. I gave the prisoner into custody. I was in Tavistock-street, Covent-garden, at eleven o'clock in the morning; I observed some one near me, turned round, and saw the prisoner moving his hand from my pocket, with my handkerchief in it - he put his hand behind and let it fall.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a very good character.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-260]
1846. JOHN BODELL was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 2 yards of woollen cloth, value 3s. 6d.; 1 yard of cambric, value 6s.; 2 yards of lace, value 1s. 10d.: 1 piece of linen cloth, containing in length 26 yards, value 2l.; 10 yards of printed cotton, value 10s., and 2 yards of silk, value 6s., the goods of Samuel Cater and Thomas Wood, from the person of Charles Lovegrove ; and MARY HAMMOND was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
CHARLES LOVEGROVE. I am servant to Messrs. Samuel Cater and Thomas Wood, linen-draper, of Chiswell-street. On the 8th of September, about eight o'clock, I was conveying a parcel to Mr. Brown, of Guildford-street - Saville gave it to me; when I was in Old-street, about eight in the evening, Bodell came behind me and pulled it from under my arm, and ran across into Golden-lane - I saw his face as he turned round, but did not see what kind of clothes he had on - I am certain of his person from his clothes and his features; I saw him by the light of the gas. I pursued directly, calling Stop thief! and somebody came behind me in Golden-lane, and struck me a severe blow over the ear, which hindered my observation - I had, before that, seen Bodell turn up Busket-alley, which leads into Whitecross-street. I described him to Harrison - he wore a blue coat, and, I believe, corderoy trousers; I went that night with Harrison to the Barley-mow, public-house, Blue Anchor-alley, and saw about a dozen people there, but he was not there. On Sunday evening the officer called, and shewed me some lace, cambric, and Irish linen; I went to the office on Monday, there were about thirty persons there; I pointed out Bodell at once as the person.
GEORGE SAVILLE. I am shopman to the prosecutors. I delivered the parcel to Lovegrove; it contained the articles stated in the indictment - there was a private mark on the articles; and it was tied in brown paper.
THOMAS HARRISON. I am an officer. On Saturday, the 8th of September, Lovegrove described the person and dress of Bodell to me; but I did not know where he lived. I went to the Barley-mow, he was not there - next evening Lock and I went to the Barley-mow again; we found Bodell there, and a man named Burke - we took them both, but Burke was discharged at the office; we put them into the watch-house, then went to Burke's lodging, and found Hammond there; I asked her where Burke was, she said she expected him every minute to tea, the tea-things were on the table. I saw a bag hanging by the fire-side, and in it found this piece of lace, and a piece of cambric; she said Burke had brought them in, in his hat, and gave them to her - Lock turned the bed down, and under the clothes found this Irish linen. I went to Worship-street; put Bodell into the room with twenty or thirty others, and Lovegrove immediately identified him.
ROBERT LOCK. I was with Harrison; his account is correct. I went to Worship-street; Lovegrove pointed out Bodell directly, among about thirty persons, without any intimation from us.
Hammond put in a written defence, stating that the property had been left at her room, by a man who had spent the previous night with her; and was to return for them, and give her some money; but not wishing to expose herself, she had not stated this to the officers.
BODELL - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
HAMMOND - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-261]
1847. CATHERINE HERRING was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 half-crown, 9 shillings, and 5 sixpences, the property of William Whent, from his person.
WILLIAM WHENT. I am a labourer. On the 17th of July, about two o'clock, I was at the Three Compasses, public-house; I saw the prisoner and three other women there - I had seen the prisoner before; I remained there about half an hour: I had some gin and porter - I then went home with the prisoner, to some street near the Commercial-road, and had tea - I went to bed with her, having wound up my watch and put it into my trousers' under my head; in about three-quarters of an hour I was taken sick, and she called a young woman, who brought some water - the prisoner then got out of bed; she had her gown half-on. I saw her take my money, but not my watch; she went out - I then got up, and missed my watch: which I have not seen since - I lost 14s. I was about an hour and a half with her, and am certain of her; I was quite sober - I suppose the heat of the room made me sick.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. On the 18th of July, I found the prisoner concealed in a house, in a court; she denied having seen the prosecutor.
The prisoner put in a written defence, denying the charge - and stating that the prosecutor was intoxicated.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-262]
1848. JOHN LINDSAY was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, 1 handkerchief, value 1s.; 1 pair of buckles, value 3s.; 1 pencil-case, value 2d.; 1 key, value 6d.; 1 pair of shoes, value 5s.; 1 knife, value 5s.; 1 shilling, and 2 sovereigns, the property of Robert Thomas Griffiths, from his person.
ROBERT THOMAS GRIFFITHS. I am a servant. On the 12th of September, about half-past two o'clock in the morning, I was at the corner of Pickett-street, Strand, sleeping; the watchman awoke me, and asked if I had lost any thing; I missed my shoes from my feet, and these other things.
MICHAEL BOWER. I am a watchman. About half-past two o'clock I passed the prisoner, but said nothing to him - he passed me again shortly after - I asked where he was going; he said home - soon after, I saw Griffiths laying down without his shoes, and asked the prisoner where they were - he said he did not know - I took hold of him and found them on him, also the handkerchief and all this property.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been to the play, and found the handkerchief with the things in it - the shoes laid by the parcel.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-263]
1849. WILLIAM WILLIAMS and JAMES WILSON were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 half sovereign, and 22 shillings, the monies of James Murphy, from his person.
JAMES MURPHY. On the 14th of August, between six and seven o'clock, I was at the corner of George-street, looking at a mob, and had some money in my breeches pocket - Williams came and seized me by the shoulders; they thrust me into a passage of a house in George-street; Williams got my purse out of my pocket - I struggled to get it; he bit a hole in the purse and took out a half sovereign, which I have not got back - I cannot tell how much I had.
FRANCIS THOMAS GATES. I apprehended the prisoners, whom Murphy pointed out - he charged them with an assault - I said he had better get a warrant, but he gave charge of them for a robbery in about five minutes after.
WILLIAMS' Defence. He was quarrelling with a woman whom he struck, and I struck him - he pushed me into a passage; Wilson was chief evidence against Murphy, and he gave charge of him, because he should give no more evidence against him.
JAMES MURPHY. I gave charge for an assault, as I did not then know I had lost my money; I struck nobody.
WILSON's Defence. I saw the prosecutor quarrelling with a woman; he struck her, and I then struck him; he ran in and shut the door; he came out and gave charge of Williams for an assault. I was going as a witness for Williams - he then said I had assisted in the robbery.
MARIA MINNS. I was in the street - Murphy came up and struck me - I had quarrelled with him before, because some coiners were taken up in his house, and he thought I had told of them - he struck me in the chest, and then in the side - I was three weeks in the hospital; I saw no robbery - he keeps nobody but thieves and coiners in his house.
MARIA WHITE. I was coming up Church-street, and saw several people running - Murphy hit and kicked this woman - Williams took her part; Murphy then said he would have his spite on him.
FRANCIS THOMAS GATES re-examined. Williams gave this account at the time I took him - Murphy keeps a lodging house in Church-lane - I afterwards saw his purse - there was a piece torn or bit out. I saw Minns that evening; she said Murphy had struck her, but said nothing about a kick - I found Williams in a room with Minns.
JAMES MURPHY. She cohabits with Williams.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-264]
1850. WILLIAM TOBIN was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, 4 half-crowns, and 8 shillings, the monies of Timothy Morgan. from his person.
TIMOTHY MORGAN. I am a labourer. On the 5th of August I went to Saffron-hill watch-house, to see a man whom I knew - the prisoner was sitting close to me, and I felt his hand come out of his pocket - I felt and missed my money. I then got up and caught hold of him, and was going to serve him out; then he and another got up and struck me; I lost six half-crowns, some shillings, and sixpence.
JOHN BARNLY. I am constable of the night; I saw the prosecutor at the watch-house. I was told he had been robbed; I searched them all, but found no money on any of them; I was looking about, and behind the door of the lock-up place, I found this handkerchief with three half-crowns and four shillings in it.
JOHN WATSON. I am a watchman; I took morgan to the watch-house to see a man; he was in liquor; he took out four half-crowns, and said, "This is my money, nobody shall have it;" he put it back again.
CHARLES VINCENT. I was constable of the night; I took the prisoner in charge before Morgan came in; I searched him he had no money; but in his hat I found this handkerchief, in which the money was afterwards found; I know it was his, for I had him in custody a night or two before, and he had the same handkerchief then.
Prisoner's Defence. The handkerchief came with bread and butter in it, to a prisoner named Sullivan.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-265]
1851. ANN BUTLER was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of July, 1 sheet, value 2s.; 1 blanket, value 3s., and 1 flat-iron, value 6d., the goods of George Read.
MATILDA READ. I am the wife of George Read. The prisoner lodged with us; on the 17th of July I missed these things on going into her room.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I am an officer, and found the duplicate on her.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I am a pawnbroker. I have a sheetand iron, pawned by the prisoner, on the 28th and 29th of January.
JOHN LLOYD. I am a pawnbroker. I have a bed-quilt, pawned by the prisoner, on the 2d of July.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-266]
1852. ANN CONNOR was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 1 shawl, value 15s., and 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Richard Elliott.
SARAH ELLIOTT. I am the wife of Richard Elliott - we live at Windsor. On the 17th of July, about eleven o'clock, I went into the Ship public-house, James-street, Covent-garden; I had this handkerchief and shawl on my neck; there were four females there - one of them took up my glass of porter, and drank it; they then surrounded me, and insisted on my giving them liquor, but I had only three halfpence, which I paid for the beer - I went into the bar, to the landlord, and asked him if he would take either of my silk handkerchiefs for half-a-pint of gin, which they pressed me to give them. The prisoner, who was one of them, then spoke up violently, and said she would take my pockets off; she reached over the bar, took my handkerchief and shawl off, and ran away with them.
JOHN KNOWLES. I am a pawnbroker. I produce a shawl, which was pawned on the 17th of July, by a woman, in the name of Ann Connor - I cannot swear to the prisoner.
FRANCIS FAGAN. I am an officer. I received information, and took the prisoner on the 17th of July. I found no duplicate on her.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw the prosecutrix in liquor - several men were with her; I was afterwards in the Ship: she came in, and had some liquor; some time after she came with a beadle, and took me, but he let me go twice - he took me a third time; the prosecutrix is an unfortunate woman, and is not married.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-267]
1853. DANIEL HAGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, 1 saw, value 3s. 6d., the goods of William Nicks.
THOMAS ARMSTRONG. I am a pawnbroker. I have a saw, which the prisoner pawned on the 10th of September, in the name of Jones.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Did you know him before? A. No; he was taken in a quarter of an hour.
WILLIAM NICKS. I lost this saw from St. Katherine's Dock.
Cross-examined. Q. Were you not on intimate terms with the prisoner? A. No - I knew him. I had pawned his saw some time before, as it could not be used. I have sometimes gone by the name of Jones.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-268]
1854. FREDERICK MOODY and THOMAS GALLIGHAN were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 17 handkerchiefs, value 24s., the goods of William Jones.
ROBERT MARKS. I live with Wm. Jones, a pawnbroker. These handkerchiefs were safe in his shop on the afternoon of the 20th of August - I did not miss them till they were brought to me next morning - I know nothing of the prisoners.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I am an officer. I received information, and took the prisoners, in company together, between nine and ten o'clock in the morning of the 21st of August; I found on Gallighan these six handkerchiefs, between his shirt and waistcoat.
WILLIAM SCHOFIELD. I was with Timbrell. I found these eleven handkerchiefs on Moody.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MOODY's Defence. I was near the Horse Guards, and met a man running very fast with two or three bundles - he dropped one, which I took up; it contained this property.
GALLIGHAN's Defence. I met my fellow prisoner - a man in a blue cloak ran by us; he dropped a bundle - we took it up, and shared the handkerchiefs.
MOODY - GUILTY. Aged 15.
GALLIGHAN - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-269]
Fourth Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1855. THOMAS SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of August, 1 pair of shoes, value 6s.; 1 Guernseyfrock, value 2s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 5s., and 1 knife, value 6d., the goods of James Kenney.
JAMES KENNEY. I live at Limehouse, and keep a shop. I was in the back room - the prisoner came and said he wanted to fit out, to sail; I brought out some shoes, handkerchiefs, and other things, which he looked at; he wished a better assortment, and showed me an order to the amount of 2l. I went out for half an hour, to get some of the things, leaving him with my wife; when I returned he was gone with these things.
SUSAN KENNEY. I am the prosecutor's wife. My husband went out to get some things, to show the prisoner - he sat in a chair, close to the door in the parlour; he tried on a frock and shoes - I said, "Your are not going to put them on?" he said, "No, I am only going to try them;" I wrapped up two silk handkerchiefs for him: the bell rang - I went into the shop, and on my return he had gone out at the passage door. I missed this property - he left his own shoes behind.
JAMES DRYDEN. I am an officer. I have a pair of shoes and a Guernsey-frock, which I took off the prisoner's person, about six days after the robbery - he was intoxicated.
Prisoner's Defence. The prosecutor agreed to let me have the things, and he was to be paid for them when I got to Gravesend. I merely went out for some tobacco, and seeing the ship moving, I went to assist, which was my duty- I went to join the ship, and found another man was engaged in my room.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-270]
1856. WILLIAM DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 4lbs. of beef, value 2s., the goods of Edward Coles.
JOHN COLES. I live with Edward Coles, my cousin, who is a butcher. On the 11th of September, about nine o'clock, the prisoner came, and told me to weigh a shoulder of lamb; I turned round, and saw him reach 4lbs. of beefout of the window, and run away. I gave an alarm - he was pursued and taken.
RICHARD CUFF. I am an officer, and have the beef.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Strongly recommended to Mercy. - Confined Seven Days.

[trialAccount t18270913-272]
1858. THOMAS DEAN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 119lbs. of lead, value 16s., the goods of the St. Katherine's Dock Company .
JOHN THORRY. I am one of the watchmen of St. Katherine's Docks. On the 7th of August, about half-past eight o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoner taking some lead off a small wash-house; he broke it and doubled it up; I sent for an officer and had him taken - he had removed it from the top to the bottom - it belonged to the Company.
ROBERT HENRY BURGESS. I was called by Thorby - we could not see the prisoner; I called for a light, and found him in the adjoining washhouse, and found the lead on the ground; I compared it with the place - it fits exactly with what remains.
WILLIAM SITGRAVE. I am clerk to Messrs. Paulin and Co., who are employed by the St. Katherine's Dock Company - they have been in possession of this house some time - it is in Mablin's-rents, all of which is in the Company's possession; I had possession of the house for them.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going into this house to look for my trowel - I had been at work in Burr-street.
JOHN THORBY. I am sure he is the man whom I saw pulling the lead off the wash-house.
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-273]

1860. JOSEPH WIDDOWSON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of July, 1 box, value 2s.; 1 coat. value 7s.; 1 waistcoat, value 2s.; 2 pairs of breeches, value 2s.; 3 pairs of shoes, value 4s.; 2 shirts, value 3s; 4 pairs of stockings, value 4s., and 1 Bible, value 3s., the goods of John Kennett.
JOHN KENNETT. I live with Mr. King, in Grosvenor-Place. I left this property in the area passage on the 25th of June, when I went down to the country house - it was all packed up together - it was missed on the 31st; I saw it again on the 3d of August - I know nothing of the prisoner.
JOHN LEE. I am butler to Mr. King. Kennett left this property in the passage, in a box - I saw it safe on the 31st of June - I was informed by Dorman that he had men a suspicious man carrying it by his door; we pursued and caught the prisoner crossing Belgrave-place with it; he said a gentleman had given it him to carry to a coach office, and that he was to have 1s, for it.
HENRY DORMAN. I am a publican. On the 31st of July I was in the bar, and saw the prisoner pass with the box, which I had seen at Mr. King's, as I serve them; I went and found it was gone; we then pursued and took him.
THOMAS WITT. I was in Chapel-street, about one hundred yards from this house, and saw some men with this box on one of their shoulders; the prisoner said to them,"Come along;" they went along together.
JOHN HILL. I am am an officer. I received him in charge with the box.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was going to Chelsea, and met a man, who asked me to carry the box.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.

1862. MARGARET BUNBURY was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 25 lbs. weight of feathers, value 10s., the goods of Daniel Gartland.
ESTHER GARTLAND. I am the wife of Daniel Gartland - we live in Drury-court, Strand; the prisoner lodged with us for five weeks, and slept with my children. I saw her go out three times, on the 25th July, with something tied in a shawl, and when I went to put the child to bed, I found feathers scattered about the room; I thought she had done it because we had had a few words, but on looking I found the bed had been cut in two places, and sewed up with new thread - almost all the feathers were gone; she came home in about an hour, and I gave her in charge; I have found none of the feathers; she had told me the tick wanted mending.
JAMES COUSINS. I took her in charge; the feathers were strewed about the room - I found a quantity of feathers sticking to her shawl.
Prisoner's Defence. The feathers were thrown about in changing the sheets that afternoon.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-277]
1863. JOHN PARROTT and THOMAS FLYNN were indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 4 hats, value 3l. 12s., the goods of James Fisher.
ANGELIOUS BERTRAUN. I am a constable. On the 31st of July, at eight o'clock in the morning, I was in Oxford-street; I saw the prisoners together, and watched them to the prosecutor's shop; Parrott went in, and Flynn was looking in at the window; Parrott came out with the hats - they ran off together; I pursued them to the corner of King-street; I saw Parrott give Flynn two of the hats to hold while he opened a bag; they went on; I followed, calling Stop thief! I secured Flynn; another person took Parrott in my sight; I took them to the watch-house, and took the hats to the prosecutor - I knew Flynn before.
JAMES FISHER. I live in Regent-street, near the corner of Oxford-street; these hats are nine; I did not miss them till Bertiann brought them in.
PARROTT's Defence. I was in Oxford-street, looking for employ; a young man came and asked if I knew any thing of the hat business; and at the corner of King-street he gave me two hats to look at.
PARROTT - GUILTY. Aged 20.
FLYNN - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-278]
1864. JOHN GRACE was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, 2 books, value 6s., the goods of John Brooks, his master.
DAVID HERRING. I went, in company with Mr. Brooks, and found the prisoner in a room in St. Giles'; these two books were in the room; I asked where he got them; his mother said they were bought in Holborn, but she could not tell the shop; but at the office the prisoner said he took them to read and to return - he was servant to Mr. Brooks; I do not know Mr. Brooks' Christain name.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-279]
1865. JOHN DUDLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 1 spoon, value 12s., the goods of Richard Wharton.
ISABELLA VASEY. I am servant to Richard Wharton, who lives in Grafton-street, Bond-street. On the 11th of September, the prisoner was there, at work with others, white-washing the house - I lost a spoon off the shelf in the kitchen.
GEORGE ELIAS LAZARUS. I am a silversmith, and live at No. 10, Broad-street, Bloomsbury. On Tuesday, the 11th of September, the prisoner brought this spoon to sell, between two and three o'clock - I asked where he got it; he said he found it among some rubbish - I said that could not be, he had better go and get the owner; he went and brought Winsland, who said it was Mr. Wharton's.
NICHOLAS WINSLAND. I am a painter. I met the prisoner, who had worked for me; he came into my house, and confessed that he had stolen the spoon - I went with him to Lazarus, and told what he had said.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-280]
1866. DENNIS GALLAKER was indicted for feloniously assaulting John Pluck, with intent to rob him.
JOHN PLUCK. I am a carpenter, and live at Ponder's-end. On Saturday night, the 4th of August, about half-past eleven o'clock, I was coming towards Ponder's-end from Waltham-cross, in the road - I saw the prisoner in the footpath, reeling about, as if intoxicated; he was going in the same direction as me - I walked on to get out of his way; he came into the road, and asked how far I was going - I said, not far - I walked on at a good pace; he followed me, and went into the foot-path - I found he got nearer to me - I ran, and he ran, for about half a mile - I then got up to some houses; he came within about a yard of me, off the footpath into the road; he appeared to pull something out of his pocket, and said if I resisted the least, he would blow my brains out in a minute - I saw something black in his hands - I supposed I should be shot; he did not put any thing up to me - I ran up to some houses, about twenty yards off; he followed and asked where I was going; I said to speak to a friend; he went over the bridge - I went a back way to a constable's house, and as I was calling him up, the prisoner came by the door - I do not know whether he saw me; the officer and I followed him, and took him - I did not know him before.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What was in his hand? A. I do not know; he was searched, and a razor was found on him in a sheath.
JOHN WILSON. I am a constable. Pluck called me up and seemed much frightened - I went about three hundred yards and took the prisoner; he said he had only stood in his own defence, and he told the Magistrate that Pluck had shoved him in a ditch; he had nothing but this razor in a case - a pistol was found by somebody, in a ditch near the spot; the man who found it is not here.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been drinking pretty fresh; this man shoved me over into the hedge; I saw him running away - I ran after him, and said he ought to be shot.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-281]
1867. WILLIAM BROWNUTT was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 bonnet, value 10s.; 3 caps, value 3s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 1s.; 2 collars, value 1s.; 1 pair of bracelets, value 6d.; 4 shillings, 4 sixpences, and 6 halfpence, the property of Richard Wells.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I am an officer. On the 8th of September I saw the prisoner, with three others, in the New North-road, trying several area-gates; they stopped, and one of them disappeared for a short time; in a few minutes I saw the prisoner with this box under his arm - I sent Berry after the other two, but he mistook me; he went round and met the prisoner with the box, just as I got up to him - I had seen him at Mr. Well's house in Bridport-place, New-road - I went there, and they claimed the box - Mrs. Wells did not attend the office without a summons; and when she came, she would not swear to the property.
JOHN BERRY. I am a butcher. I saw the prisoner with three others in Bridport-place - I saw another go into the house, and bring this box out; he gave it to the prisoner - I ran and took him.
HANNAH WELLS. I am wife of Richard Wells, and live in Bridport-place. I had a cap like this, and have no doubt it is mine, and the bonnet is mine; they were in this bonnet-box; the bracelets are mine; the box was taken of a chair in the parlour - I do not know whether the door was left open.
Prisoner's Defence. I met the other lads, who asked me to carry the box, and told me to follow them; they brought it out of the house, and told me to go to the City-road.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-282]
1868. DANIEL SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of August, 1 tub, value 8s., the goods of Joseph Goodwin.
THOMAS SMITH. I am a watchman. On the 23d of August, about a quarter to three o'clock in the morning, I stopped the prisoner about a quarter of a mile fromGoodwin's premises - he had his hand on this tub, and was leaning on the rails - a man named Gaskin came in same direction, but I did not see them together; when I came up to the prisoner, he said "Is that you Bill - is that you Gaskin?" I said Yes; as soon as he found it was me, he shoved the tub from the rails, and ran away - I knew him before, and took him between nine and ten o'clock in the morning.
JOSEPH GOODWIN, JUN. I am the son of Joseph Goodwin - we live at Newington - this is the tub - it was stand - in a pond to swell. I had seen it there the day before.
SIMON CALLUM. I am a watchman. The prisoner and Gaskin passed my beat, and went towards the field where the tub was.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw the tub laying against the gate, and was going to take it home, not liking to be taken to the watch-house - I ran home.
JOSEPH GOODWIN. The prisoner worked for us at hay time - there was nothing against him.
NOT GUILTY.

[trialAccount t18270913-284]
1870. ROBERT OSBORN and THOMAS BRACE were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, 4 bushels of oats, value 12s.; 2 bushels of chaff, value 1s., and 1 sack, value 1s., the goods of Charles Batley.
CHARLES BATLEY. I job horses for the coal merchants. I have a stable at Mr. Beetham's wharf, near the canal at Hoxton. I missed some corn and this sack, which was in the granary about a fortnight at three weeks before the officer took the prisoner, I missed five quarters of corn in the space of a fortnight.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Was it not mixed with peas? A. I do not know.
CHARLES MIELL. I am an officer. On the 3d of August, about half-past two o'clock in the morning, I was coming over Kingsland-road bridge, and saw the prisoner with another man coming out of the prosecutor's stable - the prisoners had each a sack; the third man opened a door to see if any body was coming - he did not see me; he said something to the prisoners - they came into the road, and went into a stable belonging to Brace. I followed, and saw them throw down the loads in the stable. I pushed the door open, went in, and there saw Brace, who had been holding the door, so that we should not come in - he keeps a horse and cart. I asked him what they had got - he said a bit of corn and chaff for the horse - we took them in charge; another patrol went after the third man, but he was gone. I went and took Batley's horse-keeper, but he was discharged - here are the two sacks which I found in Brace's stable.
Cross-examined. Q. Where were you when you saw them? A. By the wall, about thirty yards from the stable. I could see the door by the light of a fire from a coak burner. I saw them within two yards of the stable door; the third man was in the stable.
JOHN STEVENSON. I was with Miell. I went and took the prisoners in Brace's stable.
Cross-examined. Q. Were they not discharged on bail? A. Yes.
CHARLES BATLEY. I have too many oats to miss any; both these sacks were in my stable, and one is my own. I have no doubt that the oats and chaff are mine - there is none there but what is mine.
OSBORN's Defence. I met Brace on the road with two sacks - he told me to carry one which I did.
BRACE's Defence. I bought the sack of chaff the overnight.
ROBERT EDWARDS. I am a carman. I occasionally sell corn and chaff - I live in Kingsland-road. I sold Brace a sack of chaff - I cannot tell the day of the month, but it was the night before he was taken - I really believe this to be the same chaff. I sold him about five bushels.
JOHN SMITH. I lent Brace a sack five or six days before he was taken - this is it. I know it by the corner being tied, which I did - the name of Love is on it.
OSBORN - GUILTY. Aged 35.
BRACE - GUILTY. Aged 55.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-285]
1871. JOHN PROTHEROE and WILLIAM JONES were indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, 1 fixture, i. e., 1 stove, value 5s., belonging to John Davis, and fixed to a certain building of his.
SECOND COUNT, calling it the property of Samuel Wilson and William Blackmere Noble.
GEORGE WEST. I live with Mr. Franks, of Cumberland-street. At nine o'clock in the evening, on the 28th of August, I was going to master's stables, and heard a noise in Mr. Davis' stable; I opened the door, and saw the prisoners there, bringing a stove down stairs, and there appeared to have been one taken from the room over the coach-house. I called the watchman, who took them - Protheroe said a man had sent him for it, but he could not tell me his name; he said he was to meet him in Bond-street.
WILLIAM SOMERFIELD. I was fetched to a stable in Cumberland-mews, and took the prisoners; the stove was close by them. I found on Protheroe a turn-screw and three keys; and with the stove was a large poker. I had been in the room the morning before, and saw a stove fixed there; I went to the room next morning, and found a porter's knot, which Protheroe said he took to carry it on.
GEORGE DUNN. I am a watchman. I assisted in taking the prisoners; I said it was a bad job; Jones said, "Yes, it is, it cannot be helped now;" he then said he was employed by Protheroe to take it to the corner of Bond-street, and was to have 2s. for carrying it.
JOHN DAVIS. This was my coach-house, but at that time it was in possession of Samuel Wilson and William Blackmere Noble, my assignees - the stove was fixed there.
PROTHEROE's Defence. I met a man who asked me to fetch the stove from the stable. I found it at the top of the stairs; we moved a coach which stood in the yard. I got this man to help me, being unwell.
JONES' Defence. This man asked me to help him.
PROTHEROE - GUILTY. Aged 19.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-286]
1872. JOHN STOKES was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 pair of shoes, value 4s., the goods of Henry Mander.
HENRY MANDER. I am an ostler at the White Hart public-house, Hampton-wick. On the 20th of August, when I got up, my shoes were gone; they were safe before, on the stairs - the prisoner had lodged there about ten days.
CHARLES MANDER. On the 20th of August I found my brother's shoes on the prisoner, as he was walking up Hampton-court; he said he was sorry for it, but he was tipsy.
Prisoner. I only took them while I got my own mended - I told him I had a good mind to turn thief, as his shoes were better than mine.
HENRY MANDER. He did say so, but his own were not gone to be mended.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Confined One Month.

Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1874. THOMAS EARLE and JOHN WOODMAN were indicted for stealing, on the 23d of July, 400lbs. weight of tarpauling, value 7s., the goods of James Rustat Trimmer.
JAMES HANCH. I am in the employ of James Rustat Trimmer, who is a brick-master, and lives at Isleworth; there was a large quantity of tarpauling lost on the 23d of July. I saw it safe at nine o'clock on the evening before, when I left work, and the next morning it was gone. I saw it again, and knew it - I know the prisoners, they live at Brentford, but they were not employed on the work there. I never saw them with it.
RICHARD BROWN. I was in Mr. Trimmer's employ. I went to work on the morning of the 23d of July. I missed the article, and went to Mr. Smith's in Old Brentford, where I saw it.
MICHAEL HALL. I am in the employ of Mr. Trimmer. I know this tarpauling to be his property - Smith has shut up his shop, and left Brentford.
JESSE FULLER. I took up the two prisoners on the Saturday after the robbery; they were together, looking into an oyster shop at Brentford. I told them Smith said he bought this tarpauling of them - they said they found it in a field about a mile from the place - the tarpauling was given up to Mr. Trimmer. Smith is gone away.
WOODMAN's Defence. I went out water-cressing - I found this tarpauling in a field - I took it into the town, and sold it there for 4s.; this young man was with me.
EARLE - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
WOODMAN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-289]
1875. LUCY PRENELLA was indicted for stealing. on the 16th of August, 1 pair of sheets, value 3s., the goods of George Wood.
SOPHIA WOOD. I am the wife of George Wood. On the 16th of August I lost a pair of sheets which I had let with a room, to the prisoner three days before - she came as a married woman. I wished to see her husband; she brought a man the first night, who said he was her husband; several men came there, which we did not like, and told her to go, but on missing the sheet, gave her in charge.
WILLIAM HARRISON. I am a pawnbroker. I have two sheets pawned by the prisoner on the 16th and 17th of August, in her own name.
Prisoner's Defence. I meant to get them out.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined One Month.

1876. CATHERINE BLISSET was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of August, 3 lamp pullies, value 6s., the goods of Richard Adams.
CHARLES ADAMS. I am the brother of Richard Adams. of Broad-street, St. Giles. On the 16th of August, about eight o'clock, his wife missed three lamp pullies; and in a quarter of an hour the prisoner brought them to my own house, where I keep a small shop to sell them, and I detained her.
SARAH ADAMS. I am the wife of Richard Adams. I was at breakfast, and saw the prisoner standing at the door - I thought I saw her put something into her apron; she asked the price of a candlestick; and when she was gone I missed the pullies, I went to my brother-in-law's shop, among others, and desired whoever offered them for sale should be stopped.
SAMUEL FURZMAN. I took her in charge, with the pullies.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-291]
1877. WILLIAM FILDER was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 1 coat, value 7s., the goods of George Heath.
GEORGE HEATH. I am a hackney-coachman, and live in Circus-street, New-road. I left this coat in a person's stable for a fortnight, some months ago, and it was gone when I went for it. I saw the prisoner with it on his back on the day Mr. Canning was buried.
JOSEPH CADBY. I am a constable, and took him in charge; he said he bought the coat in Monmouth-street for 7s., and took us to the house; the man said he had bought a fustian coat there, but he knew nothing of this coat; the prisoner persisted in it, and brought two women to the office to prove that he did buy it there.
MOSS EMANUEL. I live in Monmouth-street. I think I have seen the prisoner, but am not sure. I never sold this coat; the buttons are worth more than 7s.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-292]
1878. WILLIAM HUNTER and BENJAMIN GRAY were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 sheet, value 4s., the goods of Cornelius Hogan.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to belong to Mary Desmond.
MARY DESMOND. I am servant to Cornelius Hogan, of Queen-street, Seven-dials; the prisoners came to lodgethere on the 8th of September, about half-past eight o'clock at night - I knew them before; Gray went out next morning at a quarter past seven, and Hunter about three-quarters of an hour after. I missed the upper sheet from their bed, and have not seen it since.
FREDERICK INNES. I lodged in the same room; I saw Gray get up - he asked Hunter where the bag was: he replied, under the bed; he took it out, drew something white from under the bed, and put it into it; he went down stairs. I saw Hunter in Covent-garden afterwards, and told him he was wanted; he said he could not come - he ran away - I pursued, and took him.
PHILIP RILEY. I am an officer, and received Hunter in charge; he said he had sold the sheet - he took me to a marine-store shop, and said he had sold it there, but they positively denied it.
HUNTER's Defence. I know nothing about it.
HUNTER - GUILTY. Aged 17.
GRAY - GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-293]
1879. * THOMAS was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, 2 shoes, value 6s., the goods of James Darvill.
JAMES DARVILL. I am a shoemaker, and live at Uxbridge. On Wednesday morning, the 29th of August, I missed two shoes off my counter - I had seen them on the 27th; the witness Thomas brought them to me.
RICHARD THOMAS. I am a tailor, and live at Uxbridge. On the 29th of August the prisoner came into my shop, and was in the act of taking a very large parcel off the counter - he came to sell matches. I saw these shoes sticking out of his pocket; I took them from him - he said they were his - I gave him in charge.
JOHN TAYLOR. I am a constable, and took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner being a native of Otaheite, had the evidence communicated to him by an interpreter, through whom he pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
* The prisoner had no other name.

1880. WILLIAM ROGERS and WILLIAM PITT were indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 12 chisels, value 10s., the goods of Frederick Hughes; 15 chisels, value 8s., the goods of George Forbes; 1l chisels, value 4s., the goods of Richard Flatt; and 7 chisels, value 4s., the goods of William Blackwell .
FREDERICK HUGHES. I am a stone-mason. On the 13th of September I was working in Five Fields, Chelsea. I left my tools, on Wednesday night, in a work-shop in the fields; they were gone on Thursday morning - I lost twelve chisels.
GEORGE FORBES. I was at work at the same place, and lost eleven chisels from the mason's shop.
RICHARD FLATT, I lost my chisels from this workshop.
WILLIAM BLACKWELL. I lost my chisels from the same shop - the prisoners are strangers.
JOHN BOSTON. I am an officer. On Thursday morning, at half-past nine o'clock, I stopped the prisoners, and found a quantity of chisels on each of them - they were taken to Guildhall; and there, they said, they got them from Five Fields, Chelsea.
ROGERS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
PITT - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.

1882. JAMES SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September. 1 wooden till, value 2s.; 12 penny-pieces, and 24 halfpence, the property of Robert Chapman.
ROBERT CHAPMAN. I keep a public-house in Belton-street, Long-acre. On the 8th of September, while I was out, my till was stolen from the bar; when I came home, the prisoner came in, and called for half-a-pint of porter - my wife charged him with it - he denied it.
SOPHIA LYALL. I live opposite Chapman. I saw the prisoner and another go into the house and bring out a box, as I thought; it was between twelve and one o'clock - the other went in, and brought it out to the prisoner, who took it, and they went off together - I told a man, who went into the house and told the woman in the bar - I had seen the prisoner before, and believe him to be the man.
SUSANNAH HADLEY. I am in Chapman's employ. I saw the prisoner and another at the door; about one o'clock I left the bar to get some water, and as I came back along the passage, I heard a noise - I went into the bar, and saw Lyall; I then missed the till.
JOHN TURNER. I apprehended the prisoner a little after two o'clock - he promised to pay the money by instalments.
Prisoner's Defence. I was at the door, a and man ran out, by me; I had some pears in my apron.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
