1884. MARGARET BUTLER and MARGARET McCORMICK were indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 pair of half-boots, value 2s.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 1 knife, value 1d.; 1 shilling, and 1 sixpence, the property of Joseph Washington, from his person.
JOSEPH WASHINGTON. I am a waterman, and live in Charles-square, St. George in the East. On the 31st of July, at five o'clock in the morning, I went into the White Hart, public-house, nobody was in the room - I had been uptwo nights, and fell asleep in the room, and when I awoke, my handkerchief was taken off my neck, the boots off my feet, and my money and knife, from my pocket - I know nothing of the prisoners.
Prisoner BUTLER. I had been with him all night. Witness. I had not not been with her at all.
THOMAS GEORGE SIZER. I am a pawnbroker, and have a pair of boots and a handkerchief, which were pawned by McCormick, on the 31st of July, about one o'clock - Butler was at the door at the time.
JOHN MIDDLEDITCH. I am headborough of Shadwell. I went into the White Hart about one o'clock or later, and the prosecutor charged the prisoners, who were there, with robbing him; he said that Mitchell had told him nobody but them had been in the room, while he was asleep; they resisted being searched - this duplicate fell from Butler; McCormick took it up, and put it into her mouth.
MARGARET MITCHELL. I saw the prosecutor at the White Hart on the the morning of the 31st of July, asleep; the two prisoners were there - I had not seen them with him before he went to sleep. I saw them go out, and directly missed the boots off his feet.
Prisoner McCORMICK. Q. Did not he come in with us, and you bring us a quartern of gin? A. No; I never saw them come in.
BUTLER's Defence. He met me and another woman at eleven o'clock at night, and kept me with him all night, and promised me 2s., which he would not give me - he took me and the other prisoner to the White Hart in the morning, and gave us some gin, and then he would not pay for it - he told me to pawn his boots, and said in an hour he should have money to get them out - we went back to the house, and he gave us in charge.
McCORMICK's Defence. I met them, and he took us to the White Hart.
JOSEPH WASHINGTON. The constable said they would very likely come to the house again, which they did, and were taken. I swear I had not been with either of them.
BUTLER - GUILTY. Aged 28.
McCORMICK - GUILTY Aged 19.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-299]
1885. JAMES CAIN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 1 brooch, value 3s.; 2 half-crowns, and 5 shillings, the property of John Williams, from his person.
SAMUEL FURZMAN. I am an officer. On the 7th of August the prisoner and Williams were locked up in the watch-house together, and in the morning John Williams complained to me that the prisoner had robbed him of a brooch and 16s.; he denied having a farthing about him, or any thing else, but I found both the money and brooch on him.
Prisoner. It was my own money, which I received from my master. Witness. No; he gave all his money to his sister the night before, and she came that morning, and said to him, "How can you say this is your money, when you know you gave it to me last night." I found 12s. and the brooch at the bottom of the leg of his trousers, which Williams claimed as his, and described the brooch before he saw it.
Prisoner's Defence. I had occasion to get up, and saw the brooch on the floor.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-300]

MARY HATTON. I was at Islington work-house last Saturday, and saw the prisoner with this blanket pinned round her.
THOMAS COPE. Hatton gave me information - I took the blanket from the prisoner.
JOSEPH BIRCH. I saw the blanket found on her.

Transported for Seven Years.

1888. ROBERT WILKINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of July, 5 handkerchiefs, value 10s., the goods of John Jay.
JOHN JAY. I am a linen-draper, and live in Tysoe-street, Clerkenwell. I saw the prisoner take five handkerchiefs off a line in my shop - he ran out, and threw them down; I collared him immediately, and took him to the watch-house.(Property produced and sworn to.)
THOMAS GARNER. I took him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. Two men passed me - I had just crossed the road.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270913-303]
1889. WILLIAM TOOK was indicted for embezzlement.
SARAH SHACKLE. I am the wife of John Shackle, who keeps the Shakspeare's Head public-house, Percival-street. The prisoner was our pot-boy - I keep the accounts; he should always bring what money he received to me.
MARY HARRIS. I live in Skinner-street. On the 1st of September I paid the prisoner 33/4d. about nine o'clock at night, for beer which I had of Shackle.
JANE DRAKE. I deal with Shackle. On the 2d of September I paid the prisoner 10d.
MRS. SHACKLE. He told me these persons had not paid, and I entered it in the book - I never received it.
JAMES DAVIS. I apprehended the prisoner - he said there was sufficient clothes in his box to pay his master.
Prisoner. I was led into it by bad company.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-304]
1890. JOSEPH BLACKETT was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of September, 1 half-crown, 1 sixpence, and 4 shillings, the monies of James Shedick, from his person.
JAMES SHEDICK. I am a machine-maker. Last Sunday morning I was at the Ship public-house Shoreditch, about eight o'clock in the morning; I laid my head on the table, and was half a sleep. I awoke, and felt the prisoner's hand coming out of my pocket; I seized it, with my money in it; he had taken a half-crown, four shillings, and a sixpence - I tried to get it from him, but could not. I told shelandlord, who told me to go next door and get an officer; I did so - the prisoner then got away, over the road; I had not been above ten minutes in the house - he was sitting close by me all the time; there was nobody between us.
JAMES WACKETT. I was at the Ship, and saw the prisoner sitting one off from the prosecutor - there was a person between them; Shedick was asleep - he rose up, and said, "I have lost 7s.;" he laid hold of the prisoner, and said he had got it.
Q. Did you tell the Magistrate there was another man between them? A. Yes.
JOSEPH ADAMS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in charge, in Lamb-street, Spitalfields; I found 3s. 6d. on him, and 6d. in copper; Shedick appeared sober, and said he found the prisoner's hand in his pocket - the prisoner said nothing to it.
JAMES SHEDICK. When I awoke I saw the prisoner's hand coming out of my pocket.
Prisoner's Defence. There was another man between us- I received that 4s. for my work.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-305]
1891. HENRY SCORNELL, GEORGE LILLEY, and JOHN STEVENS were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of September, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
JOHN GROOM. On the 3d of September, about nine o'clock at night, I was going along Queen-street, with my wife, and saw Scornell put his hand into a gentleman's pocket several times, and at last he got a handkerchief out; he put it under his coat, and then gave it to Stevens; I ran and collared Stevens - he dropped it; my partner took the others. Lilley and Stevens were close behind, covering him, when he took it; we had a scuffle with them, and could not find the gentleman; Lilley must have seen him pass it to Stevens.
WILLIAM WHITTINGHAM. I was in Queen-street, and saw the three prisoners following the gentleman; I crossed over, and seized Scornell and Lilley, who were running.
SCORNELL's Defence. The handkerchief was laying down - I was going to take it up, but Stevens did.
STEVENS' Defence. I picked it up.
Stevens received a good character.
SCORNELL - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Whipped and Discharged.
LILLEY - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months.
STEVENS - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-306]
1892. CATHERINE FITZGERALD was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of September, 1 half sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 10 shillings, and 10 sixpences, the monies of Thomas Goff, from his person.
THOMAS GOFF. I am a carpenter. On the 1st of September, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night, I met the prisoner in the street, and went to her room in Maynard-street, St. Giles'; I counted my money before I went to bed - I had one half sovereign, two half-crowns, and the rest in shillings and sixpences; I put it into my trouser's pocket, which I put under my pillow; I awoke about six o'clock in the morning - she was gone, and the money also - I rapped at the door, which was fastened with a padlock - I could make nobody hear - I got out at the back window, and waited there till about twelve o'clock; she then came home; I followed her up stairs, and accused her of it; she came down, and gathered about one hundred women, who covered me with mud.
Prisoner's Defence. I have known him ten years; he went home with me; he gave me 1s., and I went away.
THOMAS GOFF. I never knew her before; the door was locked inside when I went to bed.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Of stealing, but not from the person.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-307]
1893. MARY SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of September, 5 shillings, the monies of James Donohue, from his person.
JAMES DONOHUE. On the 16th of September, rather after ten at o'clock at night, I was at a public-house in Catherine-wheel-alley - I was rather drunk; the prisoner and some men were there; I intended to call for a pint of beer; one of the men asked if I objected to make it a pot; I said No, and gave a 6d. - I had no change back; I had 5s. in my hand; I laid my hands on the table, and laid my head down, but did not go to sleep; the prisoner was at my left side; I felt her hand coming to mine; she made a grasp and got my money; I asked her for it; she got up and ran out of the house; I followed her into a house; she ran up stairs; I went up into the room with two watchmen, and found the 5s. in her hand.
GEORGE DUNWORTH. I was at the public-house, and saw the prisoner sitting on the prosecutor's left side; he got up, and pursued the prisoner.
GEORGE HUBBARD. I heard a cry of Murder! and went to No. 27, Rose-lane, where I found the prisoner and prosecutor - I found five shillings and a purse in her bosom.
JOHN POWELL. I am a watchman. I found five shillings and a purse in the prisoner's bosom - one shilling was under her left arm, and four shillings under her right arm; the prosecutor stated what he had lost before I found it.
Prisoner's Defence. I met him, and went to two houses with him; I took 1s. out of my purse to pay for some beer - he said it was his money; he then said, "Go on, and I'll forgive you;" he followed me home, and would have my money - I called Murder!
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-308]
1894. SUSANNAH SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 sovereign, the money of Thomas Kimber, from his person.
THOMAS KIMBER. I am a commission agent, and live in Edgware-road; the prisoner was five or six weeks in my service. On the 14th of September in the morning, she came and asked for money to get tea and sugar for breakfast; I gave her 2s.; I then put my purse on the table; I breakfasted and dressed myself; I went to Oxford-street to pay 5l., and when I got there I missed my sovereign; I went home and told her I had lost a sovereign; she said, "I am sorry for that, for my aunt is going to make me a present of a new gown to-day, and, perhaps, you will suspect I had it;" I told the people of the houseto watch her - I am sure I put the sovereign on my table with the other money.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I am a linen-draper. The prisoner bought a gown of me on the 14th of September, and paid a sovereign for it.
JOHN SMITH. I took her in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-309]
1895. WILLIAM SMITH, alias SAMUEL ASHFORD , was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 7 dwts. and 17 grs. of gold, value 20s., the goods of Francis Carpentier, his master.
FRANCIS CARPENTIER. I am a goldsmith, and live in Clerkenwell - the prisoner was in my employ. On the 13th of September he came to dinner as usual, but did not return to work - I found him in custody on Saturday. Mr. Simons produced some gold, which I compared with part of a diamond ring - they appeared to be the same; here is a piece which corresponds with it.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. Was this standard gold? A. No, but it is jeweller's gold.
JOHN JAMES SIMONS. I am a jeweller, and live on Clerkenwell-green - I saw the prisoner at my house on Thursday afternoon - he put these pieces of gold into the scale, and asked me to buy them. I asked where he got them; he said his mother gave it to him, that his name was William Smith, of Barbican-court, and that he worked at a rule makers; I said it had come from a goldsmith's, and if he did not tell me who his master was, I would give him in charge; he said he worked for no master - I then took him to the officer.
JAMES WALBANCK. I am in the prosecutor's employ, and know this gold - I gave the prisoner part of it to work.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I took him in charge.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18270913-310]
1896. GOODMAN SAUNDERS and JOSHUA LYONS were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of July, 1 watch, value 3l.; 1 chain, value 1l., and 2 keys, value 5s., the goods of George Russell, Esq., from his person.
GEORGE RUSSELL. ESQ. On the 11th of July I was in Hyde-street, near Manchester-square, and lost my watch from my fob - I did not see it taken; the two prisoners came up to me in company; and one of them held up some lemons to sell, and at that time I missed my watch and key they were gone in a minute. I looked for it on the ground, but it was not there - Saunders held the lemons up almost close to my eyes - I had seen Lyons with him.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Is your sight not defective, Sir? A. Yes; I missed it directly they were gone; nobody else had come against me - it was in my waistcoat pocket - it was safe a few minutes before - nobody came near me but them. I advertised it as lost, and offered 15l. reward for it.
JOHN FORRESTER. I am a City officer. I received information of this, and took up Saunders.
JOSEPH TAYLOR. I am a City officer. I received information, and apprehended Lyons, who I know goes about with lemons and oranges.
ABRAHAM COHEN. I am a confectioner, and live in Petticoat-lane. On the 11th of July, about seven o'clock I saw Lyons give a watch into the hands of Henry Levy; he looked at it, went into his house, and returned with some sovereigns, which he gave into the hands of Saunders - they then all three went into a public-house, at the corner of Arrow-alley, Petticoat-lane. I heard some silver ringing - I went to Taylor's house, but he was out; and at night I saw him and gave him information.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you known Taylor long? A. Yes; I knew he was at Aldgate watch-house - I was never locked up there; I have sometimes been out late, and Taylor let me in there; that is not once in a year - I do not go by the name of Tyo; they call my brother so. I work for my brother - I knew the prisoners by sight - I knew nothing of a reward till afterwards; I expect nothing - I do it for the good of my country - Levy lives at No. 1, Petticoat-square.
SAUNDERS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
LYONS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-311]
1897. JAMES WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 umbrella, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Hartley.
THOMAS HARTLEY. I mend umbrellas in the street. On the 14th of September I was in a skittle-ground near Frederick-street, St. Pancras - the prisoner was also there - he went away about eight o'clock, and soon afterwards I missed the best umbrella out of my bundle. I followed the prisoner and took him, and found the duplicate of it on him.
DANIEL SALKELD. I was in the skittle-ground, and saw the prisoner take the umbrella from the bundle - I thought he was an acquaintance, but as soon as he got out he ran away with it - we afterwards pursued, and found him with the ticket; the pawnbroker is not here.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Fourteen Days.

1899. JOHN SMITH was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of James King, on the 20th of July, and stealing 3 planes, value 7s., and 1 oil stone, value 2s., the goods of John Kerr.
JOHN KERR. I am a carpenter. I was working at Mr. James King's, shop, at Walham-green; he was my master. On the 20th of July, at seven o'clock at night, I left my planes there; when I returned in the morning they were gone - the shop is under the same roof as the dwelling-house; a pane of glass was broken to get them off the bench; a hand could reach them - the glass laid inside.
JOHN WARREN. I saw the prisoner about half-past one o'clock in the morning, about one hundred yards fromMr. King's house. I said, "What have you got?" he said"Cannot you see." I then said, "Where did you get these?" he said "You must find that out?" he had these tools.
GEORGE HUNTLEY. I was with Warren - his evidence is correct.
JAMES KING. I was called up, and found the prisoner in custody. I do not know whether my window was broken before: the gates weretopen.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The gates of the premises were open - I thought I would get under shelter. I saw the pane broken, and, being destitute, - I certainly took them.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Of stealing only. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-314]
1900. JAMES LANGHAM was indicted for feloniously assaulting William Cormack, on the 22d of July, at St. James, Westminster, putting him in fear, and taking from his person, and against his will, 1 watch value 5l.; 1 seal, value 10s.; 1 key, value 7s., and 1 chain, value 3s., his property.
WILLIAM CORMACK. I am a coach-painter. I was with my wife on the 22d of July, at the Hampshire Hog, public-house, at the corner of Kent-court, Betwick-street, between ten and eleven o'clock at night; we had been there about a quarter of an hour - I went in with my wife and a friend - I was quite sober, and had my watch safe. I saw the prisoner there when I went in - I knew him before - I had no conversation with him; two other men were drinking there with him - the prisoner kicked up a row with my friend. I prevented their fighting, and he gave me a violent blow on the head, got me against the wall, got his head in my stomach, and at the same time drew my watch from me - I could not make resistance - I was down in the corner; my wife went to the door to call Watch!
Q. Was he ill-using you at that time? A. Yes - he gave me a violent blow, in fact we were fighting - he went away. I have not found my watch - the officer did not know of it for about nine days.
Prisoner. We were drinking there - he came and struck me first. Witness. I did not, he was quarrelling with Gillagan.
ELIZABETH CORMACK. I am the prosecutor's wife - I was with him at the Hampshire Hog. I saw the prisoner knock my husband down. I went to the door to call Watch! and Murder! I saw the prisoner come out with the watch and chain - I could swear to the chain. I begged of him not to rob me; he gave me a push against the wall, and ran off - I cannot exactly say which way, as I was confused with the blow.
Prisoner. Q. As I was going out, the watchman caught hold of me? A. I cannot say whether he had him then, as I was so much hurt with the blow.
EDWARD GILLAGAN. I was at the Hampshire Hog, and saw the prosecutor and his wife there. I saw Cormack had his watch in his fob when he came in - I saw the chain and seals. I did not see the prisoner take it.
THOMAS GOOK. I am an officer. I received a description of the prisoner, and took him twelve days after the robbery, on the Sunday fortnight. I told him it was for a piece of work in a public-house, and then said it was for robbing a man of his watch; he said he had not been in the house at all - I took him to the watch-house. I put him with a number of other prisoners, and took the prosecutor there - he clapped his hand on his shoulder, and said "This is the man;" and wanted to know where the property was - the prisoner said, "If I tell you, you will prosecute me all the same;" he then said, if I went to his father's, I should hear of the watch - I went there, but his father denied it; a man was charged with being with him, but there was nothing against him. The prosecutor told me of the robbery on the same night, but I could not find the prisoner.
HENRY HOWLETT. I am a watchman. I was in Berwick-street, and was called; the prisoner was coming out at the door. I seized him, and asked if he was the man; the landlord came up to me, and said "Come and clear the house." I let him go to clear the house - the watch had not been named at that time. I saw Mrs. Cormack, but she did not tell me of it till I had let the prisoner go. I then cleared the house.
ELIZABETH CORMACK. I was so confused with the blow, I could not mention the watch at first.
ELIZABETH WAITE. I am landlady of this house. I saw all these people there that evening. I heard a great riot, and wanted the watchman to come to restore quietness.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 19.
[trialAccount t18270913-315]
1901. ESTHER MURRELL was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 2 coats, value 4l.; 1 waistcoat, value 1l.; 1 shirt, value 2s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 1s.; and 1 snuff-box, value 4d., the goods of John Hammond, in his dwelling-house.
JOHN HAMMOND. I live in Old Burlington-mews, Regent-street, and am a groom; it is master's house - I live in it alone; it is provided for me. On the 13th of September, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night, I took the prisoner home to sleep with me - I awoke between two and three, and missed her and this property, which was safe overnight. I cannot swear to the prisoner being the woman - I gave her some gin; we went to several public-house - but she had her veil down. I did not swear to her before the Magistrate - I will not swear to her; I had no candle in my room.
JEREMIAH RANDALL. I am a watchman of St. James'. On the morning in question I stopped the prisoner in Regent-street, with a bundle containing these articles - she said she got them from a man she had been sleeping with; and was going to take them to her brother's - that he gave them to her instead of money; I found the prosecutor at Marlborough-street, in the morning.
JOHN VIRGO BUCKLAND. I received the bundle and the prisoner in charge, about two o'clock that morning.
JOHN HAMMOND. These are my property - I had the coat and waistcoat that night; I did not authorize her to take them.
Prisoner's Defence. If he is the man, he put the things on the table and told me to take them; he had given me a great deal of brandy.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Of stealing only. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-316]
1902. GEORGE BALLARD, WILLIAM CAIN, and MARY WILSON were indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Patrick Dunn, on the 1st of September, at St. Andrew, Holborn, and stealing, 1 watch, value 30s.; 10 sovereigns, 24 half-crowns, 60 shillings, 80 sixpences, 360 penny-pieces, and 240 halfpence, his property.
PATRICK DUNN. I rent the house, No. 40, Laystall-street, in the parish of St. Andrew, Holborn. I know Ballard and Cain; Cain's father lived at my house at the time of the robbery, and for some time before. On the 1st of September, at half-past six o'clock in the morning, I saw Ballard coming from my house (I was going towards the house) he stood, and looked up and down the street; then turned his head, and he spoke to somebody through the key-hole of my door - he looked up and down the street; then turned and spoke again - he did the same thing a third time; then Cain immediately came out, and they went away together - they turned into the next street, and I saw Cain give Ballard something but could not see what it was. I then turned into my house, found my street door open, and my shop door broken open. I went to my chest in the shop, where my property was kept; I found it wrenched open, and part of the lid off - I missed ten sovereigns, a silver watch, and 8l. in silver, from the chest. I had seen them all there on the 26th of August: I had the key of the chest - I saw the chest safe at five o'clock that morning, but did not open it. I directly went in pursuit, but did not overtake them. I took Ballard between one and two that day; I took Cain in Smithfield-market, on the 2d of September. I found my watch in pawn at King's, in Holborn; it was in the chest with the gold - I have found nothing else. I know nothing of Wilson. The officer fitted to the place an instrument belonging to Cain's father, and it corresponded with the marks; I fastened the street door when I went out that morning.
Cross-examined by McDOWELL. Q. What time did you return to your house after you lost sight of the prisoners? A. I returned to my house directly.
COURT. Q. At what time did you go to bed the night before? A. Between ten and eleven - I locked the door, and believe I was the last person up.
MICHAEL LYNCH. I am a labourer, and lodge at Dunn's. Between six and seven o'clock on this morning, I saw Ballard standing at the door; Cain came out - they joined together and walked together; they walked close, and Cain was showing something to him, but I could not see what it was; Dunn told me his box had been broken open. Before Cain came out, I saw Ballard peeping in at the door - I saw Cain go into a wine-vaults in Holborn, and saw Ballard counting some halfpence there, but I had no idea of their having this sort of property. I was looking to see if they had any wearing apparel - I afterwards saw Dunn and told him; we went to the wine vaults for them, but they were gone.
MARY DUNN. I am the wife of Patrick Dunn. On this morning, at six o'clock, I was going to market, and saw Cain standing inside the passage, against the shop door, looking at it. I stepped back for about ten minutes, and then sent my little boy to see if he was gone - he was then outside; I stopped about ten minutes longer, and then went out - Cain was then gone; when I got to the top of the street, I turned and saw Cain go into the house - Ballard was standing opposite. When I returned home, I found this chopper, which has been part of a sword, laying by the side of my husband's chest; I had seen Cain frequently chopping wood with it - I shut the door when I went out: my husband had gone out at half-past five o'clock. Cain returned and went into the passage when he saw me gone.
Prisoner CAIN. When our street-door is shut, we cannot get in without somebody coming down to open it. Witness. Yes; there is a lock which ties back in the daytime - I had left the door on the latch.
WILLIAM KING. I am a pawnbroker. On the 1st of September this watch was pawned by a woman - I believe it to be the female prisoner - it is worth 30s.
ANDREW LLOYD. I am a constable. I took Ballard on the 1st of September - I charged him with this business; he made no reply whatever - I only found a penny on him - I found this chopper, and a piece of brick by the side of the chest in Dunn's room; the marks on the chest, and on the shop-door exactly fit the chopper.
CHARLES VINCENT. I am an officer. On Sunday, the 2d of September, Dunn gave Cain in my charge; he said he knew nothing about it - I found on him two duplicates, one for a waistcoat, and one for a handkerchief.
PATRICK DUNN. This is my watch.
BALLARD'S Defence. I was never inside the house.
CAIN'S Defence. Ballard came every morning to call me, but was never inside the house.
BALLARD - GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 18.
CAIN - GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 16.
WILSON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-317]
1903. JOHN NORRIS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 watch, value 5l., the goods of David Daly, in his dwelling-house.
DAVID DALY. I live at the corner of Pell-street, New-road, St. George's. On the 14th of September I saw the prisoner come straight into my parlour, where I was, blowing the fire - I knew him before; he said I was up early; we talked a little; he then said, "There is a customer in the shop" - I went into the shop, and by the time I had cut half a loaf, the prisoner said, "Good morning," and went away - I went into the parlour, and missed the watch - I ran out, and at last saw him running down a lane - I pursued; a man tried to stop him; he turned back, and said, "Here is your watch," and gave it to me - I gave 5l. for it nineteen years ago.
JOHN VINCENT. I saw him secured.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Of stealing to the value of 99s. only.
Confined Two Months.

1904. WILLIAM PENNY and THOMAS SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 cruetstand, value 1s., and 5 cruet-glasses, value 1s., the goods of Edward Simmons.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a cordwainer. On the 20th of August, I saw the prisoners in company together, about four yards from Simmons' house; the castors which I had seen on the window before, were in Smith's hands - I went and told Simmons; he then pursued - I returned to my shop; in a few minutes I saw them brought back. I have some little knowledge of Smith.
EDWARD SIMMONS. I am a broker and bookseller, and live in Barbican. These castors were on the stallboard at my window - I received information from Thompson - I went after the prisoners, and got beyond them - a neighbour gave me some information - I then went to a receiving-house, where I found the prisoners, with the custors on the table.
JAMES VINT. I am an officer. I received the prisoners, and produce the castors.(Property produced and sworn to.)
PENNY - GUILTY. Aged 18.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy - Whipped and Discharged.

1906. THOMAS PARKER was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, 4 memorandum-books, value 1s. 6d.; 131/2 quires of note-paper, value 3s. 6d.; 21/2 reams of thick laid post paper, value 18s., and 20 copy books, value 9s. 6d., the goods of Henry Thomas Green.
JAMES NEWTON. I am a cork-cutter. On the night of the 29th of August, I saw the prisoner go into Mr. Green's house, in King-street, Snow-hill - he was very busy in taking the things out of the window, and putting them in a handkerchief on the ground; he came out, I followed him down Snow-hill; he heard me, dropped the bundle and ran - I pursued and took him in Fleet-market; a lad took up the paper.
HENRY THOMAS GREEN. My attention was drawn to this circumstance, and I missed this paper; it was all in my window, and no one could get it without coming into the shop.
NICHOLAS POWEL. I am an officer. I received this bundle of paper from Newton.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence (written). I went in for a sheet of paper, and knocked on the counter for a quarter of an hour; nobody came out, and outside of the door I found the bundle; I heard a cry of Stop thief! and threw it down, supposing it was stolen.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Two Months.

1907. JAMES EDWARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, 1 pair of boots, value 15s. 6d., the goods of John Alger.
ROBERT HOCKLEY. I am shopman to Mr. Alger - he keeps a boot and shoe warehouse in Ludgate-street. At seven o'clock in the evening of the 24th of August, I saw the prisoner come to the shop door, and take a pair of boots off the floor; he did not come in - I pursued, and came up with him near the Old Bailey - he dropped them.
JOHN ALGER. I am the owner of the shop - these are my boots.
JAMES SNOW. I took him in charge; he said he was almost famished with hunger: I found nothing on him.

1908. CORNELIUS McCARTY was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 3 handkerchiefs, value 12s., the goods of Thomas Hall.
ANN HALL. I am the wife of Thomas Hall, a linen-draper, of Bishopsgate-street. On the 25th of August, between nine and ten o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoner pulling these handkerchiefs off the line; several persons were in the shop; he put them under his jacket, and was walking out, when I caught hold of the end of them, and stopped him.
THOMAS HALL. I was at home. I had driven the prisoner away from the shop twice within half an hour; he appeared much intoxicated, but when he was taken five men could not handcuff him.
THOMAS SAPWELL. I am an officer. I had been watching the prisoner - he tried to get some other handkerchiefs, but could not; he then went into the shop, and took these, I secured him with difficulty.
Prisoner's Defence. I went in to buy two yellow handkerchiefs - Mr. Hall would not let me have them.
GUILTY. Aged 22.

1909. ANN PHILLIPS was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 46 pocket-books, value 20s., and 1 billcase, value 1s., the goods of Michael Aarons.
PETER MONTELL. I am a hair-dresser. On the 31st of July, in the morning, Mr. Anderson, of Finch-lane, Cornhill, sent me in pursuit of a woman in a black gown; I ran out, and overtook the prisoner at the top of Finch-lane. with this property in a bundle.
JOHN TAYLOR. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in charge. The Lord Mayor ordered me to return part of the property to the prosecutor; she said she was happy of being taken; that she meant to have destroyed herself that morning, and that she had been confined in St. Luke's.
MICHAEL AARONS. This is my property - I had left it at Mr. Anderson's, in Finch-lane; I sell the pocket-books about. I left it safe under his counter at six o'clock in the evening.
GUILTY. Aged 42.

1910. WALTER BURKE was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Dunning, from his person.
JOHN DUNNING. I am a tailor, and live in Cleveland-street, Fitzroy-square. On the 14th of August I was going up Cock-lane, Smithfield, about half-past eleven o'clock in the morning, a man came up, and pointed the prisoner and another out - I ran down Cow-lane; a man came round the corner, and took the prisoner with my handkerchief, which was in my pocket just before; I saw him running, but had not seen him near me; he was within twenty yards of me.
JESSEE PHILLIPS. I am a glass-dealer. I was in Hosier-lane, going to a glass-warehouse, and saw the prisoner running towards me; I laid hold of him - he threw this handkerchief from his pocket: I took it up, and gave it to the officer.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a mob running, and this man said I threw the handkerchief down - I had not seen it.
The prisoner received a good character.

1911. ANDREW RUGG, alias RUBY , and MARGARET COX, alias RUBY, alias THOMPSON , were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of August, 1 ring, value 2s., and 1 petticoat value 1s. 6d., the goods of Jemima Sarah Betts.
JEMIMA SARAH BETTS. I am single, and live with Mr. Rieg, in Middlesex-street, Aldgate - the prisoner Rugg lodged in that house, for two or three years - Cox came and lived with him as his wife, after he had been there a year; they both went by the name of Ruby there. On the 15th of August, 1826, I pulled off my ring while I washed my hands, and left it in the kitchen; after I had washed them, I returned and missed the ring off the table. I also lost a petticoat from the kitchen - I found the ring in pawn - Mrs. Ruby stood at the kitchen door, and saw me put the ring there - when I missed it I accused her of it; she said, if I accused her of it, she would knock me down; nobody else could have taken it.
JOHN DELBRIDGE. I am shopman to Mr. Cordell, pawnbroker, of Aldgate - I have a petticoat, which was pawned on the 15th of August, 1826, and a ring pawned on the 15th of September, 1826 - I know both the prisoners well, by their coming to the shop, but it being so long ago, I cannot say they brought these - they were both pawned by a woman, in the name of Ruby.
ANN MEAL. I lodge at this house - I knew this petticoat four years ago, when the prosecutrix's mother brought it.
JOSEPH BIRCH. I am an officer. I took the male prisoner on Saturday, the 28th of July, I found on him forty-seven duplicates; the female prisoner was within a few yards of him - she was taken; no duplicates were found on her - Rugg gave me twenty-two duplicates at first, and said they were all he had - he then gave me twenty-five more, one of which was for this ring, and one for the petticoat; the prisoners are not married.(Property produced and sworn to.)
RUGG'S Defence. They were pawned unknown to me.
RUGG - GUILTY. Aged 42.
Transported for Seven Years.
COX - GUILTY. Aged 40.
Judgment Respited .
There were four other indictments against the prisoner.
[trialAccount t18270913-326]
1912. JOHN DIXON and JOSEPH BARRY were indicted for stealing, on the 21st of July, 12 bushels of soot, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Badger.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS BADGER. I am a chimney sweep - Dixon was in my service. On the 23d of July I directed an officer to watch my premises; Dixon was taken into custody, and in about an hour and a half Barry was taken; I never gave Dixon leave to take or give away any sack of soot from my premises.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. You say you never gave Dixon leave to take away any soot? A. No, not by any means; he had been in the habit of conveying some from one of my premises to another for three months - but I had given him orders not to take any on the 23d of July. Barry was not in the habit of buying soot of me; I never knew of his having any soot till the 23d of July.
COURT. Q. Dixon never had any authority to let Barry have any? A. No, and he never received any of mine; Dixon had been employed by me to take it for me from Queen's-court to Coleman-street.
HENRY TUFF. I am a sweep, in the employ of Mr. Badger. I know the two prisoners - I have seen Barry at my master's cellar, and Dixon told me if I saw my master I was to say, "Master, do you want Jack?"
Cross-examined. Q. How much did you get? A. We had part of one sack between us; Dixon was in my master's service - I knew Barry before Dixon came; he used to come to another man who was there. I have been there three years - I first thought of telling my master of this on Sunday, when I came home from school. I do not know when they took it rightly - they had been at it about six weeks; I had no share of it till the last; Dixon told me he would beat me if I told; master did not say he knew all about it, and he would beat me if I did not tell him; I had a share of it.
JOHN GEORGE WILLIAMS. I am fellow-apprentice with the last witness. Barry never was in my master's employ - on Saturday, the 21st of July, I saw the prisoners in the cellar; Dixon filled a sack, and Barry took it away; then he came back with the sack empty; Dixon filled it again, and he took it away again - he came back in the afternoon about three o'clock, and then took another sack; he told me to cut the lumps down, and put the lightest of the soot in; Dixon gave me and Tuff 1s. between us - they promised us 1s. out of every three sacks.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you remember Barry coming there before Dixon was there? A. Yes, and Tuff knew it too; my master never lost any soot before Dixon came, to my knowledge. I did not tell my master of it till the Sunday, when we came from school, because they said they would beat us - we had only 1s.; Savage came and told our master, and then he asked us if we knew any thing of the robbery.
WILLIAM ALLWINKLE. I am an officer. The prosecutor set me to watch on the 23d of July - I saw Dixon come out of the cellar about eleven o'clock, with a sack on his back; I asked where he was going - he said to Green-court; Barry was out in London-wall, about one hundred yards from Mr. Badger's - they walked on to Quaker-street.
Cross-examined. Q. Were the other two little sweeps there? A. They were in the cellar - if they have said that Dixon and Barry were in the cellar, they have said what was not true.
PETER SAVAGE. I deal in soot. On Saturday, the 21st of July, Barry sold me four sacks of soot. I went and informed Badger. I know nothing of Dixon.
BARRY'S Defence. The soot I sold him I gathered myself. I have dealt with him for four years nearly.
DIXON's Defence. My master told me to go to the cellar and turn the soot over, which I did. I then asked if I should go to Green-court to take some soot, and he said Yes: I had taken one sack on my back to go there, and the officer came up and took me.
THOMAS BADGER. I had ordered him that day not to take any out.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-327]
1913. THOMAS DONNOVAN and EDWARD TOPPER were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, 1 pair of shoes, value 8s., the goods of David Reid.
CHARLES HERDSFIELD. On the 30th of July I was in Cheapside, and saw the two prisoners coming down Wood-street into Cheapside - Donnovan went into two Manchester warehouses; they then went into Trump-street, and Donnovan went into the prosecutor's - Topper walked backwards and forwards, close by the door; Donnovan came out with something under his coat, and rejoined Topper. I took them both, and found the shoes on Donnovan.
WILLIAM MARCHANT. I was with Herdsfield, and saw Donnovan come out of the shop. I went up, and he dropped the shoes at the time Herdsfield seized him.
DAVID REID. I keep this shop. I was up stairs, and knew nothing of it till the prisoners were brought back; I missed the shoes off a nail in the back part of my shop.(Property produced and sworn to.)
DONNOVAN'S Defence. I know nothing of the other prisoner; I only asked him the time; and went in to ask if they wanted a boy.
TOPPER'S Defence. I was not within ten yards of the place; he came up and asked what it was o'clock; we walked together - he went into two warehouses.
DONNONAN - GUILTY. Aged 16.
TOPPER - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.

1988. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of August, 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Joseph Sharpe, from his person.
JOSEPH SHARPE. I am a gentleman's servant. 1916. JOHN WARE was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of John Jones, from his person.
JOHN JONES. I am clerk to Mr. Goss. About nine o'clock in the morning I passed three lads just by the Fleet-prison, standing with their backs against the wall - they followed me till I got near the Old Bailey, on Ludgate-hill. I felt something touch my heel - I turned and saw the prisoner with my handkerchief inside his coat, under his arm - he ran across the street. I followed calling Stop thief! - he threw it down - I took it up, and he was taken- it was safe a minute before.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q. Did the others appear in his company? A. Yes - I believe they spoke together. I do not know what became of the others.
ALEXANDER JOHNSON. I am an officer, and took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner Defence. It was thrown down by me - I did not throw it.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Strongly recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.

1917. ELIZABETH KING and ANN LANE were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 basket, value 2s. 3d., and 3 pecks of apples, value 4s., the goods of Theophilus Salmon.
THEOPHILUS SALMON. I am a ticket-porter, and attend Fleet-market. On the 14th of August, at a quarter to five o'clock in the morning, I had five baskets of apples in my care. I left them a short time, while I unloaded something at the top of the market, and heard an alarm - I missed one basket. I looked up Ludgate-hill, and saw the prisoners carrying it away - it contained about three pecks; each had hold of a handle, and had got about one hundred yards. I overtook them at the corner of St. Martin's-court - they said they had paid 6s. 6d. for it, but it was not for sale.
JAMES FISHBURN. I am a watchman. I was at the market and saw the prisoners together, sitting on a basket, for about a quarter of an hour; I saw Lane take hold of this basket three times; then they both took it away - I gave an alarm.
GEORGE GOUGE. I am an officer, and received them in charge.(Basket produced and sworn to.)
LANE'S Defence. I had been out, and had been drinking.
LANE - GUILTY. Aged 29.
KING - GUILTY. Aged 29.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270913-332]
1918. CATHERINE JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 purse, value 6d.; 5 sovereigns; 1 half-sovereign; 2 half-crowns; 1 shilling and 2 sixpences, the property of William Brown Westcott, from his person.
WILLIAM BROWN WESTCOTT. I am an accountant, and live at Pentonville. On the 10th of August, about eleven o'clock, I was passing homeward from Fleet-street, up Fleet-market, I met the prisoner and two others - they pressed me to give them gin. I refused, but at last consented - I went into a house and gave them some - I had none myself. I came out and saw no more of them till I got about fifty yards, when the prisoner rushed by me, and took out my purse with five sovereigns and a half in it from my pocket - the silver was loose in my pocket; she ran away. I pursued her up the market calling Stop thief! and caught hold of her gown at the top of themarket - she turned round, and said "There is your purse," giving it to me, but it was empty; the watchman came up and took her. I said, "You had better give me the money, or you will be transported;" she said, "That is just what I wish."
JAMES TARR. I am a watchman. I received her in charge; the prosecutor had got hold of her; I took her in the watch-house - he said she had stolen five sovereigns and a half - I found two half-crowns, one shilling and two sixpences in her hand.
THOMAS GREEN. I am a constable. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house about half-past ten o'clock.
WILLIAM BROWN WESTCOTT. I could not find the sovereigns - we searched the market all over. I never lost sight of her. I gave charge of a man who was said to be acquainted with her.
Prisoner's Defence. I am innocent - a friend gave me the silver; he brought a woman to Guildhall to swear falsely against me, and promised her a new gown.
PROSECUTOR. It is false - a woman said, as I was going along, "Are you the gentleman who was robbed?" I said Yes - she said, "I saw that woman soliciting money from other people for gin," and I thought her evidence might be of use. GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-333]
SEVENTH DAY. THURSDAY, SEPTEMBER 20.
Third Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1919. GEORGE PATTERSON and JAMES WOODYER were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 35lbs. weight of lead, value 5s., the goods of the St. Katherine Dock Company , and fixed to a certain building.
WILLIAM WINTER. I am a rope-maker. On the 17th of August, about a quarter before nine o'clock in the morning, I saw Patterson take this lead off an empty house in Star-court, and put it in at the window. I did not see any other person.
MICHAEL HARRAGAN. I live at a public-house near St. Katherine's Docks. On the 17th of August I saw Patterson take the lead off the top of the house, No. 9, Star-court; he said to me, "Go round, and see if you can see any body;" I said, "No, I will go and see if I can see any body to take you;" I told Sitgrave.
WILLIAM SITGRAVE. The house this lead was taken from was in possession of the St. Katherine Dock Company. Harragan came and told me he saw the lead taken- I tried the front door, and could not get in - it was bolted - I ran round to the back door, and saw the two prisoners about fifteen yards from the back door. I secured them both; Patterson said I was no officer, and should not hold him; if I let him he would walk quietly; I let go, and he ran away - I followed, and took him; I went on to the house, and found this piece of lead doubled up in the parlour. I compared it with the top of the dormer - it fitted exactly; Woodyer was there, but did nothing.
PATTERSON'S Defence. I was not in the house at all - I was going to work; this man came and asked if I had seen the master come down.
PATTERSON - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
WOODYER - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-334]
1920. ANN RANDALL was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of September, 1 coat, value 12s.; 1 waistcoat, value 3s., and 1 pair of breeches, value 15s., the goods of William Halt.
SARAH HALT. I am the wife of William Halt; we live in Turnmill-street. On Saturday night, at eleven o'clock, I put his clothes in the passage; and missed them when he asked for them on Sunday - the prisoner visited one of the lodgers.
JANE BROWN. I keep a hair-dresser's shop. On Sunday morning, about seven o'clock, the prisoner came and asked if her husband had been to get shaved; I said I could not tell; she asked if she might leave a bundle till he came, as it was his clothes, which he wanted to take into the country; but if he was in liquor, I was not to give them to him - she put them on the bench; she afterwards came with the officer, and had them away.
CHARLES VINCENT. I am an officer. Halt sent for me- I took charge of the prisoner; she took me to Brown's, where I found the clothes; she said she took them out of a lark to bother them.
Prisoner. I only did it to frighten Mrs. Halt, as I thought it a wrong place for them.
SARAH HALT. I offered her 5s. to tell me about them, and she said she knew nothing about them.
GUILTY. Aged 31.
Confined One Month.

1921. ELIZABETH SELLIS was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of July, 1 blanket, value 7s., the goods of Henry Moriarty.
JANE MORIARTY. I am the wife of Henry Moriarty; we live in Tash-street. On the 26th of July, about half-past ten o'clock in the morning, I saw the prisoner in our passage - I saw her take this blanket off a line in the passage, where it hung to dry - she put it under her shawl - I was going to take hold of her, and said, "You have stolen a blanket;" she used a bad expression, and said she had not got it. I stood talking to her some time - she then dropped it, and ran away - she was quite a stranger.
MATHIAS WELHEN. I am an officer. I heard a cry of Stop thief! and saw the prisoner running - several people were following her. I secared her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw it till it was in her hand.
GUILTY. Aged 43.

[trialAccount t18270913-336]
1922. ANN (THE WIFE OF ISAAC) SOLOMONS was indicted for that she, on the 28th of August, at Christ-church, feloniously did receive, of a certain evil disposed person, 1 watch, value 6l., the goods of Joseph Ridley, which said goods had been lately before, (by the said evil disposed person.) feloniously stolen, she well knowing the same to have been stolen, against the statute.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
JOSEPH RIDLEY. I am master of a ship. On the 17th of August I was robbed of a watch - I do not know the person who stole it - I am sure it was not the prisoner.
Cross-examined by MR. BRODRICK. Q. Where did you lose it? A. I do not know - I lost other property at the same time - I had been drinking, and was off my guard.I was not perfectly sober. I lost it in Whitechapel - I had it safe at seven o'clock. I was in company with Mr. Watson, a respectable man. I live in Mile-end-road - I had taken brandy and water - I fell in company with a woman in Whitechapel after I left Mr. Watson, about ten o'clock, and went to a house with her. I am sure I did not give her my watch - I gave her what I had in my waistcoat pocket, it was about five or six shillings. I will not swear that I gave her any thing - she told me that I did. I did not give her the watch, I am certain.
Mr. ADOLPHUS. Q. Will you swear you did not give her your watch? A. Yes; I neither gave it, lent it, or sold it to any one - I gave information at Lambeth-street next morning.
COURT. Q. About what time did you leave Mr. Watson? A. About seven o'clock; I do not know whether my watch was safe then; I cannot say that I had it when I went into the woman's company - I missed it that night, and am almost positive that I spoke about it that night - I named it to the woman, as she observed, that if she had known I had lost it, she would not have invited me in.
JAMES LEA. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. I received this information on Monday, the 20th of August, and entered it in our robbery book. The prosecutor had been there on Saturday, but I did not see him.
WILLIAM BROWN EDWARDS. I am an officer of Hatton-garden. On the 29th of August I assisted in searching a house in Bell-alley, Petticoat-lane, Spitalfields - I believe it is No.12; the prisoner was there; one party secured her- I went up stairs, and did not hear her say any thing; she went up stairs, and saw me searching there - she was secured, with her son and servant, in the first floor; we went up to a bed-room on the second floor; there was a large four-post bedstead, which I removed, and on moving part of the flooring, about a foot and a half in size, I found this watch, with its face upwards - there was another watch and several other things there; Limbrick and the others were searching the first floor. It is a private house.
Cross-examined by MR. LAW. Q. Did you see her son there? A. I saw a young man about eighteen, and a person she called her servant; she was about twenty-one or twenty-two; I took them into custody; they were discharged by the Magistrate.
JOHN LIMBRICK. I am an officer of Hatton-garden. I was present at the search of this house; I saw the prisoner there - she made a great noise, and asked what we wanted- I told her we had a warrant to search for bad money, and we found some - she made no answer; she, her son, and servant were taken into custody; we then went up to the second floor; I drew the bedstead out from over a place we had received information of - Edwards or Mitchell took up the carpet, and took the boards up; I there saw this watch, with the face upwards. Mitchell said, "Whose bed-room is this?" she said, "This is mine." Edwards asked her if she knew any thing of the property - she said she did not.
ALEXANDER MITCHELL. I am a surveyor of the Thames Police. I went with the other officers; I got first into the house, and saw the prisoner in a little back parlour; I asked her name - she said Solomons; I asked if she owned the house - she said Yes; I said we had a warrant to search - she made a great noise, and the officers came in; I sent her up stairs: I remained below some time, and fastened the doors. I then went up, and saw Edwards take the watch out of the hole - I asked if it was her bed-room - she said it was, and that she slept there; we took her and the property away.
MR. BARRETT. I am clerk of the papers at Newgate. Isaac Solomons ceased to be in our custody on the 25th of May; there was no discharge lodged for him; he was taken to the Court of King's Bench, by a Habeas.
Part of an affidavit made by the prisoner, and upon which she had moved the postponement of her trial, was here read, to prove that she described herself as "The wife of Isaac Solomons, late of Bell-lane, Spitalfields, but now of North America, as she believed"
JOSEPH RIDLEY. This is the watch which was stolen from me, I am quite sure of it - I have had it fourteen years; the maker's name is Phillips, of Brighton; it was a hunting watch, and I had it altered.
Prisoner's Defence. I am quite innocent - I do not know any thing at all about it.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-337]
1923. ANN SOLOMONS was again indicted for a like offence, in receiving, on the 28th of August, 1 watch, value 5l., the goods of Robert Beauchamp, knowing it to have been stolen.
ROBERT BEAUCHAMP. I am a silversmith and pawnbroker, and live in High Holborn. On the 23d of December last a square of glass was cut out of my window - a watch and other property was stolen; I have not ascertained the quantity of the property stolen. I found a watch at Hatton-garden, which I knew - I had never parted with it.
BENJAMIN GRIFFIN. I am in Mr. Beauchamp's employ. I never sold this watch to any person.
HENRY HYDE. I was in Mr. Beanchamp's employ. I never parted with this watch.
THOMAS FOGG. I went with other officers to search the house. I came to a bureau or chest of drawers - the prisoner said, the whole of the property in those drawers belonged to her, and that she had packed it up to send to her husband; she opened the drawer, and Limbrick found this watch.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MR. BARRETT. The prisoner's husband was brought into custody in May.
NOT GUILTY.

1925. JOSHUA CROSS and GEORGE COLLINS were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September, 82lbs. of solder, value 40s., and 21lbs. of lead, value 3s. 6d., the goods of Our Lord the King .
SECOND COUNT, stating it to belong to the Honorable William Sturges Bourne, William Dakers Adams, Esq., and Henry Dawkins, Esq.
MR. BOLLAND conducted the prosecution.
JOHN BOSTON. I am a porter, and live in Tottenham-court-road. On the 7th of September, at a quarter to seven o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoners in company together, in Castle-street, Leicester-fields; they appeared bulky in front; I met Murray, we followed them into a plumber's shop in Gray's-inn-lane - I went in and seized Cross, and said, "I must have what you have got here;" I unbuttoned his breeches, and suspended to a strap, was this piece of lead; the buckle of the strap was fixed to the center brace - I opened the lead and found this solder inside- the lead was nailed round it; I asked where he got it, he said he found it near the Diorama, Regent's-park, and they were both out of employ.
BENJAMIN MURRAY. I joined Boston, and followed the prisoners into the shop. I found six bars of solder under Collins' clothes, nailed in a piece of lead - here is a hole in it which fastened on his braces in front; he said he found it in some rubbish in Regent's-park.
JOHN GROOM. I am a constable. On the 7th of September I saw the prisoners coming down Pall-mall, towards Cockspur-street; about twenty minutes to six o'clock; they had something bulky in front; but I could not stop them, as I was going on business.
WILLIAM ROLES. I am clerk of the works at Buckingham-palace; the prisoners have been employed there some time, as plumbers' labourers, they leave work about half-past five o'clock; there is such lead and solder as this at the Palace, it is new - they might have access to it with the assistance of the plumber's foreman, who absoconded on the following morning. Here is scratched on the lead, "Centre building," and on the solder, here is "North;" which I presume, means the north wing.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Who wrote that you cannot tell? A. No; the prisoners were employed there that day; the solder and lead is delivered to the foreman. I cannot swear it ever was at the Palace; nothing was allowed to be taken away.
THOMAS JOHNSON. I am a labourer in trust. I was at work on the centre building of Buckingham-place; the prisoners were labourers there - the men are all told they are to take nothing away. I saw Collins on the roof of the centre building at five o'clock - the foreman delivers out the solder.
CROSSY - GUILTY. Aged 28.
COLLINS - GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.

ANN FIELD was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of July, 1 blanket, value 2s.; 2 sheets, value 4s.; 2 bolsters, value 12s.; 1 tea-kettle, value 1s. 9d.; 1 saucepan, value 2s.; 1 pail, value 1s.; 1 fender, value 6d.; 20lbs. weight of feathers and small pieces of flannel, mixed together, value 10s.; 1 quilt, value 4s.; 1 window curtain, value 1s., and 1 set of bed curtains, value 3s., the goods of William Summer.
WILLIAM SUMNER. I am a printer, and live in Fenchurch-street. I let the prisoner a room in a furnished house, in Grub-street - I had suspicion; in consequence of information, I went and broke her room open, and missed the articles stated; I have found nothing but the flocks and feathers.
MARY EARL. On the 24th of July the prisoner come and asked me to take her as a lodger - I said I would in a few days, but I was going to make a bed for a woman, and was deficient in a pillow - she brought some flocks and feathers in her apron next day, and on the day after brought some more, and slept at my house. I delivered the same feathers to the officer.
JAMES PEACHY. I produce the flocks and feathers.

1930. JOHN CRAMER was indicted for embezzlement. MAURICE EVAN EVANS. I am a carpet dealer, and the prisoner was occasionally in my employ; he used to receive money, which he should have brought me immediately; he was in my employ on the 3d of September, but did not account to me for any money that day; when he went out, I gave him change to receive Mr. Well's account; he went out, and I did not see him again till the Friday night following; he came voluntarily; his wife had been to say he would come: I asked him for the money he had received for me; he said he had not got it, but if I would let him go, he would find it by twelve o'clock next day.
Prisoner. Q. What did you give me in the morning? A. The difference of 10l. - 3l. 9s. 4d.; I have no other complaint against him; he took my truck out; that was brought home by another person.
WILLIAM WELLS. On the 3d of Sepetmber the prisoner brought me some floor-cloth, and I paid him 6l. 10s. on his master's account, about four o'clock in the afternoon.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you think me ill? A. No; my wife said she thought so.
RICHARD SAUNDERSON. I am a constable, and took the prisoner in charge: he said he had got drunk, and lost the money - he is a watchman of Cripplegate Without.
The prisoner put in a written defence stating, that he had gone to a public-house, fallen asleep, and lost the money; and a friend having promised to lend him the money, he went to his master to tell him it should be paid.
- JONES. I have employed the prisoner as a porter- he received money for me from two gentlemen, and came back with only 91/2d. - he did not appear to me to be right in his mind.
NOT GUILTY.

1932. EDWARD WILLIAM HOWE was indicted for embezzlement.
WILLIAM PIGGOTT. I am the son of Thomas Piggott, a shower-bath maker, who lives near Fitzroy-square; the prisoner has been about seven months in our employ, and received money for us, which he ought to bring to me or my father immediately.
HENRY HANDCOCK. I live in Holborn, in the City. On the 10th of August I paid the prisoner, on account of Messrs. Wallis and Co., four sovereigns, four half-crowns, and three shillings; it was between eleven and twelve o'clock in the morning; he gave a receipt on the bill.
WILLIAM PIGGOTT. He was not told to receive this money, and never paid it us; he did not return; I found him next day in Princes-street, dressed in new clothes; I asked what he had done with our truck; he said he gave a boy 6d. to bring it home, but it had not come; he said he had lost the money; that he put it on the truck while he fastened his cords, and forgot it for some time, and when he looked for it, it was gone.
GUILTY. Aged 19.

1933. SARAH BAILEY was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, 1 bird-cage, value 2s. 6d., and 1 live tame parrot, price 5s., the property of Sarah Elizabeth Ansell.
SARAH ELIZABETH ANSELL. I am a widow, and live at Winchester-house, Old Broad-street; my parrot and cage were safe on the 30th of June, when I went to Canterbury; I returned on the 21st of July - it was then gone - here it is - I can swear to the cage and parrot.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Are you positive it is your cage? A. Yes; I know it by its appearance - [The witness tried to make the parrot repeat the words "Jacko, Pretty;" but would not.] - it has a defect in the right wing - it was moulting time; some large feathers are out of the right wing, and have never grown again.
THOMAS GOODING. I am an officer. On Tuesday night, the 14th of August, I found this parrot and cage hanging up in the prisoner's room, in Rose-lane, Spitalfields - I asked if they were hers; she said Yes, that she gave 14s. for it fourteen months ago of a man named Adams, in Rosemary-lane. I took her in charge.
MARY ADAMS. I was keeping house for Mrs. Ansell; the bird and cage were taken about ten minutes past eight o'clock in the morning, the 20th of July, off the dresser; I am certain of the bird and cage.
WILLIAM DICKINSON. I am an the officer. I was with Gooding and confirm his evidence.
MR. PHILLIPS called.
ANN SMITES. I am wife of Joseph Smites. I occasionally char for the prosecutor - I saw her buy this parrot about fourteen months ago in Rosemary-lane - it has hung publicly out of her window every day since.
COURT. Q. Did all the neighbourhood see it? A. She bought it of Adams, he had two others at the time; she bought the cage also of him - he asked 1l., and took 14s.
THOMAS GOODING. The parrot was advertised - Mrs. Ansell told me what it could repeat, and it did repeat it; and she described the defect in its wing before it was found. I am often in the prisoner's neighbourhood, and never saw it hanging out of her window; if I had seen one in that low neighbourhood I should have noticed it.
Before the case was concluded, the parrot repeated, the words"Jacko pretty."
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-347]
1934. MARY PINFOLD, alias ELIZABETH BELLAMY , was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, 1 watch, value 3l.; 1 chain, value 20s.; 1 seal, value 5s., and 2 keys, value 2s., the goods of George Dyson, from his person.
GEORGE DYSON, On the 20th of July, about nine o'clock at night, I was coming down Fleet-street, with Mr. Wilkinson, and had my watch in my pocket; I was not sober; I think we went into a public-house; I do not know whether any women came to me.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What did you have? A. A glass of ale; I was drunk. I cannot tell whether any women were there; I do not remember any thing about it.
JOHN WILKINSON. I was with Dyson, coming from Westiminster - he had his watch in Fleet-street; we went on, and in Smithfield, a female asked him to treat her with something to drink, which he did; we went into a housein Smithfield, and several females were there; the prisoner was one of them. In a short time he fell asleep in the house; I immediately sent for a coach; when it came I awoke him, and did the best I could to get him into it, and then I missed his watch, which I had seen safe in the house ten minutes before. I took him back into the house directly, and an alarm was given - the prisoner was not in the house then, but the other females were; the watchman went out, and in a very few minutes brought the prisoner in with the watch.
Cross-examined. Q. Were you quite sober? A. I do not mean to say that I was - I knew what I was about; I do not know where we had dined. We had left Westminster between seven and eight o'clock, and had not been wandering about - we were at a public-house, and had some ale after that; I do not know how much we drank - we might be in that house two hours; I swear we did not drink two quarts of ale; we had three glasses of gin and water at Westminster; the prosecutor was not very tipsy; he treated some people with drink in Smithfield; I do not think be drank himself there - I did not see all he did there; he might have given the watch to somebody - I did not see him take it out.
GEORGE BUSH. I am a watchman. I was in Smithfield, and saw a hackney-coach near the Bull and Butcher public-house, and a mob about it; I saw the prosecutor come out of the house. Wilkinson and the prisoner were with him - one had hold of each arm; the prosecutor was stooping, and all in a minute I saw the prisoner start round the coach and run across the street - Wilkinson said,"Where is your watch, Dyson?" - Dyson said, "I do'nt know." Hawes said something, and I pursued the prisoner; we found a person dressed very much like her, and took her first by mistake - we went down to the bottom of West-street, and took the prisoner in a court there; I said, "What was it you took from the man at the Bull and Butcher?" she pulled out the watch and gave it to me - I am certain she is the girl whom I saw close to Dyson. I saw her every night; I took her within ten minutes.
Cross-examined. Q. She had ten minutes to get away with it? A. Yes - Wilkinson was rather gone, but not so drunk as Dyson; he was unable to walk; I thought the other was the girl at first.
WILLIAM GLOVER. I am a watchman. I was in West-street. Bush came and asked if I had seen Pinfold - I said No - we went and found her in a court - I said I wanted her; she said, "I know what you want, it is here, take it, this is the watch."
JAMES HAWES. I keep the Bull and Butcher public-house. I saw the witnesses at my house - Dyson appeared in liquor, but not Mr. Wilkinson - I recollect seeing Mr. Dyson's seal hanging out, as he left the house; there were three girls with him when he first came in; they brought in two girls, and the prisoner was the third - I assisted him into the coach; there was a great mob round the door, and the girls were very officious - one carrying his hat, and the other helping him along - I did not see the prisoner there after that.
Cross-examined. Q. Mr. Wilkinson was rather sober by your account? A. Yes; he ordered brandy and water, and paid for it, and shared it with the girls.
EVAN JONES. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house, with the watch.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-348]
1935 JOSEPH VALENTINE was indicted for feloniously assaulting Charles Ireland, on the King's highway, on the 12th of August, putting him in fear, and taking from his person, and against his will, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 chain, value 6d., and 2 seals, value 9s., his property.
CHARLES IRELAND. I am a ladies' shoe-maker. On Sunday, the 12th of August, I had been to the Blue Coat Boy public-house, to get a glass of gin and noyeau. I was then going home, and was stopped close to my house, by the prisoner and a female. I had been treating them at the public-house, and we came out together; they wanted me to go further, but I would not; they then came, and each took hold of my arm, and the female made a catch at my watch; the prisoner seized me at the back of my neck, and held me while the woman took my watch - I seized fast hold of each of them, and saw her give the watch to the prisoner, when her things began to tear - I let go of her then; he then gave the watch to her, and she ran away - I detained him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Have you told the whole story? A. Yes - when the woman was near, the prisoner tried to throw me down, but I kept hold of him; they came into the house, and asked me to treat them at the Blue Coat Boy, and then we went to the Hoop and Grapes public-house - I might drink a drop of gin and water, and some beer - I did not go into any other public-house.
Q. Did you not state, at the Mansion-house, that another person was the man? A. I was asked if that was the man, and said Yes, thinking it was the prisoner - I remember the Lord Mayor telling me to come sober next time.
NICHOLAS BERRY. I am a watchman. I was coming up Petticoat-lane, calling twelve o'clock, and saw a woman dart off as quick as possible, and when I got to the corner, I saw the prisoner in the prosecutor's custody; he dragged him a considerable way - I took him in charge - he said the prisoner had held him while he had been robbed of his watch.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not the prosecutor rather drunk? A. He had been drinking; he was not very drunk - I saw him at the Mansion-house; he was not sober then.
JAMES MITCHELL. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house; the prosecutor was drunk, and said the prisoner had received the watch from the girl, as he has stated.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-349]
1936. HENRY KING was indicted for feloniously assaulting Matilda Williams, in a certain open place, near the King's highway, on the 15th of July, at St. Sepulchre, putting her in fear, and taking from her person, and against her will, 1 shawl, value 3s.; 1 cap, value 6d.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 1 pair of pockets, value 6d.; 4 shillings, 1 sixpence, and 12 halfpence, her property.
MATILDA WILLIAMS. On the 15th of July, between twelve and one o'clock in the morning, I was going along Long-lane; the prisoner passed me, and said, "Are you going home, Mrs. Williams?" he knew me; he is a drover, I believe - he said, "Shall I go home with you?" - I said, "No, I thank you," - I had not gone many steps before he tripped me up, and struck me on the left eye; he tore my shawl off, then tore my gown open, and took my handkerchief off my neck, and my cap off; he then took out a knife, and cut off my pockets, which contained my money - I screamed out, and he struck me over the head, with a stick, and got away.
SARAH CLINTON. On the morning of the 15th of July, about four o'clock, I saw the prisoner in Fleet-market; he said to me, "Will you buy a shawl?" and showed it to me - I said, "That is Mrs. Williams';" he said, "It is, but you need not mention that, and you shall have it for 1s." - I said I would not have it on any account, and went away.
DAVID EVANS. I am a watchman. I took up the prisoner on Monday morning, the 16th of July, between twelve and one o'clock - I had received information of this robbery from one Benning - I called the prisoner out of a public-house; he objected to come - I then called my fellow watchman, and we took him to the watch-house; we searched, but found nothing on him; he said he did not know any thing about Mrs. Williams, but afterwards confessed that he saw her part of the way home.
WILLIAM BUSHNELL. I am a watchman. I went with Evans to the Bull's Head public-house, and told the prisoner what he was charged with; he denied it, but afterwards said he had taken the prosecutrix home to where she lodged, and the landlord refused to take her in.
MATILDA WILLIAMS re-examined. I raised an alarm, but it is a bye place, near the pig-market; my forehead was bleeding, and my left eye.
Prisoner's Defence (written). I assure your Honour, that I am perfectly innocent, not withstanding the false allegations made to prove my guilt, of the charge, and which evidence, were I sufficiently competent, or enabled to employ counsel, I feel confident, would clearly be made to appear, in the course of the investigation, as completely false; and a case made up between the parties against me, who are of the most abandoned character, and whose manner of obtaining a livelihood, is by the most base means; as to the above-named person (Williams) I know her to keep a house of the most abandoned character, it being a common receptacle for prostitutes; and the witness, that she has caused to appear against me, I am totally unacquainted with; but I have no doubt, if questioned, that she would appear to be one connected with the prosecutrix in those, her abandoned pursuits, by which means I have, in the present instance, tallen an unfortunate victim. I, however, trust that by the following statement, you will see the case against me is altogether a false charge:- In the month of July, at about twelve o'clock at night, I had (on Saturday) been spending the evening with some of my workmates, at the sign of the Horse and Groom public-house, Cow-cross, and in leaving the company, and passing through Smithfield, on my way home, I met the prosecutrix, in company with a watchman, who I asked what he was going to do with her; he said he was going to take her to the watch-house, she being then in a state of intoxication - I told the watchman I would see her home, if he would allow me; and accordingly, knowing where she lived, from being in the habit of passing and repassing her house, I proceeded to take her home, where, after a deal of trouble, I arrived, and the woman belonging to the house came to the door, who, on telling her I had brought home on of her lodgers, denied that she (Williams) was a lodger of hers, and refused to admit her - with this I left her at the door, and went home.
SARAH CLINTON re-examined. I had been carrying out things for washer-women, but was out of place at that time - I knew Mrs. Williams by working at different laundresses.
DAVID EVANS. The prosecutrix lives at a Mr. Webb's- she had marks of violence on her.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 19.
[trialAccount t18270913-350]
1937. THOMAS SMITH and WILLIAM BUTLER were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, 1 basket, called a flat, value 1s.; 1 gown, value 2s. 6d.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 3 dead pigeons, value 1s. 6d.; 2 dead fish, value 1s.; 2lbs. weight of pork, value 1s. 6d.; 4 quarts of currants, value 1s. 4d.; 20 eggs, value 1s. 6d., and 4lbs. weight of potatoes, value 4d., the goods of William Barker.
SECOND COUNT, stating them to be the property of John Reed.
WILLIAM BARKER. I am carter to Thomas Wright, of Sawbridgworth. On the 16th of July I came to Smithfield with some lambs in my cart - there was a flat on the top of the cart, and as I crossed the market, with a load under my arm, I saw Smith leading my horse - I called to him to stop - he did so; I then missed the flat from the cart - it contained several things; I had received it from John Reed, to leave at the Ram Inn, Smithfield; Smith brought the flat back - I had not seen him take it.
GEORGE BUSH. I am a watchman. On the 16th of July, about a quarter to four o'clock in the morning, I stood about forty yards from the cart, and saw Smith take the flat off at the corner of King-street - I ran down West-street, and saw Butler standing near the work-house in the street; Smith went up and spoke to him, and gave it to him: Smith saw me, and came up towards me; we came up to Smithfield, and he gave the flat up.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Who is Butler? A. His father keeps a public-house in Smithfield; I do not know that the house is usually opened early; he had not got hold of Smith; he said, when I was taking them to the watch-house, that he had taken it from Smith, having stopped him with it - that was within a minute or two of the time. I ate nothing that was in the basket.
SUSAN REED. I live at Harlow, and am John Reed's brother. I know this gown and handkerchief; the other things have not been saved.
ABRAHAM GRIFFITH. I am a constable. I received charge of the prisoners. This gown and handkerchief were in the basket when it was opended at the office.
SMITH's Defence. Butler knew nothing about it.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
BUTLER - NOT GUILTY.
