1938. WILLIAM WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 4 half-crowns, 8 shillings, and 4 sixpences, the monies of John Gurney.
EDMUND CRISP. I am in the employ of John Gurney, of Salisbury-court, Fleet-street. I laid four half-crowns, some shillings and sixpences on the counter, for a gentleman- the prisoner came in, and stood close to the gentleman - he was having some rum and water. I went to another part of the counter, to give another person change, and the gentleman asked for his change, which I thought he had taken up, and immediately I said some one had taken it, he went out, leaving half his liquor - I stopped Proudfoot, who was taken to the watch-house; the prisoner was brought there afterwards - it was about a quarter-past ten o'clock; he confessed it to the Alderman.
GEORGE PROUDFOOT. I was at Gurney's wine-vaults; the money laid on the counter, near where the prisoner and I stood; I went out; Crisp came after me, and charged me with taking it, and took me to the watch-house; I had some money of my own, but not theirs.
Prisoner. Q. Did I not go voluntarily with you to the watch-house, to see you righted? A. Yes; I accused him of it - he denied it, but went to the watch-house quietly - there were eight or ten other people there.
WILLIAM ELMES. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house by Proudfoot, about a quarter to eleven o'clock- I found on him three half-crowns, 41/2d., and a gold ring; I heard him confess it before the Alderman, and say he did it through distress. He was rather in liquor.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in liquor - I had been in Proudfoot's company from seven o'clock till ten.
GEORGE PROUDFOOT. I had been in his company.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-352]
1939. RICHARD GREENFIELD was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 memorandum-book, value 9d., the goods of a person unknown, from his person.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am an officer. On the 31st of July I was at a fire near London-bridge; I saw a gentleman on Fish-street-hill - the prisoner and a number of others were about him; I saw the prisoner lift up the flap of his coat over his shoulder, and take this book out of his pocket; I went up; the prisoner dropped the book on the ground, and I secured him; a girl took it up - she refused to give it to a friend of mine, and I took her to the watch-house, where she gave it up - the gentleman got away.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is the girl here? A. No; she said she found it in the street, and a young man dropped it; I was close to the gentleman, but there came such a rush I could not get to him.
WILLIAM CLARK. I am an officer. I was with Robinson, and saw the prisoner attempting to get his hand into the slip pocket of the gentleman, but he could not; I saw him turn the flap of the coat up, and get the book out - it dropped, and the girl took it up.
Cross-examined. Q. How long did this take? A. It might be a minute or two - I was two or three yards from the gentleman, but I did not give an alarm, because there were so many of them - I had a neighbour with me.
WILLIAM TARRANT. I was going down Fish-streethill, and saw the prisoner with another person - they tried every one of the gentleman's pockets, and then turned up the flap of his coat, and the book dropped out; a girl picked it up - she would not give it up at first.
Cross-examined. Q. How near were you to the gentleman? A. As near as I could be, next to the officers, not more than two yards from him; the prisoner was two or three minutes putting his hands into his pockets; the moment the prisoner was caught hold of, there was a mighty rush, and the gentleman was carried away with the stream.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-353]
1940. ROBERT THOMAS CAPPS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 1 table-spoon, value 10s., the goods of William Gibson.
DANIEL SARGENT. I am a waiter at Baker's coffee-house, Cornhill, which is kept by William Gibson. On Friday, the 17th of August, the prisoner came there about half-past one o'clock; he went up stairs into the coffee-room; I asked if he was going to dine; he said No, he was going to wait a little time; he asked for a paper, but there was none; he asked for a glass of sherry, which I went down and got him; I thought he was looking at the tables, and before I went for it I counted the spoons - one table had four on it, another three, and another two; when I returned with the sherry I counted them again, and they were right; I went down and told Mr. Gibson; I went up again, and then one of them was gone - the prisoner was then coming out of the room; I said, "I must detain you;" he said, "For what?" I said, "For a spoon;" and he produced it.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. What part of his person did he take it from? A. I could not see, as he turned round; he gave Mr. Gibson his name and address when he came up.
JOHN COLLING. I was dining at this coffee-house; Sargent went down; he came up again, and accused the prisoner, who seemed in great trepidation, and took up the newspaper, and, after perusing it for a moment, he was endeavouring to take something from his pocket, and then on a table, on which I had seen but three spoons, I saw four; one was a different sort to the others; I will not swear he placed it there.
JOHN DIXON SMITH. I am an officer. I went to the coffee-house, and took the prisoner; he was accused of taking one spoon from the George and Vulture, and one from Mr. Gibson.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I put it to the Jury to say whether I could commit the offence, as I am not in want of character, money, or business.
The prisoner received an excellent character
GUILTY. Aged 30.
[trialAccount t18270913-354]
1941. ROBERT THOMAS CAPPS was again indicted for stealing, on the 17th of August, 1 table spoon, value 10s., the goods of William Mariner.
JOSEPH CASLEY. I am a waiter at the George and Vulture. kept by William Mariner. On the 17th of August a person, whom I believe to be the prisoner, came and had some soup, between one and two o'clock; he had a silver table spoon with it. I did not see him go out; a person came from Baker's coffee-house - I then went into the room where he had been, and missed one spoon out of thirty-six, which were laying on the tables there - this isone of them - they were all right about an hour before he came.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. How can you swear to the prisoner? A. I believe him to be the man; the spoon has our mark on it; the spoon was not taken from the basin.
JOHN COLLING. I was dining at Mr. Gibson's coffee-house. I found the prisoner there, and heard a dispute about a spoon - I saw him sit down at a table where there were three spoons - he took a newspaper in his hand, and, in a few minutes, there were four spoons - nobody could have put it there but him; nobody else had been near the table.
Cross-examined. Q. Can you swear this is not one of the three spoons which were there before? A. Yes, it is much smaller than them.
DANIEL SARGENT. I found this spoon, belonging to the George and Vulture, on our table. GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-355]
1942. JOHN HAWKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 1 watch, value 6l., the goods of William Light.
WILLIAM JONES. I live with Mr. Light, of Skinner-street; he keeps a Birmingham warehouse - the prisoner came with two others to buy a pin-cushion; they went out, came in again, and wanted another like it, which we had not got - we said we would change the first one - the prisoner then bought a comb, and went away; they came again with a fourth person, who wanted a knife, and while my mistress was gone into the parlour, I saw the prisoner with a gold watch in his hand; he was shutting down the glass-case from which he had taken it; he ran out; I pursued - the others ran after me, and I was tripped up. I do not know what became of the watch - the glass-case was on the counter.
Cross-examined by MR. McDOWELL. Q. Was it not a dark night? A. Yes; I never lost sight of him - my master told me to make quite sure of him.
COURT. Q. Do you swear your master told you to swear to him at all events? A. No.
WILLIAM LIGHT. I keep this house, it is in the parish of St. Sepulchre. I told my boy he might be asked if he was positive of the prisoner, and to speak accurately to what he knew. I was out at the time - I had seen a gold watch in the case about an hour before.
Cross-examined. Q. What was the lowest value of it? A. The selling price is 7l. 10s. I paid about 6l. 10s. for it.
GEORGE BUSH. On the 13th of September, between eight and nine o'clock at night, I was in Skinner-street - I saw the prisoner come out of this shop; he was running towards King-street, and Jones running after him - three more came out, and ran right before him. Jones fell down - I ran and caught the prisoner - we brought him back to Mr. Light's. Mrs. Light said, "That is the boy who took the watch out of the case."
Cross-examined. Q. Was it not dark? A. There was plenty of gas-light. I found 2s. on him.
GEORGE GODFREY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in charge.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 12.
Of stealing to the value of 99s. only.
Whipped and Discharged.

GEORGE GODFREY. On the 6th of September, at night, I went up Wheatsheaf-yard, and found the prisoner asleep in a cart, with this basket of damsons under him. I asked where he got it - he said "I wanted money, and intended to sell it."

Third London Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1944. THOMAS HEFFIELD, alias BROWN , was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, at St. Giles without, Cripplegate, 1 gelding, price 6l., the property of John Stevens.
JOHN STEVENS. I am a higler, and live at Chingfordgreen, Essex. On Tuesday, the 24th of July, I was at Chelmsford races, where I had a drinking-booth. I saw my gelding safe at ten o'clock that night, when I sent my man with it - it was a black gelding - it was brought down to be fed at the booth; it was turned out to grass on Galley-common, adjoining the race-course - there were other stall-keepers' horses there. I sent my man for it next morning and it was gone - my man is not here. I sent him nearly twenty miles to look for it; my wife's brother found it in Smithfield. I saw it there myself, at the Ram Inn-yard, on the following Saturday - I know nothing of the prisoner.
THOMAS BARKER. I am an officer of Smithfield. On Friday the 27th of July, I was on duty in Smithfield - it was horse market day; some man came to me, and asked if I would come to the Ram-yard, and take possession of a horse which they had found, belonging to somebody in the country. I went and found this gelding there, in possession of a man named Clinsey; and from what he said, I went to Dixon's Repository, and from what I heard there, I went there on the Monday at nine o'clock - I left, and went again at ten; I then found the prisoner in custody. I asked him where he got the horse; he said a man met him in the street leading the horse, and asked him to take it and sell it for him, and he would give him 5s. if he would sell it, and bring him the money. I asked the prisoner his name, he said it was Thomas Brown, and that the man who employed him was John Linton, of Church-street, Bethnal-green. I asked where the man was - he said he was waiting for him at a public-house close by - I asked him where the public-house was, and said I would go and take this Linton - he said it was in Beech-street or Barbican, on the right hand side of the way, but he did not know the name of the street, nor the sign of the house; he said he would go and show me; I asked if he could not give me a better description; he then said it was at the Star public-house, in the City-road, opposite the Lying-in Hospital - I asked what sort of a man it was; he said a tall thin man, with drab coat and top-boots - I secured him, and then went to the Star in the City-road; I had some porter, and inquired if they had seen such a person,but they had not. I afterwards showed the same horse to the prosecutor, who claimed it - as I took him down the yard, he said, "I have told you part, I may as well tell you all; my name is Heffield;" I said, "Is it not Thomas?" he said Yes.
THOMAS SEARES. I am clerk at Dixon's repository, Barbican. On Wednesday, the 25th of July, about twelve o'clock at noon, the prisoner brought this horse to our repository - I entered it in the day book - I asked him what name; he said, "Linton, Church-street, Bethnal-green;" he booked it, "Quiet to ride, and quiet in harness, to be sold to the highest bidder" - it was sold to the highest bidder, at the Friday's sale for 1l. 10s. - it was small and very lean - it was delivered to the purchaser, who paid me for it; he is not here, but I understand he took it to Smithfield - it was knocked down to a Mr. Hedges, but afterwards transferred to Mr. Clansey, who paid me, and took it away.
THOMAS BARKER. I shewed the prosecutor the same horse, and also shewed it to Seares.
THOMAS SEARES. I saw it - it was the same horse; the prisoner gave his name as Thomas Brown - he called on Monday for the money, and I detained him.
JOHN STEVENS. The horse they showed me was mine, and the one I lost from Chelmsford.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming from Islington, down the City-road - this man was coming down Old-street-road with the horse; he beckoned to me, and asked me to take the horse to Dixon's, which I did - and asked the boy if that was the sale day - he said No. I asked if I should take it home; he said I might leave it, which I did, to be sold on Friday. I went and told the man, and he asked if I should be that way on the Monday - I said, Yes; he told me to meet him, and he would pay me for my trouble - I went and met him, and then went to get the money - he said he would give me 5s.; when I got there, I told them the name I booked it in.
THOMAS BARKER. I went to Church-street, Bethnalgreen; next day, and was about there some hours, but no such person as Linton could be found or heard of.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 23.
Recommended to mercy by the prosecutor, understanding him to bear a good character.
[trialAccount t18270913-358]
1945. JOSEPH MACHEE was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 1 gold pin, value 5s., the goods of Charles Gibson.
JAMES YOUNG. I am in the employ of Mr. Charles Gibson, a jeweller, of Bishopsgate-street. On the afternoon of Tuesday, the 11th of September, the prisoner came into the shop, and asked to look at some gold pins; I showed him some, which did not please; he then asked for some brooches, and while I was reaching them I saw him take a pin from the card, (with the two fingers of his right hand,) and put it into his pocket - he was then about twenty minutes bargaining for another pin, for which I asked him 6s., and he offered me 3s. 6d. - he went out, without buying any thing - I followed and brought him back, before he got out of sight; I made him produce the pin, which was in his watch pocket - I could not understand half he said, being a foreigner; I said, "Produce that pin you took away;" "Here is that pin," said he - he appeared to understand me, though I could not understand him. I then sent for the officer. He did not appear at all intoxicated.
THOMAS TUNNELL. I am an officer. Mr. Young sent for me, about a quarter after four o'clock, to take charge of the prisoner - he gave me this pin, and said he had stolen it; I took him to the Compter; I found 11s. in his possession, and in a bundle which he had, was a new pair of stockings, some butter, bacon, and pork - he offered me the 11s. if I would let him go - he spoke plain.
Prisoner's Defence (through an Interpreter.) I was intoxicated - I know nothing about it.
GUILTY. Aged 45.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-359]
1946. JOHN GRAHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 bag, value 1d.; 2 tobacco-boxes, value 1s. 6d.; 1 snuff-box, value 6d.; 1 knife, value 2s.; 2 hooks, value 1s.; 1 key-ring, value 6d.; 4 keys, value 1s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 8s.; 2 half-crowns, 1 sixpence, 3 pence, and 3 halfpence, the property of Joseph Baker, from his person.
JOSEPH BAKER. I am a carman. On the 14th of August I was coming home from my club, to the Maze, in the Borough, where I lodge. I was going across Newgate-market, about a quarter before one o'clock; John Woods, one of my club-mates, had gone with me as far as there, and then left me; when he was gone I sat down on some boards, and fell asleep; the prisoner came and sat by me, and took my property; my jacket and side pockets were buttoned up- he awoke me, by untying my apron, and getting his hand into my pockets: he took out my purse and a bag, with my money in it. I had seen it all secure a quarter of an hour before. I was sober, but being tired I fell asleep.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Were you quite in your sober senses? A. Yes; I had only drank a little beer; this man came and sat down by me: I had dropped asleep, and was awoke by his fumbling about; he was about five minutes in doing it - I did not seize him, because it did not directly strike me that there was any thing wrong- it was not possible for me to drop these things from my pocket; I was not off my legs five minutes; the clock had struck one before I sat down.
JOHN SPEECHLEY. I am an officer. The watchman brought the prisoner to me, and gave me this bag, containing a knife and some buttons - I found this tobacco-box on him.
SAMUEL BOND. I am the watchman. At half-past one o'clock the prosecutor called Stop thief! I saw the prisoner running and two men after him; I stopped him, and he gave me this bag and knife.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not the prisoner tell you that what he had, he had picked up? A. Yes; the prisoner pulled the bag out of his pocket, and gave it me, and then the prosecutor gave charge of him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 27.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-360]
1947. JOHN DONOGHUE and WILLIAM EDWARDS were indicted for stealing, on the 25th of July, 5 shirts, value 2l. 5s.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 7s.; 3 towels, value 2s., and 3 pairs of stockings, value 3s., the goods of Esther Mackenzie, from the person of Mary Morrish .
SECOND COUNT, stating then to be the property of William Edward Holderness.
WILLIAM EDWARD HOLDERNESS, I am a boot and shoe maker, and live in Barbican. I left this linen in care of my boy on the 25th of July, and he was to deliver it to whoever came to fetch it for Esther Mackenzie; it was for her to wash.
MARY MORRISH. I am eleven years of age. I received the bundle, and was going to take it to Mrs. Mackenzie, to be washed; she lives at No. 3, Lee's-buildings - when I got to the bottom of Long-lane, Donoghue came and said,"Little girl go over the way, for a halfpenny-worth of tobacco;" he held my bundle, and when I came back he gave me a halfpenny worth of cherries, and gave me the bundle, told me to sit down on a step, and see that it that it was all right - I told him I did not know what was in it. I then went on to the bottom of Long-lane, and he came to me again, and said, "Go over the way, and get two pipes." I left my bundle and went; and when I returned, the prisoner Edwards had run away with it - I asked Donoghue where the bundle was, and he said a boy had run away with it; I had seen Edwards following us before, but not close to us - Donoghue said, "Wait there, and I will run after him." I have not seen the bundle since - I am quite sure it was Donoghue who spoke to me; I did not take particular notice of Edwards, but I believe he is the person.
FRANCIS GIBBINS. I am an officer. I saw a crowd of people at the corner of Long-lane, about a quarter-past eight o'clock in the evening of the 25th of July; a gentleman said, "I believe that child has been robbed of a bundle of linen;" I saw an ass and some hampers of apples there; I locked them up in the Green-yard, and by that means we got information of the prisoners.
HENRY SUMNER. On the 25th of July, about half-past seven o'clock in the evening, this little girl came for the bundle of linen; I gave it to her, and told her to take care of it.
JAMES VINT. I am a constable. The ass and apples being taken to the Green-yard, I gave orders that no one should have them but the owner; and Mrs. Page, to whom the Green-yard belongs, came, and brought Donoghue to me; he said that he had run after the other boy - in the evening, Mrs. Page sent to me again, and said she had got Edwards; he said that the other had induced him to take the bundle, and before he took it, he had taken a handkerchief out of it; he said he had sold it in Petticoat-lane - I went there, but could hear nothing of it.
EDWARDS' Defence. The things were on another man's head; he asked me to take them, which I did. When I got home my parents would not let me in - I went and sold them.
DONOGHUE'S Defence. When I sent the little girl for the pipes, some man said this boy had taken the bundle; and he persuaded me to go after him - I said I would if any body would take care of the donkey; a gentleman said he would mind it; I went, but could not find him.
DONOGHUE - GUILTY. Aged 17.
EDWARDS - GUILTY. Aged 13.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-361]
1948. JOHN DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of September, 1 pocket-book, value 1s.; 1 snuff box, value 6d., and 2 shillings, the property of John Wheeler, from the person of Mary Ann, his wife .
MARY ANN WHEELER. I am the wife of John Wheeler; we live in High-street, Kingsland. I was in Bartholomew fair about nine o'clock in the evening of the 5th of September; I had a pocket on, which contained a snuff-box, 2s. in silver, and a pocket-book. I was opposite Richardson's show; all at once, there was a great bustle, and I felt something pulling from my side - I turned, and saw a short man in a great coat, cutting the bottom part of my pocket off, with a knife or a pair of scissars. I said, "You have got my pocket and my little property;" he said,"No, I have not indeed;" he turned to get away from me, as I had got hold of him, and gave the pocket to the prisoner. I saw him take it from his hands - I called, and the officer came up and took him. I can swear positively the prisoner was close to me, when the other cut my pocket and took it from him. I did not hear any conversation between them; I saw it in the prisoner's hands.
Prisoner. She said she could not swear the pocket was put into my hands at all. Witness. No, I said he did not cut it off. I can say distinctly that he had it.
MATTHEW MOORE. I am a book-binder. I was standing close to Richardson's show - the prisoner and a number of others made a rush - I saw them about this woman's clothes, and one man took something of a light colour from her person, which he gave to the prisoner - the woman said, "That man has got my pocket;" I said, "The other man (meaning the prisoner) has got it." I saw him drop it at his feet, and the prosecutrix took it up.
Prisoner. He swore at Guildhall I never had it. Witness. No, I did not.
WILLIAM WORCESTER. I am an officer. I was in the fair, and seeing a great crowd near the show, I went up to inquire what was the matter; the prosecutrix pointed out the prisoner, who had had the pocket in his possession - I took him in custody.
WILLIAM DRINKWATER. I was with Worcester - the prosecutrix gave me this pocket, which has been cut off with a knife. I found only this pocket-book and thimble in it - there was a great gang about at the time.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been at work all the day, and came out to get a pint of beer - I happened to drop into the fair - there was a crowd of people, and I was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-362]
1949. PHILIP POWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, 1 plane, value 1s.; 4 saws, value 10s.; 1 gauge, value 2s., and 1 square, value 1s., the goods of Reuben Edwards.
REUBEN EDWARDS. I am a master carpenter. I lost these tools from my shop under my dwelling-house, in Liverpool-street, Bishopsgate, between ten and eleven o'clock, on the 28th of August. I never saw the prisoner till he was at the watch-house.
JOSEPH MICKLEFIELD. I am a pawnbroker, of Broad-street, Ratcliff. I have a hand-saw, pawned on the 28thof August, in the name of J. Williams. I do not know the prisoner - this is the duplicate of them.
WILLIAM PUTMAN. I have two saws and a plane, which were pawned at Mr. Fleming's, in Whitechapel - this is the duplicate of them.
JAMES DRAPER. 1953. JAMES BROWN, alias JAMES KING , was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 1 canvas bag, value 9d., and 1 bushel and 1 half of another bushel of walnuts, value 5s., the goods of John Smallpiece and another.
THOMAS SMALLPIECE. I am the son of John Smallpiece - we are orange merchants, and live at No. 4, Fish-street-hill. On the 30th of August I saw the prisoner go down Fish-street-hill, about two feet from our warehouse, with a bag, which looked very much like one which had been in our warehouse - I followed him, and it proved to be our bag and our walnuts.
JURY. Q. Can you swear to the bag? A. We have others like it in the warehouse - I missed some walnuts, which were there.
JAMES CLARK. I am in the service of Msesrs. Smallpiece. I know this bag is theirs. I had placed it in the warehouse, and this quantity of walnuts in it.

[trialAccount t18270913-367]
1954. JOHN ELSON was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, 1 reticule, value 6d.; 1 purse, value 2s.; 1 ring, value 2d.; 6 keys, value 6d.; 3 shillings, and 4 sixpences, the property of James Donald, from the person of Mary, his wife.
MARY DONALD. I am the wife of James Donald, who is a haberdasher. On the 6th of September I went to Bartholomew fair with my husband - I had a reticule, containing two bunches of keys, 5s. in silver, and a purse; we got before Richardson's show: we were only going through the fair, going home. My reticule was snatched from me by some man - the prisoner was standing by me at the time- I saw him when he was taken. I have never seen my reticule since.
WILLIAM BENNS. I am a constable. I was at the fair by accident, not on duty. I saw a gang of well-known pickpockets, and the prisoner among them. I saw this lady and her husband - she had a reticule on her left arm. I saw the prisoner make a snatch - he got it, put it behind him, and some one took it, but I do not know who; I laid hold of him, and did not let him go.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. What are you? A. A constable of St. George, Bloomsbury: I had been to see my brother, and then went to the fair; I had got my staff. I am a special constable, paid by the committee of Torrington-square. I was opposite Richardson's show; I had seen the prisoner about an hour before- I was by the side of this lady, felt a push, and saw him snatch the reticule; some one said, "That was well done;"I saw it on her left arm before he took it; I saw him take it - he was standing up.
Q. Do not you remember the woman saying he was not the man, when you took him? A. No, I do not; the instant he snatched it her husband took hold of him, and was crying out for an officer, and in two or three minutes the officer came.
JOSEPH MOORE. I am a City constable, I heard a call for a constable, and took the prisoner.
BENJAMIN CHARLES MURREY. I was in the fair, opposite the show - I saw the prosecutrix with her reticule, and saw the prisoner make the snatch. I caught hold of him, and was thrown down.
Cross-examined by Mr. PRENDERGAST. Q. You saw a hand? A. I saw the prisoner's hand, and saw him make the snatch. I was close by him.
MR. PRENDERGAST to MARY DONALD. Q. Do not you remember that you said he was not the man? A: I could not say so - I could not see his face, but my husband and those about said he was the man.
MR. PRENDERGAST called -
WILLIAM BAKER. I was at the fair on the night in question. I was passing, and saw this young man collared; I heard the woman repeatedly say that was not the man, four or five times.
Q. Is that all you heard? A. Yes. I live at No. 4, Mapes-street, Hare-street-fields. I do not know where the prisoner lives; I have seen him before, but have never been in his company.
COURT. Q. What are you? A. A weaver; I was pretty near the prisoner; I do not know how many people were there; I did not hear the complaint of the robbery; I believe she had been robbed - I did not hear that she had lost her reticule, nor that she had been robbed; I had seen the prisoner before - I saw him taken up; I do not know what became of his companions; I heard the woman say he was not the man; I was subpoenaed here - I do not know who by; I had no conversation with him after he was taken up - I cannot tell who subpoenaed me - the subpoena was brought to my house; I suppose the solicitor subpoenaed me.
Q. Will you swear that you heard that woman say, in Bartholomew fair, that that young man was not the man? A. Yes, Sir, I can.
MARY DONALD re-examined. Q. Did you ever say that was not the man? A. No. I could not; I saw the prisoner by the side of me; but I could not say he was the man who did it.
WILLIAM BENNS re-examined. I was present all the time, and heard all that was said; if she had said he was not the man, I must have heard it; I never saw this witness there to my knowledge; if this declaration had been made I must have heard it.
Cross-examined by MR. PRENDERGAST. Q. You say you were standing by him? A. Yes; I followed the lady and the prisoner down to the Compter; I had hold of the prisoner, and the lady was next to my left arm.
COURT. Q. Did you see from the first to the last of it? A. Yes; I went with the lady till she got to a place of safety, and if this passed I must have heard it.
WILLIAM BAKER re-examined. Q. Do you swear that the lady said the prisoner was not the man? A. Yes.
Q. And at the time? A. Yes; I do not know how I came to be subpoenaed - I received my subpoena on Sunday - I have got it with me; I was not sent for to attend the examination before the Magistrate; I have never spoken to the prisoner, not to my knowledge; I have seen him at different times; I never knew him in jeopardy before - I have never been in trouble myself; how I came to be subpoenaed was, I happened to tell a young woman who happened to be in company with him, and by that means they told the solicitor; I had seen that young woman in company with him before, five or six different times- I have spoken to her before.
Q. Did she keep company with him? A. I do not know, I am sure, Sir; not to my knowledge, I do not know that she keeps company with him - I have seen them together five or six different times, seven or eight weeks ago.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Life.
The witness Baker was committed for wilful and corrupt perjury.
[trialAccount t18270913-368]
EIGHTH DAY. - FRIDAY, SEPTEMBER 21.
Third London Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1955. EDWARD EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of September, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of a certain person, whose name is unknown, from his person.
WILLIAM COLTON. I am an officer. On Sunday evening, the 2d of September, I was going down Long-lane, with Cadby, at half-past nine o'clock - I saw the prisoner, with two others, behind a gentleman and lady - I saw one of them take the handkerchief, and give it to the prisoner - I seized two of them, and called the gentleman, who stood by, when we got the handkerchief from the prisoner; but we struggled across the road, and the gentleman got away.
JOSEPH CADBY. I was with Colton. I saw the prisoner and two others; one of them took the handkerchief, and gave it to the prisoner, who was behind him; this was just at the end of Long-lane.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Three Months.

1956. JOHN COLEMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 5 yards of rush-matting, value 5s., the goods of John Oliver.
JOHN OLIVER. I am an upholsterer, and live in Houndsditch. I lost some rush-matting on the 14th of September, between two and three o'clock, while I was at dinner- I had seen it safe an hour or two before.
MARY HODDER. I live in Leathersellers' Alms-houses. I was standing to rest my basket, opposite Mr. Oliver's door; I saw the prisoner go into the shop empty-handed, and come out with a roll of matting on his shoulder - I went in and told Shaw, who was rubbing chairs at the bottom of the shop. I am certain of his person; he was alone.
JEREMIAH SHAW. I am in the employ of Mr. Oliver-Hodder gave me information - I went in the direction she told me, and found the prisoner with the matting under his arm.
JAMES ROBERTS. I took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 22.

1957. JOHN RUSSEL was indicted for embezzlement.
ABRAHAM ABITBOL. I live in Lombard-street. The prisoner was in my service, for one week; on the 5th of September, at ten o'clock, I sent him with a cheque for 3l., to Martin and Stone's; he was to get the money for it; he never returned - I found him again on the 14th of September; he had been in the habit of coming to my house every morning, or of sleeping at my house; the cheque has been returned to me as paid.
GEORGE SMITH. I am an officer. I took up the prisoner on the 14th of September - Mr. Abitbol was with me - I told him I took him for receiving the money for the cheque; he said he had received two sovereigns, and 20s. in silver, but had not any of it then; he said he was ashamed to come back, for a man had stopped him, whom he owed some money to, and he had paid it to him. The person he paid it to, he said was not in town; the clerk is not here who paid the cheque.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-371]
1958. HENRY ROGERS was indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Richard Meggison Donkin, on the 29th of July, at St. Sepulchre, and stealing, 6 silver tea-spoons, value 2l. 2s.; 2 tablespoons, value 1l. 15s.; 2 salt-spoons, value 10s.; 1 pair of sugar-tongs, value 20s.; 2 muslin dresses, value 3l. 5s.; 1 lace veil, value 15s.; 1 hat, value 10s.; 1 other hat, value 1s. 6d.; 3 coats, value 6l; 1 great coat, value 3l.; 1 pair of trousers, value 2s.; 1 pelisse, value 7l.; 1 pair of boots, value 20s.; 1 shirt, value 10s.; 1 piece of linen cloth, containing 29 yards, value 2l.; 1 wafer seal, value 1s.; 4 shillings, 6 sixpences, 420 pence, and 120 halfpence, his property.
MARY DONKIN. I keep the White Hart, public-house, Fleet-market ; my husband's name is Richard Meggison Donkin. On Saturday night, the 29th of July, about a quarter-past twelve o'clock, when we went to bed, these articles (enumerating the articles set out in the indictment) were safe; one dress was in the cupboard, the other hung there; the plate was all in the secretaire, and the other things were about; in the morning they were all gone - I have since seen the seal, which I had used overnight, to seal the lease of a house; both of which I locked up in the secretaire - I have found nothing but the seal.
JAMES SIMONS. I live with Mr. Donkin. The prisoner offered me a sovereign to leave my master's door open - I had lived with Mr. Donkin three weeks; the prisoner had used the house about a fortnight; he gave no reason why I was to leave the door open - I did not do it - I went to bed on Saturday night, and at six o'clock next morning, the place had been robbed - I had seen the prisoner there as late as twelve on Saturday night- I am sure it was as late as twelve; the shutters were shut between seven and eight o'clock.
Prisoner. Q. Did you tell your master I had offered you a sovereign? A. No.
MARY DONKIN re-examined. My husband is not here - he and I have had a little unpleasantness, and he has left me - I hear he is on the Continent. I fastened the door on Saturday night, and took the cash up stairs, and locked it up. In the morning my husband came to me, and said the house was broken into - I then went down nearly as soon as him; the street door was fast, but the upper part of the bar window was down, and there three locks opened; the lock of the parlour door going into the yard was one.
Prisoner. Q. Pray what house do you keep over the water? A. It is No. 71, Stamford-street - it is a truly respectable house; we have had but two people in it.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I am a constable. I took up the prisoner on the 10th of August, at the Bull public-house, in St. John-street. I found this seal, this screwdriver, and a fork on him - he was drunk and fell down. I took him up, and took him to the watch-house; when he was sober I told him what I wanted him for; he said he knew nothing about the things. I went to Mr. Donkin's, to see if the screw-driver would match with any marks on the premises, and the top drawer of the secretaire seems to have been opened with it - it has been made out of the blade of an oyster knife: when he was locked up there was a girl there who calls him her husband; the prisoner called to her, "Tell Tom to get out of the way."
Prisoner. That could never open a drawer. Witness. Yes, it could, and it fitted exactly; the veneering is broken.
MRS. DONKIN re-examined. The inner doors were found open in the morning; there were marks on the outside of the small palour door, into which the bar window looks; that door leads into the yard - I saw the marks on the door before the officer came; there is only a small lock on that door - and when they got in, they had to force the other door to get into the bar. I found a blue pocket handkerchief on the sofa in the bar, and I had seen that or one very like it, in the prisoner's hand, in the tap room, in the evening; here it is. I found it when I came down with only my gown on, when my husband alarmed me. I know this seal by the letter W. on it - it has no other mark; my husband gave it to me, and I said it would do reversed for my name, "Mary." I believe it to be mine - the property lost is worth about 35l.
RICE PRICE. I took up Simons on suspicion, and he told me of the affair of the sovereign.
JAMES SIMONS re-examined. I was taken on suspicion, and then stated that the prisoner had offered me a sovereign.
WILLIAM JORDAN. I saw the prisoner on this Saturday night, between seven and eight o'clock, while I was putting up the shutters; the prisoner was putting up the tap-room shutters at the same time.
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw that handkerchief before; if I had it, the boy must have seen it. I have shut up for the boy seven or eight evenings, as he was not tall enough; the person I got the seal from I have sent for, but she was afraid to come up, because she could not say who she bought it of - it was Mrs. Brown, of Portpool-lane; the boy states I was in the house at twelve o'clock, but Mrs. Dunkin says it was between ten and eleven.
RICE PRICE. The house is in St. Sepulchre's parish.
JURY to MRS. DONKIN. Q. Have you any thing here which you have sealed with this seal? A. No - I saw the prisoner and Simons talking together while I was sealing the lease, and said to my husband, "All is not right," andI took the cash up stairs, as I thought I saw the prisoner looking at me through the window - he never came to my house afterwards - he used it before, and another man named Tom.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. There was no appearance of violence on the street door, but there was the mark of a person having got over the wall into a small yard - the parlour door opens into that yard, and on that door was a mark just the width of this oyster-knife - the marks still remain; on the secretaire drawer part of the veneer under the drawer is broken off - there is a small piece of the veneer in the middle, which is not broken off, which makes me think it was done with this, as it is a forked screw-driver.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 23.
[trialAccount t18270913-372]
1959. GEORGE KENT was indicted for embezzlement.
DAVID WHITBOURN. I am a fishmonger - the prisoner was my clerk. When he collected money he should bring it home to me immediately - he was to account to me every day.
Prisoner. Q. Had I any salary? A. No - you had your board and lodging, and sometimes a little money; you may have had money of mine two or three days in your possession, but not to my knowledge - he never told me of it.
Q. Did I not come by on one Saturday night, and say, I had some money, and you told me to keep it a few days longer? A. I believe it was so.
RICHARD CHESTER WOOLNER. I deal with the prosecutor. I paid the prisoner a cheque for 8l. 9s. on Saturday, the 8th of September, about eleven o'clock for the prosecutor.
MR. WHITBOURN. He never accounted to me for this money - he did not come back on the Saturday. I found him in the watch-house at Richmond on the Sunday - he had been taken up with a female by a friend of mine.
THOMAS BRAND. I am cashier at Messrs. Masterman's. I paid a cheque of Mr. Whitbourn's on the 8th of September, for 8l. 9s.
Prisoner's Defence. When I came to London, not having any thing to do, I became a sort of clerk to the prosecutor, for which I was to have board and lodging - I. of course, looked out for something better. I frequently went and collected money, and retained it in my possession a few days. After receiving this money, I heard of some private business, and went to Richmond, intending to return and account for his money, and on the way there, wanting money, I used part of it. I was captured there the same night, and on my return, the prosecutor refused to wait at my house while I could have the money made up.
MR. WHITBOURN. He did not ask me to wait at his house for the money to be made up.
NOT GUILTY.

1961. FREDERICK EDWARDS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 spoon, value 3s., the goods of George Edwards.

[trialAccount t18270913-375]
1962. ELIZABETH BULL and MARIA JOHNSON were indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, 1 watch, value 5l.; 2 seals, value 1l.; 1 watch-key, value 10s.; 1 pocket-book, value 2d.; 1 handkerchief, value 6s.; 4 sovereigns; 1 penny; one 5l. Bank note; one 5l. promissory note, and one other 1l. promissory note, the property of Alexander Jordan, from his person.
MR. PRENDERGAST conducted the prosecution.
ALEXANDER JORDAN. I live in Nelson-place, City-road, and am a cook. On the 12th of September, I was returning from Fulwood's-rents, and was getting towards a state of intoxication - a friend accompanied me as far as the House of Correction. I remember after that being in company with one or two females - I will not swear there were two. I remember afterwards being told I was robbed. I remember being in a house where there was a piece of work.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Were you not quite in a helpless state of drunkenness? A. I was going that way. I know my money was safe about half-past ten o'clock. I do not remember being at an oyster-shop or seizing any persons there.
MARY BISHOP. I live at No. 7, Charlotte-street, Sadler's-wells. On the night in question, within ten minutes of eleven o'clock, I was sitting in my parlour, and heard two females talking with a man. I knocked at my window, and said, "I don't approve of such language, and if you don't go away. I'll give charge of you." I soon after heard the watchman, I went to the door, and saw two females, and a gentleman in light trousers. Johnson who sat on his left hand took a watch out of his fob, and gave it to the one on his right - she then took something dark, which looked like a purse or pocket-book, and gave that to her; she then took something, which looked like a ribbon or chain and gave her. Johnson lives within three doors of me, I knew her well - the other had a light dress on; she went to Mrs. Hart's oyster-shop - Johnson followed, and left the gentleman within thirty yards of me. Mrs. Green called to me and said, "You saw that done, why don't you tell the gentleman where to go." I called the watchman, and pointed the house out. Mrs. Green fetched the gentleman and told him, that was where the women were gone - the watchman had got to the door by that time. I am certain the women who went into the shop are those who had been with him; but I will not swear to Bull's person.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you married? A. Yes - my husband is a bricklayer. I have lived there nine months; the street is infested with loose women - I frequently drive them away.
SARAH HART. I live at No. 1, Charlotte-street, and keep an oyster shop. On the night in question, about eleven o'clock, the two prisoners came into the shop; I was in the back room at supper; I went to them - they wanted a piece of fish; they gave me a penny-piece and a halfpenny for it, and one penny-piece fell on the floor; I took the candle to look for it, and the prosecutor ran in and seized me by the throat; I called Watch! the watchman came in, and said, "You are mistaken, these are the parties youwant;" and caught hold of the prisoners; they said what did he want with them, he might search them; the shop door was closed; the watchman was looking about, and a voice through the door said, "Look into the fire-place:" the watchman looked, and found a pocket-book there; I found a silk handkerchief; they searched every where, but found nothing more; the next day but one I found this Irish penny-piece on the floor; no women had been into the shop except the prisoners - the handkerchief and pocket-book could not have been there before they came; my sister was in the parlour.
Cross-examined. Q. How long had you kept the shop? A. Not three weeks; I let my first floor to a female, who says her husband is in Scotland - she was in bed at the time; I shut up immediately the prisoners were taken away; I was taken to the watch-house, and detained till morning, and taken to Hatton-garden, on account of the pocket-book and handkerchief being found in my house; I said they might search me; I was liberated - I do not know whether I was bailed - I do not know the meaning of it; they had not been a minute in the shop when the prosecutor came in; the handkerchief was near a basket - I pawned my ring after I left the office - I did not give any of the money to the prisoners.
SAMUEL WILD. I am a jeweller, and live in Wynyatt-place; I saw a crowd round the oyster-shop window; I looked in, and saw the two prisoners, two watchmen, a person in his shirt-sleeves, and a woman: I saw Bull take something from her breast and put it towards the fire-place - I believe she put it into the fire-place, but there was a dreadful crush at the time so that I could not see distinctly; I said nothing, but a person in a blue coat, at my side, tapped at the window, and said, "Look into the fire-place;" there was a great crush, and we were pushed away.
Cross-examined. Q. How many people were outside? A. Thirty or forty; Bull was sitting in a chair, near the window, with her back or side towards me.
WILLIAM EDWARDS. I am a watchman. I was on duty, and was called by a woman; I went directly down to Hart's, and saw the prosecutor holding Hart and her sister; I went out, sprung my rattle, and got assistance; the prosecutor said he was robbed of his watch; I took the prisoners to the watch-house: I did not see what happened in the shop, as I went back to see if any thing was in the yard.
Cross-examined. Q. You found the two sisters in the prosecutor's hands? A. Yes; I said they could not be the people, as I knew the prisoners to be loose characters.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Had you a particular reason for going backwards? A. Yes; as Bull appeared to come in from the back yard.
JOHN BATES. I am a watchman. I was on duty, and went to the shop, just as I was calling eleven o'clock; I saw Johnson there, and Bull came in as if from the yard; I searched her, and in her bosom found a purse, with a half-crown and two shillings in it; somebody knocked at the window, and said, "Look into the fire-place:" and the man in his shirt sleeves took this pocket-book from there - I saw it lay there when the person called out; I do not know who the man in the shirt sleeves was.
Cross-examined. Q. Whether he put it there, you do not know? A. No; I do not know where he lives - he came to the watch-house, but did not give his name; here is the handkerchief which Hart found.
WILLIAM BROWN EDWARDS. I am an officer. I have an Irish penny-piece which Mrs. Hart gave me - I cannot find who the man in his short sleeves was.
ALEXANDER JORDAN. This is my handkerchief; it has my name on it - the pocket-book is mine; there was one 5l. Bank note, and a 5l. and 1l. country note in it. I lost four or five sovereigns and a watch; I had an Irish penny in my pocket for some months - I know my purse was safe at half-past ten o'clock. I had been to pay a bill for fish.
MARY BISHOP. I am certain Johnson was one of the women - I am certain Hart was not the other woman, for I knew her well; I believe Bull to be the woman, but am not positive - Hart is a very different stature; they were sitting on the ledge in front of my window.
DINAH GREEN. I live nearly opposite Bishop. I saw her at her door; she said she thought some females had robbed a gentleman - Bull had been with me just before, and asked me for a pair of scissars; she then went up to Bishop's door, and I saw her come up the street with a gentleman in a blue frock coat, and light trousers - there was another female with them; they went to the corner of the street, then left the gentleman, and went into the oyster-shop - I did not lose sight of them; Bishop spoke to me directly they went down the street - I did not see them do any thing to him.
BULL - GUILTY. Aged 19.
JOHNSON - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Fourteen Years.

1964. RICHARD BRIGGS was indicted for stealing. on the 16th of September, 6 quarts of beer, value 2s., the goods of Harvey Combe and others.
RICHARD ANDREWS. I am clerk to Harvey Combe, and others; the prisoner was a watchman at their premises, in Store-street, Bedford-square. On Sunday morning, between four and five o'clock, I saw him unlock the door of their vaults; he went down and came out with a pail of beer, and left it in the yard. I said nothing to him - he then opened the gate, and let in two watchmen, who went up to where the pail was; I went out, but could find no watchman - I found a patrol and took him in, and gave charge of all three - the prisoner said he was sorry for what he had done, and he hoped I would forgive him.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Have not the men an allowance of beer? A. Yes; half a gallon a day or night - I give it out before I go away; another person had given him his that night; he has been there ten years, as a labourer - there is both porter and stout there; I cannot swear this was beer - when I came back, the pail was gone.
COURT. Q. Are stout and porter called beer? A. Yes; there was about a gallon and a half in the pail; I did not know he had the cellar key - he did not say it was his portion.
GEORGE PLATT. I took the prisoner in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me the key out of his own hand - he never denied the watchmen coming in; I thought it no harm to let them in to light the fires.
RICHARD ANDREWS. I did not give him the key - he had the key of the gates; and that (we have since found) opens the cellar.
NOT GUILTY.

1966. DENNIS SWINNEY was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of September, 3 pelisses, value 16s.; 1 coat, value 5s.; 1 shawl, value 6s, and 1 tea-pot stand, value 1s., the goods of Hannah Gravatt, widow; and 1 pelisse, value 4s., the goods of Mary Gravatt, spinster.
MARY GRAVATT. I am the daughter of Hannah Gravatt, who is a widow; she keeps a little stationer's shop, in Windmill-street, Finsbury. On the 19th of September, a little before nine o'clock in the morning, I saw this property safe in the parlour cupboard adjoining the shop; and about nine, the prisoner came into the passage to ask a question; I gave her an answer, and was soon afterwards called by Mr. Bruce, who said, "The pelisses," and ran off - I went to the cupboard and they were gone. I am sure the prisoner is the person whom I answered; he was brought back with them in three or four minutes, by Bruce.
HENRY BRUCE. I am a fishmonger, and live opposite Mrs. Gravatt; about nine o'clock I saw the prisoner looking into her window. I was writing on my counter, and in about ten minutes I saw the prisoner coming down her steps, with the pelisses on his arm - I ran over, and said,"Pelisses;" I pursued, and took him with them.
SAMUEL BRIDGES. I took him in charge with the property; this shawl was in his hat, and a bottle of catchup was in his pocket.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19.

FRANCIS LACLAIR was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of September, 2 shirts, value 4s.; 2 nightgowns, value 1s.; 3 aprons, value 1s. 6d.; 1 towel, value 6d.; 11 pairs of stockings, value 12s.; 2 sheets, value 4s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s.; 1 table-cloth, value 2s.; 2 pairs of drawers, value 4s., and 1 night cap, value 6d., the goods of Robert Brooks.

WILLIAM MILLS. On the 19th of September, in the morning - I heard Mr. Lake call for assistance - I went and found the prisoners on his premises, behind some timber, with this bundle by his side. I collared him, and took him to the watch-house with it.
WILLIAM ALLEN. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house - I found two sheets round his body, a handkerchief in his hat, and these drawers in his pocket.(Property produced and sworn to.)

1969. THOMAS KELLY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., and 1 pair of gloves, value 6d., the goods of Charles Bigsby.
JOHN BOSTON. On the 27th of July I saw the prisoner take a handkerchief from Mr. Charles Bigsby, who gave his address at the Hummums. I have called there, but cannot see him - he was coming out of the English Opera House, about twelve o'clock at night - these gloves were wrapped in the handkerchief: Mr. Bigsby claimed it at the watch-house, in the prisoner's presence.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. Where do you live? A. At No. 59, Tottemham-court-road. I am a porter, and work for any body - I was not in employ at the time- I had been in the gallery of the theatre - it happened in the Strand, near Southampton-street - the gentleman had a female with him - Roberts was present; he went and brought the gentleman to the watch-house - the Magistrate did not take Robert's evidence down - he said the gentleman had been there; we had some difficulty in getting the gentleman to go to the watch-house - several persons persuaded him not to appear; the gentleman said he could not say whether he had dropped it or not. I cannot find the gentleman - I have had another case here; I am not an officer - I was once accused of having a lamp in my possession - I was never charged with perjury.
COURT. Q. You say you saw this man take the handkerchief? A. Yes - he threw it over his shoulders.
THOMAS ROBERTS. I saw the prisoner take this handkerchief - Boston took him, and I went to the gentleman, who claimed it.
Cross-examined. Q. What are you? A. A painter and glazier. I live at No. 8, Feather's-court - I never wentby any other name - I have had cases here when I was a constable; I mean to ask for my expenses - Boston picked the handkerchief up.
Prisoner. Defence. I was going home when this man came and accused me of this - I had been to Gravesend.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-383]
1970. WILLIAM JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of August, 1 jacket, value 2s., the goods of John Joy.
JOHN JOY. I am a plasterer's labourer. On the 22d of August, at half-past four o'clock, I lost my jacket from Belgrave-place, Pimlico, while I was gone to tea - I saw the prisoner with it on, on the 13th of September - he had it on inside his waistcoat - he said it was his own, but I am certain of it - he said he bought it twelve months ago in a public-house; I am quite certain it is mine - there is a hole in the right-hand pocket, and two marks on the shoulder, where I carry my hod, and some oil spilt on the right sleeve.
JOHN SUTHERLAND. I was called and took the prisoner with the jacket on - he said it was his own - I said it was rather tight - he said he bought in a public-house in Westminster, but could not tell what he gave for it.
Prisoner. I wish him to try it on (the prosecutor did so, and it appeared to fit him) - the Magistrate said if I would give it up I might go.
JOHN SUTHERLAND. That is not true.
GUILTY. Aged 37.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-384]
1971. WILLIAM HORN was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, 1 watch, value 4l., the goods of Edward Smith.
EDWARD SMITH. I am steward of the ship Hector, West Indiaman, and live at Limehouse. On the 29th of August, at a quarter-past two o'clock, I saw my watch hanging in my birth, on the starboard side, and missed it at six o'clock. I saw the prisoner on board about two o'clock - he was quite a stranger to me.
WILLIAM THOMAS. I am a watchman of the West India Docks, and live in Duke-street, St. George's; I picked up this duplicate at a quarter-past five o'clock in the morning of the 17th of September, opposite the William Manning, which laid in the docks; I gave it to a dock constable, who returned it to me, with a note for Mr. Evans. I know nothing of the prisoner.
JAMES EVANS. I am an officer, and have the duplicate. The prisoner was brought to me, and on him I found a pocket-book, with three other duplicates, all in the name of Simpson.
JAMES WALTER. I am a pawnbroker. I gave this duplicate to a man who pawned this watch, in the name of John Simpson, for 30s. - I cannot swear to the prisoner.
EDWARD SMITH. There was nobody on board but I and the prisoner, when I saw him there; I had charge of the ship: he asked if we wanted a steward; I said No: he then went ashore, and I went down to feed my pig; when I came up he came on board again, and went aft; I heard him before I got quite up, and when I got up I could not see him; he then came up the companion ladder, and asked me to have a pot of beer - I said it was a wrong time, and he went away. I went down about six o'clock, to clean myself, and then missed my watch.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I declare my innocence - I was at Deptford that day.
ELIZABETH TOWNSEND. My first husband was the prisoner's father's brother - he has visited my house for some years; I saw him at Deptford on the 29th of August; I recollect it, because I was making a curtain for my dining-room; he called to say he was going to Lewisham, and would call and take tea with me; my daughter and husband saw him, but they are not here.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-385]
1972. WILLIAM BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of July, 12 yards of linen, value 12s., the goods of Owen McPharland.
JAMES WATSON. I work at the Cock public-house, Market-street, St. James'. On the 19th of July a man named (McPharland) came in, to sell some linen and handkerchiefs; he put a piece of linen down on the Bench; the prisoner and three other men were there; another man took up the linen, and put it into the prisoner's apron - he walked out with it. In about an hour I went up to Berkeley-square, and saw him and the others sharing some money - I do not know what money it was.
THOMAS GOOK. I took the prisoner in charge; he at first denied it, but afterwards took me to a room, and pointed out the man he had sold it to; he said he only had 1s. of it - one of the others 2s. 6d., and the other 1s. 6d. The prosecutor has been kept out of the way - I saw him before the Magistrate.
PETER ASHWORTH. I am a watchman. The prosecutor, Owen McPharland, came to me, and said he had been robbed, and asked if I could take the man; I went with Gook, and took him; he denied it, but next morning, said,"Mike took the cloth, and put it into my apron;" that it was sold, and they had the money among them."
Prisoner's Defence. I went in to have a pint of beer, and sat in an opposite box. It is false - they did not see me share the money; Gook said, "If you had told me of it I could have got you through it." I went for some water, and when I returned the man was accusing another man of it.
JAMES WATSON. He did accuse another of it.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18270913-386]
1973. JOHN RUSSELL was indicted for feloniously receiving 2lbs. of silk, value 3l., the goods of John Danford, knowing the same to have been stolen.
JOSEPH FRY HEAKES. I live at Mr. Danford's. I left my younger brother in the shop on the morning of the 14th of September, between half-past eight and nine o'clock; I returned in about a quarter of an hour, and missed a bundle of barber's weaving silk from the shelf - I sent round to different trades-people about it; my brother is not here. In consequence of information I went to Mr. Hudson's, in Porter-street, between one and two o'clock, about one hundred and fifty yards from our house, and saw the silk on his counter. I afterwards went to the prisoner's house, with Laing; he wasnot at home - we went again, and saw him; I asked if he had sent Laing to sell the silk; he said he had - I asked if he was in the silk line - he said not particularly, but that he had received this silk from his brother, at Sheerness, about a week before; he keeps a kind of curiosity shop, in St. Martin's-court. Here is the silk - it was made for us, of a size which is scarcely ever used - it was only made in July; we had a small quantity made about six years ago; here is some brown among it, of the same sort, which we bought in the City some years ago - there are two or three different sorts, made by different makers; the parcel lost consisted of all these different sorts - I have not a doubt of it.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. How old is your brother? A. Thirteen years - I cannot say that he did not sell it. I did not say what I wanted the first time I went to his house - I think the silk was produced to him. The Magistrate admitted him to bail, and when the officer told him to come he came; I did not hear him say that his father had bought it in his absence, and he had mentioned his brother to keep his father out of trouble; he and his friends have been to beg me to keep out of the way.
THOMAS LAING. I am a hair-dresser - the prisoner came to me last Friday morning, to my house in St. Ann's-court, and asked if I wanted any weaving silk, I said, No - he asked if I could tell him where he would sell it - I said, the hair merchants were the most likely people; he said he knew none of them, and if I would endeavour to dispose of it, he should feel obliged. I took it to Mr. Hudson's, and the witness came in and asked me the price: I said 22s. - he asked if I could not take less; I said, No, that it was not mine; he said he should like his father to see it, and then claimed it; I took him to Russell.
Cross-examined. Q. You knew him? A. Yes; he is a neighbour - he brought it openly, and did not desire me to keep it secret; he hears an honest character, and has lived there four years.
FRANCIS THOMPSON. I made some silk in June for Mr. Danford; I have no doubt this is our make, but I will not swear it is what I made to that order.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you make it for him only? A. We have made no more for the last three years.
HAMMOND WEBB. I took him in charge.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18270913-387]
1974. THOMAS PIKE was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 1 plank, value 3s., the goods of Catherine Harding.
CATHERINE HARDING. I am a timber merchant, and live at Somers's-town; I saw the prisoner walking from my premises with this plank on his shoulder; I followed and took him - he was formerly in my employ.
SAMUEL CLARK. I took him in charge; he said he bought it at a sale, but afterwards said this lady's foreman gave it him.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating the plank was given to him to saw by a man named Gilham.
GUILTY. Aged 70.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-388]
1975. THOMAS HILBERT was indicted for embezzlement.
MR. BODKIN conducted the prosecution.
GEORGE ILES. I am a baker, and live in Carey-street, Lincoln's Inn-fields. Previous to the 10th of September, I had levied a distress on Mr. Hipworth, of No. 25, New-road, St. George's in the East, for 18l.; I took the prisoner to the house on the 10th of September, and left him in charge with the warrant, and ordered him not to go out or be turned out till the money was paid, which was then 9l.; I called next morning, and saw him at breakfast, and said I understood he had received the money; he said he had; I said, "Let me have it:" he said, "I'll be back in a few minutes;" he went out, and did not return; I saw him next day at the Ship and Shears public-house; he then said he had lost the money, and I must take it by instalments; he has not paid any of it - I took him two days ago.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You rejected his offer, and took proceedings? A. I made no reply; he lived in a tenement of mine - he is a coal-whipper - he did not say he had lost it when I went to the house.
BENJAMIN HOPWOOD. I am tenant of this house; I found the prisoner there on the 10th of September; Mr. Spring, a friend of mine, paid him 9l., in my presence - he gave me a receipt, and left between nine and ten o'clock; here is his receipt.
CORNELIUS CRAYCRAFT. I was in possession of the house with the prisoner, and remained there till the money was paid; we then went and had a pot of beer; we then went to another house, and had a pot of beer, and three half-quarterns of gin.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he not very tipsy? A. No; he was sober; he told me he should take the money up next day.
Prisoner's Defence. I throw myself on the mercy of the Court - I was intoxicated.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18270913-389]
1976. JAMES SPRIGGS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of September, 1 pair of trousers, value 5s., the goods of James Taylor; 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; the goods of William Pratt; and 1 pair of trousers, value 2s.; 1 bag, value 6d.; 7 fish-hooks, value 6d., and 11 sail needles, value 6d., the goods of William Green.
JAMES TAYLOR. I am apprentice, on board the Huntly, which lays in the river - the prisoner is a stranger. I saw him on board yesterday; he came and asked for work; he was not engaged, but he lent a hand to shake out the chains and weigh anchor, and had a drop of grog and a piece of biscuit, which the mate told me to give him; he afterwards fell into the hold - the mate then told me to look after him - I did so; but he took an opportunity of getting away when I did not see him - when he came on board he had a frock under his arm; this was about ten o'clock. About half-past eleven the officer brought him on board, with my trousers and other things in a bag. I had sean him with a bag, but did not much notice it - my trousers were safe about ten o'clock, when he came on board, in the captain's state-room.
JOHN ROEBUCK. I am an officer. I took the prisoner with this bag, containing two pairs of trousers and a handkerchief. I took him on board the vessel, and they were claimed; the prisoner said when I stopped him, that all thearticles were his own, and that he had been a voyage in the vessel - but when he got on board he said some lumper man had given them to him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was intoxicated, or I should not have done it.
GUILTY. Aged 26.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18270913-390]
1977. JOHN ALLEN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of September, 6 window sashes, value 8l., the goods of Philip Palmer.
SECOND COUNT, for stealing 35 squares of glass, value 6l, the goods of the said Philip Palmer.
CHARLES GUMMOW WILLIAMS. I was in the employ of Messrs. Goodhall and Baron; we were finishing a house in Osnaburg-terrace, for Mr. Philip Palmer. I left it on Saturday night all safe - the sashes were in the frames, but not hung; we went on Monday morning; the sashes were then taken out, and the glass taken out of them. I saw the prisoner at the watch-house with the glass, and took the dimensions of the glass there; we have since fitted the glass to the sashes, and the little pieces which have been clipped off the sashes - they correspond exactly. I have a strong belief that they are Mr. Palmer's property.
WILLIAM HOOPER. I am a watchman. About five minutes before twelve o'clock on Sunday night, I stopped the prisoner in the New-road, about a quarter of a mile from Osnaburg-terrace, with these twenty-nine frames of glass on his shoulder - it has been compared with the sashes, and fitted exactly - I asked what he had got; he said they were books, which a gentleman had given him to carry to Gray's-inn-lane. I took him to the watch-house - he shook very much; the officer has got the glass, and I cannot find him.
C. G. WILLIAMS. There were thirty-six pance in the sashes, but some pieces were left behind - they had been broken in getting them out; the panes are four different sizes - we have tried two sizes, and measured the others - they fitted exactly in size and number and corresponded with the small pieces left in the corner of the frames.
Prisoner's Defence. A man asked me to carry a load; he said they were books - they were tied in brown paper; when I was taken the men went away.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18270913-391]
1978. JAMES ARGENT and JAMES WESTLEY were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 sow pig, price 50s., the property of Thomas Walter.
THOMAS WALTER. I live at the George-inn, Woodford. I missed a sow yesterday week at six o'clock in the evening - she had not been seen by me since the night before. I know nothing of the prisoner - I found it in Cowling's possession.
WILLIAM COWLING. I am a constable. On the morning of this day week I saw Argent driving a sow through Church-street, Hackney; the other prisoner was on the path-way. I did not hear them conversing, but I gave information to the patrol - he went forward, and stopped it, and I took Argent into custody, and the sow. I asked him where he brought it from - he said he brought it from Leyton, and was going to take it to market. I went on to the Horse and Groom public-house, and turned it into the yard. I asked him then if he had any more - he said he had four others coming up the road. I asked whose they were - he said his father's; I know his father perfectly well - I went to Leyton. I saw his mother, and ascertained it was not theirs - I then went and told Walter, and gave information of it instantly.
CHARLES LAMPIT. I am a patrol, of Hackney. I was with Cowling, and assisted in taking Argent.
JAMES MUNSEY. I am a labourer of Walthamstow. I was at Lee-bridge, and saw the two prisoners driving a sow before them along the road - I saw a sow this morning at Hackney, which I believe to be the same; it is marked in the same way, it has a white back, black head, and black quarters behind.
ARGENT'S Defence. I bought the sow at Chingford.
ARGENT - GUILTY. Aged 20.
WESTLEY - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18270913-392]
NINTH DAY. SATURDAY, SEPTEMBER 22.
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Recorder.
1979. THOMAS HARDING and JAMES JACKSON were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 2 table-spoons, value 3s., the goods of Joseph Quarterman.
SARAH PIPER. I live with Joseph Quarterman. On the 14th of September, the prisoners came at half-past five o'clock in the morning to sweep the chimneys - I had seen these spoons in the kitchen closet the night before; it was not locked - I missed them soon after the prisoners were gone; nobody else had been in the house - they were not above ten minutes in the house - I gave information; they were apprehended that morning - Hardy had called the day before, to know if we wanted the sweeps; he said they came from Mr. Donald, and I told him to come.
GEORGE CULL. I am beadle of St. Mary-le-bone. About ten minutes past six o'clock in the morning I was informed of this robbery - I apprehended the two prisoners immediately; one Brown gave me information - I found these spoons in Jackson's hosom; he owned be had taken them; he had said he had nothing about him.
JOHN BROWN. I am a chimney-sweeper. About a quarter-past five o'clock my boy gave me information - I went to the prosecutor's, and waited: the prisoners came out - I took hold of Harding, and gave them in charge, on suspicion, and saw the spoons found on Jackson; they were never in my employ; they work for Donald.
CHARLES TALBOT. I am a chimney-sweeper. At half-past five o'clock in the morning I was told the prisoners were gone to No. 8, Quebec-street to sweep chimneys - we waited them out, and secured them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
HARDING'S Defence. I did not know he had them.
SARAH PIPER. Jackson went up the chimney.
HARDING - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined One Year, and Publicly Whipped.
JACKSON - GUILTY. Aged 19,
[trialAccount t18270913-393]
1980. JAMES JACKSON was again indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 8 tea-spoons, value 1l.; 3table-spoons, value 2l., and 1 pair of sugar-tongs, value 10s., the goods of Isabella Charlton, spinster.
MARTHA STEVENS. I am servant to Isabella Charlton, who is single, and lives in Princes-street, Cavendish-square. One day in August, the prisoner came to sweep the chimneys at six o'clock in the morning, with another person, whom I should not know - he left about a quarter past six, and in about a quarter of an hour I missed all the plate, stated in the indictment, from the kitchen cupboard, which was not locked - I had seen it all safe the night before; I have found nine of them - the prisoner went up the chimney; no other person came to the house except the prisoner, from the time I saw them till they were missed.
GEORGE CULL. I took the prisoner, and found Quarterman's property on him - and a spoon which was claimed by a lodger.
Prisoner's Defence. I never was in the house.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
